# UX cohesion sweep, 6 November 2026

## 1. What this is

Colly asked, on 6 November: "Can you sweep the entire app for broader UI and UX improvements? Consider the design system and consider additional form ux research etc. Please consider larger bigger picture UX to make the overall use of the app more cohesive, consider how the flow of each job from enquiry to full completion is represented, and consider from each of the perspectives of the various user groups and their personas."

This document is the answer: research and prioritised proposals, nothing built. It looks at the app as it stood on main at `8d0dd7b` (app 1.88.0), the day after the 5 November audit ([docs/audit-ux-2026-11-05.md](audit-ux-2026-11-05.md)) whose 103 screen-by-screen findings had all been fixed. Where that audit asked "is this screen right?", this one asks "does the whole read as one app?": whether a job's journey from enquiry to paid is one story on every surface, whether each kind of person meets an app that faces them, whether the forms carry what the research knows, and whether the look follows the shared design system's grammar everywhere.

It is written so that Colly can pick. Section 4 gives the big moves, each absorbing many of the themes found, with the screens it changes and a size; section 9 puts them in an order with batches that each leave the app consistent; sections 5 to 8 give the reasoning by subject (the job's journey, each person's app, the forms, the look); section 10 lists everything found, with what the sceptic made of each, what held up and what was not checked. Nothing here is built until Colly says which batch.

What it does not do: it does not re-report the 5 November findings or one-off slips on one screen; it does not re-litigate a decision Colly recorded in an ADR without saying what the decision costs in use; it watched no real crew (the people in it are `docs/personas.md` and the six role lenses walking the bench as them); it measured no contrast (the system's checker does that) and ran nothing live.

## 2. How it was done

**The bench.** The app was run locally in passcode mode with its persona preview, against a made-up company a third of real size: 300 jobs with their phases, calls, offers and kit, 60 people (9 staff), 30 clients, 60 venues, 200 products, 1,500 items, 80 quotes, four vans, faults, tests, leave and timesheets. Every walk was on a 390×844 phone in the dark theme with touch, and on a 1440×900 desk in the light theme where the role works at a desk; the freelancer's link was walked in both colour schemes. Nothing touched the live app, the calendar or anyone's phone.

**Seven maps, then ten lenses, each with a sceptic.** Seven mapping passes recorded the app without judging it: every screen as every role (48 screens); every surface that says where a job stands (26) and every transition that moves one (16); each role's navigation, Home, actions, refusals and the words from other roles' worlds; every form and field; every state word, button and empty state; the stylesheets against the shared system; and the outside research on forms (58 sources, 22 rules). Ten lenses then walked the app with those maps in hand: the job's journey, consistency across areas, the forms, the six personas (director, office, head of department, warehouse, staff crew, freelancer) and the shared design system. Each lens's reviewer wrote themes with evidence, screenshots, a severity on the NN/g 0–4 scale, a priority (P1 before the next busy week, P2 worth doing, P3 polish), the heuristic, behaviour rule or research behind it, and a proposal with a size (S a day, M a few days, L a week or more). A sceptic then re-walked every theme to reproduce, adjust or drop it; only what survived is in section 10, with the sceptic's verdict.

**A design panel, then the synthesis.** Three architects each built a programme of moves from the surviving themes under a different weighting: user-first (what changes each persona's next visit), risk-first (the cost of the mistake each incoherence invites, and what a move could break) and smallest-change-first (cohesion per line changed, one shared part or table over many screens). Two judges scored the programmes, resolved their conflicts and proposed a merged order; a completeness critic looked for what all three missed, walked Colly's thirteen steps against the merged moves and listed the questions only Colly can answer. Section 4 and section 9 are the synthesis of that.

**Numbers.** Forty-eight screens mapped as every role; twenty-six surfaces that say where a job stands and sixteen transitions that move one; eleven hand-offs between roles, none announced; eighteen state vocabularies with twenty-seven collisions; sixty-odd forms; fifty-eight research sources and twenty-two rules; ten lenses, each with a sceptic; forty-six themes and twenty-nine smaller findings kept, nine dropped; three programmes of twelve moves each, two judges, one critic; twelve moves in five batches proposed; thirteen questions for Colly; about 840 screenshots; thirty-three reviewers in all, over about four hours.

**Where things are.** The screenshots are in the project's shared folder under `screens/ux-sweep/<pass>/` (about 840 of them: the journey of one job across every surface, the same part across areas side by side, each role's walk, every form). The maps, the lens reviews and sceptic verdicts, the compiled themes, the three programmes, the judges' scoring and the critic's report are in `session-hire/ux-sweep-2026-11-06-work/`.

## 3. How the app reads today

Seven mapping passes read the whole app before anyone judged it: every screen as every role, the job's position on every surface, each role's app, every form and field, every state word, button and empty state, the stylesheets against the shared system, and the outside research. The numbers below are theirs; the screenshots are in `screens/ux-sweep/` and the full maps in the working folder.

| | Count |
|---|---|
| Screens (Home 4, Jobs 16, Quotes 1, Stock 14, Crew 6, Menu and Company 6, the freelancer's link 1) | 48 |
| Surfaces that say where a job stands | 26 |
| Transitions that move a job on, of which can be undone in place | 16, 11 |
| Steps in Colly's thirteen the stage model does not record | 5 |
| Hand-offs between roles, of which are announced to anyone | 11, 0 |
| State vocabularies (job status, stage, task, quote, offer, call, leave, timesheet, purchase, fault, vehicle, document, tracker, planner, sync, notification, history verbs, reference words) | 18 |
| Words with more than one meaning, or meanings with more than one word | 27 |
| Button groups; empty states | 19; 44 |
| Forms; fields carrying `required` with no visible mark; `type="number"` fields; placeholders | 60-odd; 47; 22; 161 |
| Research sources opened; rules drawn from them | 58; 22 |
| Departures from the shared system's colour, type, space, state and part rules | 15 |

### One shell, drawn well

The shell is the same everywhere and is sess-sys's strongest cohesion asset: the phone's five-place foot bar (Home · Jobs · Stock · Crew · Menu) that becomes the desk's rail from 900px and gains Quotes for money roles; one sticky top bar with the sync word, the not-done count, the bell and the theme; an area row of views under it (Jobs: List · Board · Week · Month · Clients · Venues · Money; Stock: nine; Crew: three or four with counts); the shared page head with the Up link; cards with a heading and an icon; one status pill; one inline ask that never opens a dialog; one toast; "On this screen" chips above the first card when a page has three or more; one Next dock on the job page, portalled to the foot on a phone. The screens mapper found the shell consistent on every one of the 48 screens, and the design-system mapper found every colour, wash, elevation and duration reading a vendored token.

What is not one is the layer underneath the shell: where the title lives, how a record's position is said, which words a state uses, and how a form behaves. That is where "several apps" comes from.

### Two kinds of record page

A job, a person and a product put their name in the shared page head with a badge, a sub line and a picture; a client, a venue, an item, a place, a vehicle, a pick list, a call sheet, a quote, Tidy up, Kit from the notes and the imports put the h1 inside their first card, some under a kicker ("CALL SHEET", "QUOTE"). Five of the second kind repeat the record's name as the first card's heading under the same name in the crumbs. The job's status pill sits in the page head on the job page but inside the first card on its pick list, and the call sheet and the quote page show no status at all. On a desk, a product opened beside the Catalogue keeps the Stock area row above it; a job, client, venue or person opened from their lists shows no area row. Desk crumbs skip the list for a product ("Stock › d&b V8") but go through the list for a place ("Stock › Places › Bay C1").

Smaller tells of the same kind: Up reads "‹ All jobs", "‹ All stock", "‹ All people" on every list parent but "‹ Crew" on the crew import; the Menu's "Stock" opens a page titled "Catalogue"; the crumb's word differs from the h1 on Tidy up, the call sheet and the quote; search placeholders are verb-first on three lists ("Find a job, client or venue") and noun-only on three ("Name or contact"); a department is narrowed by count chips on the Catalogue, a select in the Filter panel on People, and chips plus a toggle on the pick list; the same split of time is "This week | Next week" on Jobs, "Coming up | Before" on a client, "Coming up | Before | Not going ahead" on a venue and "Coming up | Past | No dates yet" on Quotes; the fold that edits a record is "Change details" on six kinds, "Change details and checks" on a vehicle and "Change" on a phase and a kit line; the "add a record" button is filled on five lists and plain on four.

### One pill, four tones, and what they carry

The status pill is the one status part (`web/src/StatusPill.tsx`): amber for "someone has to act", an outlined grey for settled ("healthy is quiet", never green), a grey fill for off or over, burnt orange for a clash or a fault, and a dashed grey "Waiting to sync" in place of any of them. It is the right part. What it carries is where the grammar strains:

- **Amber** carries Enquiry and Quoted, Draft and Sent, Offered, Asked for more and Accepted (an offer), "2 of 4", To confirm, Waiting (leave), Asked for (a purchase), Off the road, Needs a look, Check, Gone quiet, With a job and Pencilled in.
- **Outlined** carries Confirmed (job and offer), Accepted, Invoiced and Paid (a quote), Booked, Filled (a call), Approved, Agreed, Ordered and Arrived, Working, At the warehouse.
- **Grey fill** carries Cancelled and Lost, Turned down, Declined, Filled (an offer), Withdrawn, Can't make it, Not buying, Retired, Put away, Not known and Off.
- **Burnt orange** carries "n short", Clash, Away, Not back and Not working.

So "Filled" is settled on a call and over on an offer; "Accepted" is amber on an offer and outlined on a quote on the same Money screen; "Offered" is amber on Calls, grey on the call sheet and a red fill on the freelancer's page, where red fill otherwise means "you" (the Next button, the bell badge, the Crew badge); Agreed and Ordered purchases are drawn settled though the list treats them as open. The pill's tones are sound; its vocabulary is assigned screen by screen.

### One word, several meanings; one meaning, several words

The words mapper read every state word in `shared/` and every label on the screens. Twenty-seven collisions, in families:

- **The client's yes and no.** Yes is Accepted (quote), Confirmed (job), "Client accepted" (button). No is Lost (job), Turned down (quote pill and list), "Client turned it down" and "The client said no" (buttons), declined (the key and the crew's word). Draft and "Not sent" name one state.
- **Confirmed.** A job status, the second task's name, an offer that has become a booking, and the pill tone itself; a confirmed crew booking is Confirmed on Calls, Booked on the call sheet and timesheet, "On {job}" in the planner, "Your bookings" on the link.
- **Waiting.** Bare on the leave pill; "Waiting on the office" (timesheets), "Waiting on you" (the link, Home tiles), "Waiting for an answer" (quotes), "3 waiting" and "Waiting to sync" (sync), "Asked for" and "Waiting for someone to decide" (purchases). On Leave the one pill slot can show "Waiting" and "Waiting to sync".
- **Short crew.** "1 short" (planner, Crew), "2 to find" (sheet), "2 to confirm" (Jobs row, task hint), "Crew still to find" (legend), "Crew needed" (heading); kit shortfall uses the same "n short", so "Planning · 1 short" means kit or crew only by position.
- **The office's OK.** Documents are checked, timesheets and leave approved, offers confirmed, counts finished, purchases agreed; "Check" is also the planner's soft clash and a timesheet row's action.
- **Out of use.** Items and vehicles are Retired, people Archived, trackers Put away, packages taken off the price list; the undo is "Bring back" for all.
- **Back, Going out, Planning, Show, Prep, Done, Next, Sent, Paid, Lost, Missing, Away** each carry two to four meanings: Back is a stage, a scan direction, a rack side and a link; Going out is a stage, a pick mode and a Stock heading; Planning the stage sits beside "the planner"; Away is a tracker off course on Stock and a person on days off on Crew; "Mark paid" is the client paying us and us paying a freelancer.
- **Expiry.** Documents and certificates "run out"; a vehicle's service is "due"; inspections are "due", "overdue since", "failed on", "not recorded yet"; only vehicles say "Good to {date}".
- **Removal and undo.** Remove (product, place, phase, photo, document), Take out (layout, items, rows), Take it off (road, map, list, price list, kit), Delete everything; history says Removed, Took off, Took back, Undid, took the tick off.
- **Save and add.** "Save job" and "Save product" (bare noun), "Save the pin" and "Save the package" (with the), "Save changes" and plain "Save"; creation is Add, Book, Ask for it, Start, Make, New.
- **Form of the stage and task names.** Selling and Planning (gerund), Going out (participle), On the job (preposition), Back (adverb); the tasks are past participles except "Out on the job".
- **The office's words and the freelancer's.** The office reads single words (Offered, Accepted, Declined); the link reads sentences (Waiting on you; Accepted, the office will confirm; Filled by someone else); "Asked for more" is "Rate sent to the office" on the link; the office's Withdrawn is the key `cancelled`; the freelancer's pulling out is "Can't make it", "You've pulled out", "I can't make it" and "can't make it any more".

### Empty states and buttons

Forty-four empty states, drawn by one part (`web/src/Empty.tsx`: a line starting No, Nothing, Nobody or None stands alone; anything else gets "Nothing here yet." in front). Three families of wording ("Clients are added as you type them into a job", "No clients yet: they're added as you type them into a job", "Nothing here yet. Add the first one under New job"), bare "Nothing here yet." with no direction on Faults, Labels, a place, a product's items and the set-aside numbers, "None" as a hint per Board column, nothing at all on Testing and Money when empty, and directions a role cannot follow ("Book a van for the job … with a driver from the crew" to staff crew).

Nineteen button groups. The Next dock's sixteen verbs are the app's best sentence ("Ask for the crew", "Sort 2 short on the kit", "Scan the kit back", "Tick off the deprep"). Around it, the filled button is spent several ways on one screen: three Saves on Admin › Company, "Check" on every timesheet row, "Checked" on every answer, two filled on the quote page, and the irreversible yes of every inline ask ("Remove it", "Mark it not faulty") is the loudest thing on the screen. Only the job page uses the foot dock or the page head's action slot; every other page's one action sits inside a card.

## 4. The big picture: twelve moves

The forty-six themes have two causes, and the three architects found the same two from different directions. The first is that most of what a person sees is drawn in more than one place when it could be drawn once: the red fill is decided in fifty-odd files, a state word is picked where it was typed, a count is worked out by the tile and again by the list it opens, a job's position is drawn seven ways from one shared view, the form grid is one CSS rule nobody overrode for a phone, and a group head inside a card is set five ways because no rule names it. The second is that the app grew role by role with the office as its default reader: the records built first (job, product, person) set a page model the seven built later never took, the department lens reached Home and Crew and stopped, the freelancer's link grew its own forms, and staff and heads meet the office's app with the buttons taken off. The shell is one and every lens held it up; what is not one is the layer under it.

So the moves are not new screens. Each puts one thing in one place that every surface reads, or turns one screen to face the person who opens it. They are listed here in the order the batches in section 9 build them, and each names the themes it absorbs so that section 10 can be read against it. Sizes are S (a day), M (a few days) and L (a week or more). Every move keeps the standing rules: offline first, the roles' reading limits held by the server, the app contacting nobody unless a switch is on, the server-drawn link with no script, no nested scroll on a phone, one page width, nothing offered a role cannot do, healthy is quiet, brand red as "you" and burnt orange as bad.

### Move 1 · One rule for the red fill (S)

**What changes:** the one filled red button on any screen is the thing to do next: the job's Next, or the one next step of a page without a dock. The yes of an inline ask, a row's action and a list's add are plain.

**The problem** (ds-1, consistency-2, forms-4 in part, ds-5 in part). ADR 0104 made `button.primary` "the only filled red on the page" for the one next step, and it is also the yes of all thirty-seven inline asks including "Write it off" and "Remove it", a "Check" on each of fifty timesheet rows, "Checked" on every answer, two filled buttons on a quote, "Put it on the map", "That's me", and "Make a quote" beside a dock already saying what is next. A list's add is a plain fold at the top on Jobs, at the foot of a 3,000px Stock page, and filled red on Quotes, the Purchase list and Labels. The office learns on the job page that red is the safe next step and then meets it on the button that closes a fault for good.

**The change.** In `web/src/act.tsx` the Confirm part's yes drops `primary`: it is the plain button, with its verb-and-object label and the safe no focused as now, and takes the danger look (the bad fill Delete everything already uses) only where the outcome is a loss: Write it off, Remove it, Erase, Withdraw, Cancel the leave; the amber question stays and still names the outcome. One line reaches all thirty-seven asks. `app.css` gains `button.quiet` after the system's `ds-btn--quiet`, and "Check" on timesheet rows and "Checked" on answers take it; the heading's count carries the volume. "Make a quote" and "Print or save as PDF" are red only when the dock has nothing to press for this job, as ADR 0109 did for the Confirmed card; "Client accepted" stays the quote's one primary. Every list opens with its add as a plain fold under the search, as Jobs does on a phone: Add product, Add place, Add vehicle and Add person move up from the foot; the Purchase list's, Quotes' and Labels' adds lose their fill. A form's submit on a page with the Next dock in view (Save job, Add phase, Save phase) is plain; Add job, Add product, Add person's first form and the Admin cards keep the red as the page's one next thing. The Next dock is untouched. Done in one PR so no screen is left with two meanings of red.

**Effect and parts.** Default Effect (the loudest button is the one pressed by habit, so it must be the safe next step) and Loss Aversion; NN/g 4 and 5; the system's Button rule (primary one per region, quiet for a row's actions, danger only when it cannot be undone) and principles rule 1. The inline ask in place of arming stays the recorded departure (learnings 2026-10-10); this move only takes the accent off its yes.

**Keeps.** ADR 0104 and 0109 (the dock is the only filled red on the job page), Confirm's outcome-labelled buttons with the safe one focused, 44px rows, every list's address.

**Size and risk.** A day or two: one line in act.tsx, one CSS variant, about fourteen single-line edits and four folds moved. The warehouse may have learned the loud Purchase-list button; the fold sits under the search where the eye lands, and the changelog says so. Checked by the contrast checker in both themes and the e2e specs that press the asks by text.

**Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/red-fill-one-colour-four-jobs-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-ds/inline-ask-red-yes-office-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/timesheets-office-desk-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-consistency/list-heads-phone-dark.png`.

### Move 2 · One rhythm sheet, and Money's tables back on a phone (S)

**What changes:** two head styles, one boxed-row shape, one fold summary, one "open" drawing, one invalid colour and one pending colour, written once in `app.css` and read by every area sheet; and the phone rule that was stripping Money's tables goes back to the quote sheet it was written for.

**The problem** (ds-4 parts 1 and 2, ds-2, ds-5, ds-f1, ds-f2, ds-f3, ds-f5, ds-f6, consistency-3's heads, director-f1). A group head inside a card is set five ways (12px tracked capitals, 13px capitals, 16px sentence case, the browser's 18.72px because nothing sets it, 20px); a boxed thing inside a card has three radii and two insets; h1 and h2 leadings sit off the 4px grid. "Open" is drawn as the hover wash on a task and a chart column; invalid is amber in the quote editor and bad everywhere else; pending item numbers are the one waiting thing still amber; the stage names are 11px, under the floor; eleven hover rules sit outside the hover media query so a tap leaves a tile lit; three folds carry three summary weights; two targets are 36px; Home's charts read local greys. And the 1.79.2 rule that hides a sent quote's middle columns on a phone is unscoped, so Money's By month and Margin by job lose three of five columns with no scroll: the one P1 that is pure CSS.

**The change.** All in `app.css` and the area sheets that read it. One `.card h3.group` rule (12px tracked capitals, muted) for a head over a run of rows; a sub-section with its own copy keeps ADR 0082's 16px sentence case; the two unsized heads on Money and Quotes get a size, and one comment names the two rules and the distinction. `--inset-box` and `--radius-box` beside the six space roles, read by every boxed row. h1 line-height 32px and card h2 28px. The `.quote-table` phone rule scoped to the sent quote's sheet, and Money's two tables take a stacked phone row. The open task loses its hover wash for the shared chevron; the picked chart column takes a 2px ink bar. The invalid edge takes `--bad` and the pending numbers `--muted`. The stage names go to 12px with "On the job" allowed to wrap. The hover rules move inside `@media (hover: hover)`. One `summary` rule for every fold (ink, 600, the shared chevron turning at `--ds-duration-base`, a count where there is one). The charts read `--ds-chart-mark` and `--ds-chart-pending`. The filter chip and the storage banner's button take 44px. The accent used as a card edge for emphasis on the crew share card and the warehouse claim card moves to a neutral edge, since accent means "you" or "current".

**Effect and parts.** Chunking (the eye learns which head is a group and which a section) and NN/g 8; the system's Section, states.md's Expanded, Invalid, Pending and Selected, principles.md's type and space rules, charts.md. Two head styles rather than the system's one stay as sess-sys's own (ADR 0082), written into the sheet.

**Keeps.** ADR 0082 (card headings in sentence case), the audit's held-up "one look per state", touch density everywhere, both themes and reduced motion through the tokens.

**Size and risk.** A day: about forty lines across `app.css` and five area sheets, plus an hour for the stacked Money row. CSS only, so a wrong value is a one-line revert; the contrast checker runs in both themes. The card gap inside a record (16 against a list's 24) is Colly's call and is one variable either way (section 9).

**Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/card-group-head-five-sizes-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/boxes-in-cards-three-radii-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-director/money-by-month-director-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/current-drawn-seven-ways-phone-dark.png`.

### Move 3 · One count, one list, one lens (M)

**What changes:** every count on Home, the area rows and a page head is the number of the list it opens, read from one function; the Home tile and the Board agree on which jobs still have kit to check back in and say how long it has been; a head's Stock lists and counts start on their department the way Crew already does; and an item's row says it is out or missing wherever it is drawn.

**The problem** (sceptic-jobflow-1, head-1 step one, head-2, head-f2, office-6, director-f2, warehouse-3, warehouse-4, warehouse-f1, consistency-4 in part, director-2 in part). Home says 3 jobs have kit still out while the Board's Back column shows 12, because Home reads the scanned-only list and the Board reads ADR 0109's never-scanned rule: the nine jobs the warehouse must check reach no surface it opens on a phone. Home says 73 answers to check, the list it opens is headed 74; Home says 7 jobs whose dates passed, Quotes lists 1 under the same words. A head reads "Repairs 15" on the Stock row and 5 on the next screen, because the department lens chosen on Home falls away on Stock; their leave count includes people they do not approve and their timesheet count counts rows whose amounts are hidden. Finding an item by number says where it is kept, not that it is out with a job or broken; an unsent scan carries no pending mark on its row. The cost is a count trusted that its list contradicts, every morning, and kit that stays out until the next job needs it.

**The change.** One function in `shared/src/standing.ts`, kit to check back in, returns the jobs whose Back-in task is open under ADR 0109's rule (lines out and never scanned back), with the count of lines and the days since the job ended; the Home tile, the Board's Back column, Home › Warehouse and Stock's Still out card all draw from it. Home › Warehouse gains "Still out after the job (12)", age-ordered, each row "3 of 27 not scanned back · ended 4 days ago", amber past the return days plus two; the Board's Back head reads "Back 10 · 3 late". Every tile and area-row badge takes its number and heading from standing.ts and the list it opens draws its heading from the same function, with a test that each tile's count equals the length of its list on the bench: Crew's heading becomes "Answers to check (73) · 1 running late"; Quotes' group reads "Dates passed with no answer (1 of 7 jobs)" with a link to the Board's fold; Home's money line uses `euroText` as its sentence does. The Stock row's Testing, Repairs and Counting counts come from the same tile functions, narrowed for a head through `narrower(useMyDepartment()).kit` as the Crew row already narrows; Repairs, Testing and Kit short open on the head's department from the tab, under the one line "Showing LX only. Show all departments"; a head's Leave count is the leave they approve and their Timesheets count leaves out hidden rows. The job head's CREW fact for a head reads "LX 0 of 1 · all 5 of 10" and the Crew tab lists their department first. Where the role's reading leaves money out, the Selling hint reads "Quote: with the office" instead of "No quote yet" on the list, the Board and the steps (head-3): one branch in `steps-view.ts`, safe before the fields of Move 7 because a head's device never holds the "Sent …" text the Board matches. One `ItemRow` part, lifted from the product page, is drawn by the catalogue's search rows, the scanner's last-read line and the pick list's Back mode: "SH-000047 · Mic case 24 · Out with Corporate AGM since 2 Nov · Bay C3", with Missing as the bad flag; while a scan is unsent, the row and the scan answer take the dashed "Waiting to sync" pill, never amber. The repairs list is "Repairs" on the row, the tile, the page head and the Menu.

**Effect and parts.** Aggregation and Salience (one number, one place) and Limited Choice (a head's list of forty becomes four); NN/g 1 and 4; Colly's standing rule that every count opens the list that explains it. Stat tile, Badge, the narrowing line with its undo, states.md's pending.

**Keeps.** ADR 0109 (the fortnight rule; the never-scanned words): every surface now follows it rather than its own count. ADR 0033 (a count of nothing is not shown), ADR 0046 and 0062, ADR 0024 (approvers are people), ADR 0034's "Show all departments" as the one way to widen, the Warehouse view's Going out first.

**Size and risk.** Three days: a morning for the shared rule and its unit test on the bench's eleven jobs, a day for the Warehouse group and the row part, a day for the narrowed starts and counts, half a day for the e2e cases. The kit-still-out tile goes from 3 to 12 on the bench and higher on live until the warehouse works through the unscanned jobs: that is the truth ADR 0109 chose, and What's changed says why in one line. A head covering two departments pays one "Show all" tap per Stock page, the cost ADR 0034 weighed. Nothing stored changes.

**Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-jobflow/home-warehouse-view-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-jobflow/board-office-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-head/repairs-from-stock-tab-vs-from-home-tile-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-office/home-tile-crew-needed-lands-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-warehouse/stock-find-still-out-item-phone-dark.png`.

### Move 4 · One word table (M)

**What changes:** every state word, every "who does this" sentence and every list's words for the past, a crew shortfall and a pull-out come from one shared table in `shared/src`, read by the pills, the task names, the history words, the group heads, the Menu and the freelancer's page, so each word is written once.

**The problem** (freelancer-5, freelancer-3, consistency-3's words, consistency-f1, consistency-f3, staff-4, staff-3, staff-f1 in part, office-1 in part, head-x1, head-f1, warehouse-3 in part, jobflow-f2). The words map found twenty-seven collisions. Confirmed is a job status, a task, a crew booking and a pill tone; Filled is settled on a call and over on an offer; Waiting is bare on leave and qualified everywhere else; a crew shortfall is "1 short", "2 to find", "2 to confirm" and "Crew still to find", and kit uses the same "n short"; a booking is Booked on the sheet and Confirmed on the link, two vocabularies across one phone call; pulling out is said four ways; the time groups are Past, Before, Worked and Earlier by screen; "Not yours" is said to staff in seven shapes from two tables the app already has (the dock's owner map and `whoCan`) that no sentence reads; the Menu describes the office's work to staff who reach the same lines; a head's department is LX on two tabs and Lighting on the third; history says "status to cancelled".

**The change.** `shared/src/words.ts` holds the tables and the sites read them. A crew booking is "Booked" everywhere (the sheet, the Jobs row's "Crew 5 of 10 booked", the link's tag, the office's Calls pill) and "Confirmed" is only the client's yes (ADR 0103) and the job status it sets; "Filled" stays for a call with its people, and the link's "Filled by someone else" (audit finding 61) is the sentence the app's offer pill reads too. "Waiting" is never bare: "Waiting for approval", "Waiting on the office", "Waiting on you", "Waiting to sync". A crew shortfall is "n short" wherever crew is the subject and the kit line keeps the held-up words "7 lines of kit, 1 short" and the dock's "Sort 1 short on the kit"; the chip in Move 7 gives crew its own count, which removes the kit-or-crew ambiguity without a rename. "Still needs crew" is the one list name. The way out of a booking is "Can't make it" on both sides, with "Withdrawn by the office" for the office's act and "The job was cancelled" when the job stopped. The time groups are "Coming up", "Past" and "Not going ahead" on every list that splits by time. Up reads "‹ Catalogue" and "‹ Calls" where those pages are what opens. A `NotYours` part draws one sentence from `whoCan`, "The office books the crew." or "The warehouse scans kit out and back here.", and the dock's owner map folds into the same table so the dock, the refusals and the sentence agree; a hidden rate reads states.md's "Hidden for your role". The Menu's line under each area is written for the roles that reach it ("Jobs: the jobs you're on", "Stock: report broken or missing kit"). The narrowing line says both names when they differ: "Showing LX (Lighting) only". History says "cancelled". Stock's logs use the shared day label. The keys and wire names do not change; old history lines keep the words they were recorded with.

**Effect and parts.** Fluency (a word met in one shape everywhere is read, not decoded) and Certainty; NN/g 2 and 4; the system's "one name per thing", states.md's "Not sent to you", the Empty state rule that a role with no button is told who fills it. The Confirmed task keeps its name as ADR 0103 wrote it: once a booking is "Booked" the collision is gone without a rename.

**Keeps.** ADR 0103, ADR 0073, ADR 0082, ADR 0034 and 0046 §10, the one Menu order for every role, the freelancer's counter-flow sentences, the history test.

**Size and risk.** Two or three days: the table and the NotYours part are a morning; the rest is about thirty single-line sites across `web/src`, `server/src/crew/page.ts` and `history-words.ts`, each a word swapped for a table read, and the e2e assertions that match text. A rename is only safe if it lands everywhere in one PR, app, link and history together; the table makes the blast radius one grep. The Booked question is Colly's (section 9); the move ships the rest first if the answer is slow.

### Move 5 · One form family: the link first, then the app, then one picker (S + M + M)

**What changes:** every form, in the app and on a freelancer's phone, lays out the same way: 16px fields in one column with named pairs, "(optional)" in the label, a hint under the label rather than a placeholder, the unit in the box, one footer with the submit first and never greyed out, and a refusal in the app's words. Later, one picker for the company's own records.

**The problem** (forms-5, forms-1, forms-2, forms-4, forms-6, forms-F1 to F5, consistency-5, forms-3). The freelancer's forms are a second family: 14.4px text that makes an iPhone zoom, 48px `.yes` and `.no` both filled, a bold red error line, "(€)" in brackets, two 161px date columns, and empties refused by the browser's bubble in its own voice. In the app `.grid-form` is two columns on a 390px phone with no override, so First name sits beside Last name at 156px; required is never shown and optional is said three ways; the submit is full-width red on one form, 89px beside Cancel on the next, un-primary on a third, and thirteen submits are disabled until valid so a person cannot find out what is missing; 22 `type="number"` fields let a 0 through with no refusal; nine placeholders carry instructions that vanish on the first keystroke; a switch sits inside a form with a Save; Add phase with no name does nothing at all. Picking a client or venue is a datalist, a person to offer to is a native select of sixty names, a product is the combobox.

**The change.** *The link (S, batch 1):* in `server/src/crew/page.ts` a dozen values from the vendored tokens: controls at `max(16px, 1rem)` and 44px high, `.yes` as the app's primary look (one per card) and `.no` quiet, `.field-error` drawn as the app's refusal line in the bad wash (invalid is bad, never amber), "Day rate you'd do it for, €" with the euro drawn before the box and in the accessible name, the days-off dates in one column with From and Until as a pair, instruction placeholders turned into small hints. The native `required` check stays, because it is the one validation that works with no script and no signal; whatever reaches the server is refused in the app's words under the field, with "Error: " prefixed to the page title and the redrawn page landing on the refused field. *The app (M, batch 2):* `app.css` makes `.grid-form` and `.office-form` one column under 640px with a `.pair` wrapper for the rows that belong together (From and To, First name and Last name) and caps short answers at a desk (about 8em for a count, a rate or a date) instead of half the grid. `.with-unit` lifts into `app.css` so every money and quantity field carries its unit as an affix in the box and in the accessible name; the 22 number fields become `inputMode` numeric or decimal text with a minimum and whole-number check in `required.ts`'s own voice ("A count has to be at least 1."). "(optional)" is written in the label text and placeholders hold only an example in one grammar ("e.g. 250"); instruction placeholders become hints under the label joined by `aria-describedby`. `.grid-form > .actions` is the one footer: the submit first (filled only when nothing else on the page is the Next), the quiet Cancel after it, a destructive link third, full-width children under 640px; no submit is ever disabled: That's me, Offer, Save allowance, Subhire, Give a department and Tidy press into a refusal in `required.ts`'s voice ("Choose someone to offer it to first."). Status, Kind and Leave's Type become the segmented control Faults already draws; the Travel switch moves out of its form onto its own line; Add phase's name is `required`; after every Save the result is said one way, the form closing and a quiet line where it was. List searches take one grammar, keeping "Find a …". *A day of hygiene the research asks for and no programme had:* `scroll-padding` under the sticky bars and the phone dock so a refused field is never hidden (WCAG 2.2 2.4.11); `autocomplete` tokens for names, phones and emails with spellcheck off on names and codes; Reply by shown as changed when edited and "Same as the job" for a phase's dates; Remove only on an open phase row of the new job form. *The picker (M, batch 5):* `ProductPicker` generalises into a `RecordPicker` with the system's combobox keys, and Offer to… on a call becomes it: the search box and the sixty-name select merge, the department as the meta line, applicants a checkbox under it. Client, venue, place and supplier sites wait for a month of use and Colly's word, since 1.82's Did you mean already carries the typo cost.

**Effect and parts.** Error prevention and recognise-and-recover (NN/g 5, 9); rule 18 (a refusal says where, what and next) and rule 19 (labels visible, placeholder only an example); Limited Choice; Friction only where it earns its place. The system's Field, Button, Switch, Segmented control and Combobox; principles.md's "Without script" for the link. The "(optional)" convention and the form around the field are gaps in the system: a Form part is the first learning in section 8.

**Keeps.** `required.ts`'s refusal voice and focus, Confirm's safe-focused buttons, native dates with `min`, the quote draft's autosave and line-item editor (ADR 0080, 0100), the Admin cards' Save-plus-Pending model, the desk's two-column density, no sticky Save on a phone, the link's redraw with what was typed, ADR 0002 (no script on the link).

**Size and risk.** Half a day for the link, four days for the app (a day of CSS across the sixty-odd forms, two for the number fields and the new checks, one for labels, hints, footers and the six undisabled submits, one of hygiene), two days for the picker's half step. Phone forms grow about a third in rows; the pair and the width cap keep the first screen to what is needed. Turning number fields into text could let letters through: the whole-number check refuses them and the server keeps its own. Checked by e2e refusals.spec, layout.spec at 390px and the twelve form patterns walked as the office.

### Move 6 · The link says what changed, and a staff member's time off has one home (S + M)

**What changes:** a freelancer who opens their page after the office moved a call reads "Call 07:30 (was 09:00)" on the booking and "The job was cancelled" as the card's first line, instead of the new time with no mark and "Withdrawn" seven screens down; a staff member's link no longer offers a days-off form that skips approval, and her bookings carry one name on Home › Me, her page and her link. Later, one "Work done" list with the money state and the set-once prose folded to a line each.

**The problem** (freelancer-2, freelancer-3, freelancer-4, freelancer-8, freelancer-1, staff-1, staff-f2, staff-f1 in part). When the office changes a call time the tell-the-crew prompt is off by default (ADR 0048) and the server-drawn page redraws the new time with no mark, so the person who said yes to 09:00 has no way to know it is now 07:30; a cancelled job reads "Withdrawn" 7.6 screens down, the office's word for taking someone off a job. The link's Add days off form has no staff branch, so a staff member's absence goes on the calendar without the approval ADR 0024 requires and never reaches Leave › To approve; the same person sees "Your day" on Home › Me and "Your bookings" on the link, "Days off" there and "Leave" here, and an offer in Notifications opens a page where she cannot answer. After the day one job sits in two lists 4,477px apart with two state words; about 1,600px of calendar and document prose is drawn in full on every visit; Past work and Declined stop silently at eight rows.

**The change.** *Batch 1 (S, one PR each side):* on a staff member's link the days-off block becomes "Leave", lists approved and waiting leave read from the leave records, and ends "Ask for leave in the app ›"; the form is not drawn for staff and the server refuses the post for a staff token in the app's words, so a cached page cannot post. Home › Me's card and the link's are both "Your bookings" with the same glance line and one booking row (the job, then role · phase · days · call, the pill); the Offered row reads "Answer it on your page ›" and the Notifications row for an offer to the reader opens the link; answers made on a person's own link are their own changes and leave their list. The Purchases card loses its "KIT TO BUY" kicker. Three facts are watched per booking from the mutations log since the booking's yes: call time, date and venue; the page draws the current value with the old one in brackets for seven days or until the call, whichever is sooner. A booking on a cancelled job reads "The job was cancelled" as the card's first line, on a lost job "The job didn't go ahead", and an offer the office withdrew "Withdrawn by the office"; the person page's rows in the app say "Job cancelled" and "Job lost" beside the booking's word. Pulling out has one name on both sides, "Can't make it", on the link's button, pill and banner, the office's pill and the history line, in one PR with the e2e text matches. Past work and Declined fold under "Earlier (12)" rather than being sliced off. *Batch 2 (M, page.ts only):* a "Since you said yes" block above the bookings, one line per change in the last seven days, drawn only when there is something to say, so the glance line keeps its three parts; Timesheets and Past work become one "Work done" section, each row the page's existing line with its state third: "Send your days and extras ›", "Sent €200, with the office ›", "Approved €263, not paid yet ›", "Paid Sat 31 Oct, €200"; with timesheets off (ADR 0090) the rows carry only the days, so the page is ready for the switch and costs nothing now; the calendar feed, the busy calendar, the documents and the kit prose fold to one state line each ("Your documents · 3, one runs out in 40 days") and open themselves only when they hold something that needs the person. Nothing is sent: the page reads differently the next time it is opened.

**Effect and parts.** Visibility of status (NN/g 1) and Salience; Recognition over recall (the page says what changed, the person need not remember the old time); rule 14 (bundle bad news) met on a page with no script as a block instead of a toast; the Disclosure rule (shut by default, opens when it holds something); Default Effect for leave (the default is the path with an approver). The two ended words, cancelled by the client and withdrawn by us, and the no-script "news of others' changes" block are learnings for the system.

**Keeps.** ADR 0002 (the link is the whole credential, no script), ADR 0024, ADR 0034 (the link stays the one place to answer; in-app answering is Colly's question), ADR 0048 (nothing is sent), ADR 0058 (freelancers have no notices because the link shows theirs: this is the link showing theirs), ADR 0090, the glance line's three parts, the booking card model, the folds after three, posts returning to the card, the counter flow, "You" first on the sheet.

**Size and risk.** Batch 1 is a day for the staff branch and refusal and a day for the facts, the ended words and the one name; batch 2 is three days in `page.ts`, mostly in reading the log for one person's bookings cheaply (one bounded read per page, no write per open). A change in a shape the reader does not know reads "Changed since you said yes · check the call" with no bracket rather than guessing. Staff who recorded days off on the link before keep those records readable until their dates pass; nothing is deleted. Checked by e2e freelancer.spec (staff token refused; call time changed shows "was"; cancelled job's first line) and the history words test.

**Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-freelancer/booking-after-office-changed-call-time-dark-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-freelancer/past-and-declined-dark-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-staff/compare-home-me-vs-link-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-freelancer/side-by-side-timesheets-vs-past-work-dark-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-freelancer/feed-and-busy-blocks-dark-phone.png`.

### Move 7 · Fields on the view, then one position chip (L)

**What changes:** a job's position is one part drawn the same way on the job head, the Jobs row, Home's rows, the Board card and its column heads, the dock's line and the pick list's head: "Planning · 5 of 10 booked, 1 kit line short · Aoife · in 6 days", quiet when nothing is short, amber when crew or kit is short, burnt orange when late. The status pill is kept for a sale (Enquiry, Quoted) or a stopped job (Lost, Cancelled), so "Confirmed" stops being the biggest word on a job two days into Back.

**The problem** (jobflow-f1, jobflow-1, jobflow-4, office-3, office-7, head-3, jobflow-f3, consistency-4 in part, director-2 in part, ds-f1). The task hints are strings that the dock and the Board read back with a regex to choose a button or a tone, so a wording change silently changes behaviour. One job's position is drawn in seven shapes with different words: on one day the same job reads "Planning · 1 line of kit · Confirmed · Crew 2 of 2 · Kit short" on the list, "1 line of kit, 1 short · 6 days left" on the Board, "Next · Planning · 1 line of kit, 1 short" in the dock and "Planning · Crew 2 of 2 · Kit short" on Home. Nothing but the Board says how long, and the Board's Planning column is 31 to 45 cards with no break because Planning is where a confirmed job lives for months. The selling line is computed in four places, and only Quotes says "worth a call". Errands and meetings show a dead "have no stages" line. The stage names are 11px.

**The change.** *First PR, no pixel changes:* each task in `shared/src/sync/steps-view.ts` exposes its facts as fields beside the hint (booked, needed, short, out of, until, sent on, due on, days to, days after, quote hidden); the dock and the Board read the fields and not the string; one `sellingLine()` beside the stage lines in `shared/src/steps.ts` gives the Board, the stage line, the dock and Quotes the same sentence ("Draft started · 1 line needs a price", "Quote with the client 12 days · worth a call" at the chase threshold, "Dates passed, no answer", "Quote: with the office" when the reading leaves money out); a unit test fails if the hint and the fields disagree on the eleven bench jobs. *Second PR, the part on every surface at once:* a `JobPosition` part in `web/src/jobs`, sess-sys's own look on the system's chip keys, text first, in one order everywhere: stage word · the current task's one-line hint · owner's first name · time ("in 6 days", "until Fri", "2 days after", or "by Wed 4 Nov" when a due day is set). It takes the role's narrowed view as its input so it never says what the reading limits hide. Job page head: the pill stays for Enquiry, Quoted, Lost and Cancelled; once confirmed the head draws the chip and the dock's line is the chip's text, so head and dock agree, while the task cards keep ADR 0109's own sentences ("Taken as done: it ended … with nothing still out"). Jobs row: the chip replaces the stage line, the pill and the Kit short flag; "Crew b of n" stays as its own count; the Kit filter stays. Home's Today and week-ahead rows and the Department and Warehouse views' job rows: the chip, compact. Board: the chip's hint and time as the card's one line; the Planning column grouped "This week", "Next week", "Later (n)" with Later folded; Back's never-scanned line amber until the return days plus two and burnt orange after; column heads from the fields ("31 · 4 short", "Back 10 · 3 late"). The pick list's head and the quote's What happened card draw the chip, so money and position read together. Where it is gains one quiet line, "In Planning since Tue 3 Nov · first day in 6 days". A meeting reads "Meeting · Mon 8 Mar" and folds Kit, Show pack and Quote behind More. The job tab reads "Crew 2 of 3"; the planner's cells keep "N/M" with a title, since the planner's own cells have no room. The person page and the link stay as calls (ADR 0104). The stage strip's names go to 12px.

**Effect and parts.** Recognition over recall and Consistency (NN/g 6, 4); Goal Gradient (the chip shows how far a stage has to go) and Time Scarcity (the date on the chip is the real pressure); healthy is quiet (no tone until something is short). The system's Chip and Disclosure; this is the stage chip ADR 0104's learnings asked the system for.

**Keeps.** ADR 0104 word for word (five stages worked out, one Next, the dashed rest box, the stage line, the Board read-only with no money, a column a stage soonest first) and ADR 0109 (the Next scrolls its tab into view, the fortnight rule, the never-scanned wording, lapsed); ADR 0103; the stage-line words three lenses held up ("Planning · 5 of 10 booked · 7 lines of kit, 1 short, 2 vehicles"); the Board's Selling fold; lost and cancelled reading the same everywhere; the reading limits.

**Size and risk.** A week: a day and a half for the fields, the regex removal and the test; four days for the part on its surfaces and the Board's bands; a day walking the eleven bench jobs the journey lens used. One part read by seven surfaces widens the blast radius of a wording change, which is why the fields land first and alone and a snapshot test pins each surface's text. The green "Confirmed" reassurance on a planning job goes; the quiet chip and the Where it is card keep the glance. Nothing stored changes.

### Move 8 · Arrive on the thing you came for, and the pick list's own Next (S + M)

**What changes:** a tile, a notice or a planner row that names a part of a job opens the job scrolled to that part; the job head's kit fact is a link to the pick list; within a week of going out the dock's quiet second line reads "Pick the kit · 0 of 27 out"; the pick list draws the same Next dock as the job page with the floor's words; Stock's On this screen strip is back; and a search by number answers alone.

**The problem** (office-2, warehouse-2, warehouse-1 step one, warehouse-f3, warehouse-f2). Every list that sends someone into a job for a reason links to the part, and on a phone the tap lands at the top with the tab row 800 to 1,050px down, because only the Next's own press scrolls. On a job going out tomorrow with 0 of 27 out the one Next is the planner's verb, the pick list is three taps away through the Kit and vans tab, and the pick list itself has no stage words and no Next, so the last scan says "ready to load" and stops. Stock opens on the catalogue with Pick lists a screen and a half down, and the strip ADR 0033 relies on as the jump is gone because the catalogue card has no heading and the strip needs three. A number search leaves the day's cards under the one result; Back is the narrower of two neighbouring buttons on Coming back rows.

**The change.** *Batch 1 (S):* `JobScreen.tsx` scrolls the named part into view and opens its tab whenever the address carries a part, whether it came from a tile, a notice, the Board or a typed link, with focus on the part's heading and no history step; the catalogue card takes the heading "Catalogue (209)" so the strip returns as "Catalogue · Pick lists · Kit short"; while a search is typed only the catalogue card draws, and a whole number that matches one item opens it; Back takes Missing's width with a 16px gap. *Batch 3 (M, after the chip):* the job head's kit fact ("Kit · 0 of 27 out") links to the pick list on every job with kit; on the job page, within seven days of going out and for a role with the stock ability, the dock's quiet second slot reads "Pick the kit · 0 of 27 out" in place of "Hand it to someone" for that case, the filled verb staying the office's; the pick list's head draws the chip and its foot draws the same `NextDock` part the job page uses, the only two places it is drawn, with four wordings: "Next · Pick the kit · 6 of 122 out", "All 122 out: ready to load. On the job from Mon 9 Nov", "Next · Scan the kit back · 2 still out", "Next · Tick off the deprep · 2 faults open on its kit". The sticky scan box stays under the top bar; the foot grows from 75 to 166px as the job page's does, which leaves about half of an 844px phone for the list, so the four wordings stay short and a Playwright walk of a pick at each stage checks that the scan box and the dock never fight. One line in ADR 0104's record says the dock is drawn on the pick list too; the 1 November research already names it as the dock's second place, so this informs Colly rather than asks.

**Effect and parts.** navigation.md's "arriving at a part lands on it" and "On this screen"; Goal Gradient ("0 of 27 out" shows the distance); Recognition over recall (the dock and the chip say the same thing on the pick list). The Next dock as sess-sys's own part, one per page.

**Keeps.** ADR 0104 and 0109 (one Next per job; the filled verb is the planner's until ticked; Kit and vans is ticked by hand only while planning), ADR 0033's Stock order (the day's cards' position is Colly's question), the pick list head, scan then a one-line answer with Undo, offline scanning, no nested scroll on a phone.

**Size and risk.** A day for the landing rule, the heading, the search and the row widths; three days for the kit link, the dock's second slot and the pick list's dock with its wordings. A direct link to a job's crew no longer shows the head first; the dates are one scroll up. Checked by e2e up.spec, home.spec ("a tile lands on its part"), notices.spec, pick.spec and stock.spec, and the warehouse persona from Home › Warehouse to a pick list to a scan.

**Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-office/job-crew-link-lands-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-warehouse/job-page-warehouse-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-warehouse/picklist-harbour-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-warehouse/stock-catalogue-phone-dark.png`.

### Move 9 · Hidden is never missing: each role meets its own app (M)

**What changes:** a head's department lens holds on Stock, the Jobs list and the job page as it does on Home and Crew; a job someone is booked on opens with "You on this job" first; a staff member's Menu, Stock and empty states speak in her words and offer only what she can do; and a step the role cannot take is one sentence, not a dead instruction.

**The problem** (head-1 step two, staff-2, staff-3, staff-f3, staff-4, jobflow-3 in part). A head's lens falls away on the Jobs list and Board, so a head of LX reads the whole company's Planning column to find their part; a head whose reading lacks money still meets the Selling vocabulary beyond the one hint. A staff member opens her next job and gets the office's page: her call below the fold, her name only on the Crew tab, the dock saying "Next is kit and vans settled, for the heads of department. Nothing here for you to press." The bar's Crew sends her to People while the #crew address lands on Calls; three of five tabs open company lists; the Menu's lines describe the office's work; Stock names nothing she can do though reporting broken kit is the one thing she can (ADR 0034); a van's empty state tells her to book one with no button. "Not yours" is said in seven shapes.

**The change.** The department lens is one value, remembered per tab: the Jobs list's Filter gains a Department group ("LX (12)": jobs with an LX call or kit line) that a head's Home tiles pass when they open the list, and the planner's To sort out narrows the same way while always keeping calls with no department ("and 3 calls with no department"), so nobody's call vanishes while the office gives it one. A "You on this job" card, built from the Crew-tab row the device already holds, is drawn for anyone with a booking on the job, staff, freelancer signed in (ADR 0051) or a head booked as crew: "Monitors · Show · Sun 24 to Tue 26 Jan · Call 08:00 · Riverside Racecourse (map)", the call note, "Call sheet ›", "Can't make it any more? ›" opening the link at that booking; for a reader without the jobs, crew and kit abilities it sits above Where it is and the head's crew figure is dropped, for a role with those abilities it sits under Where it is. The Jobs list has a "Mine (2)" chip first for anyone with bookings. The plain address #crew lands a role without crew on People, as the bar does. Home's views group shows only for roles with crew or stock; staff get Me with "The company's week ›" at the foot. Stock's first card for a role without stock is "Broken or missing kit · Report it ›" in the link's words, opening Scan at the report step. The `NotYours` part from Move 4 heads every view that is someone else's work and the Next dock's rest line folds into it, so a staff member reads "The office books the crew." once, in one voice; the Empty part takes the reader's role and never instructs an act they cannot take ("Nobody has asked you to drive yet", not "Book a van for the job"). The warehouse head lands on Home › Warehouse the first time.

**Effect and parts.** User control and consistency (NN/g 3, 4): a lens chosen once is the start of every list; states.md's rule that a thing hidden says so rather than going missing (ADR 0046); Limited Choice (the head's app is small on purpose); Autonomy for staff. List tools with the narrowing line and its undo; the Chip "Mine (2)"; the Empty state written for the reader. The "you on this" part and the "not yours" sentence are learnings for the system.

**Keeps.** ADR 0034 (a button that cannot work for your role is not offered), ADR 0046 (hidden, not missing), ADR 0062, ADR 0051, ADR 0104 and 0109 (one Next; the dock's rest state stays, so Where it is is not folded and no second dock is drawn for one role), the held-up "Showing LX only. Show all departments" line, Home › Me's three cards above the fold, Leave's To approve first. The server keeps holding data back by role: nothing here reads more, it only names what is held back.

**Size and risk.** Four days: two for the Jobs Filter group with its by-department index and the planner's To sort out, a day for the card, the chip, the routes and the Stock card, a day for the Menu lines and the empty states. Between batch 1 (the Stock lens, Move 3) and this move, a head's lens holds on four of five tabs; the doc says so rather than pretending otherwise. A narrowed count must never exceed the company's or show a hidden figure: counts go through the role's view as Crew's do, and e2e access.spec and departments.spec assert what each role sees.

**Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-head/jobs-filter-open-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-head/job-crew-tab-vs-crew-calls-narrowed-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-staff/compare-job-page-vs-link-booking-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-staff/stock-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-staff/my-job-tab-kit-and-vans.png`.

### Move 10 · Home for whoever is looking (S + M)

**What changes:** the Company view orders its tiles for the role that opens it; a director reads one line on whether the week is all right and finds Money on the second screen, not the fifth; each tile says what changed since the last look on this phone; the one "stuck" count becomes a signal; what was lost is summed; and a job in Back with open work stays in reach on Home instead of filing itself under Past.

**The problem** (office-1, director-1, director-3, director-4, jobflow-3 in part, jobflow-2 in part, director-f2 in part). Home's Company view is one list for every role, so the office's two biggest items (73 answers, 50 timesheets) sit seventh and tenth under Housekeeping below warehouse tiles, against the promise in `docs/personas.md`; the director's own lines (Money, the job below zero, the lapsed sales) sit on the fifth phone screen and nothing says in a sentence whether the week is all right; "74 jobs in Planning with the next task down to nobody" is 56% of coming-up jobs, a description of how the company works rather than a signal; nothing says what changed since yesterday and the bell is empty by decision; what was lost is counted but never summed in euro and Home's Money card frames the month in different words from Money's tiles; quotes to chase have no tile; and the day a job's last date passes it leaves Home's Today and Week ahead and the Jobs list files it under Past while its stage is Back and its Next is live.

**The change.** *Batch 1 (S, a morning):* the "next fortnight" set is a function of the viewer's role: for the office it is late, answers, crew still needed, timesheets, no answer and documents, with the rest under Housekeeping; directors keep today's order until the rest lands; heads and the warehouse are unchanged; the Menu's line says Home orders its counts for whoever is looking; a "N quotes worth a call" tile for money roles opens Quotes › To follow up. *Batch 4 (M):* one line above the tiles from Money's own functions, "This week: 4 confirmed jobs on, 2 short of crew or kit · €19,189 confirmed of €22,729 quoted", in the hint style when nothing is wrong and as a warn notice when a fortnight tile is bad; the Money card moves to straight after the week ahead for money roles; "Housekeeping (9)" becomes a disclosure closed by default for directors and open for the office, remembered per phone (the first by-role difference inside one view: Colly's question); each tile carries the stat tile's delta from the device's last-seen mark, "2 new since Tuesday", said as on this phone, and nothing when nothing changed; the "down to nobody" tile counts only jobs starting within fourteen days; a head whose department is the warehouse lands on the Warehouse view the first time and a narrowed Company tile names the department ("2 LX calls still need crew"); Money gains a "Lost or lapsed for November €x" tile summing turned-down quotes and jobs marked lost, By month a Lost column, and Home's Money card reads as Money's tiles do. Home's Today gains "Just finished (n)" for jobs in Back with open work within the fortnight, each with the chip, and Jobs › Past heads "Still in Back (n)" before the months, so the office meets a job in Back without opening Past or the Board.

**Effect and parts.** Salience and Aggregation (the few things that need you stand out, the bad news bundled once); NN/g 1 and 8; healthy is quiet (the line has no wash when the month is fine); Zeigarnik (an open loop out of sight is a loop forgotten). Stat tile with `ds-stat__delta`, Disclosure, Notice. A per-role order inside one view and the on-device delta are learnings.

**Keeps.** ADR 0033 (four views; every count opens its list; a count of nothing is not shown), ADR 0088 (Quotes its own place), ADR 0058 (the bell stays personal; the delta is the smaller answer to director-4 and a company feed is a later question), ADR 0041, 1.84's To sort out on the planner, the director lens's held-ups (the figures agree between Home, Money and the quote page; quiet charts; What's changed).

**Size and risk.** A morning in batch 1; four days in batch 4 across `HomeScreen.tsx`, `standing.ts` and `Money.tsx`. Two people at one Company view see different orders, said in the Menu and in What's changed. "Lost or lapsed" needs a rule for lapsed-but-unmarked jobs (named in the unit line, not the sum) that agrees with By month's note. The verdict line's figures come from Money's functions or the two drift; the tile-equals-list test from Move 3 covers it. Checked by e2e home.spec, counts.spec and quote-follow-up.spec, and the director and office personas reading Home top to bottom.

**Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/01-home-full-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-office/home-office-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/07-money-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/09-notifications-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-jobflow/home-office-desk-light.png`.

### Move 11 · One head and one row for every record (M)

**What changes:** a client, a venue, an item, a place, a vehicle, a quote and a pick list put their name in the page head as a job, a product and a person do, with a sub line of their kind and tabs where their cards were peers; and a fault or a van drawn away from its state group carries its state in a word, not colour alone.

**The problem** (consistency-1, ds-3, ds-4 part 3). ADR 0082 wrote one page head for every record and reached the three built first; seven record kinds put the h1 inside their first card, some under a kicker ("CALL SHEET", "QUOTE"), five repeat the name twice, the pill moves between the head and the card, and a job's two sub-pages name themselves opposite ways. A fault shown on a product page or Home carries its state only in a stripe.

**The change.** The shared page head takes the record's name as the h1 with a sub line in the grammar a product and a person use: client "Client · 12 jobs · Shane Lynch"; venue "Venue · Dublin 1 · 8 jobs here"; item "SH-000047" with "Mic case 24 · Bay C2"; place "Place · 576 items · counted 27 Sep"; vehicle "Van · 241-XX-100 · 3 seats"; the quote keeps the job's name with "Quote · version 2 · Sent" and its pill; the pick list the job's name with "Pick list · Wed 13 Jan to Tue 19 Jan", its pill and the chip, so the two sub-pages read one way. The kickers and the repeated first-card heading go. The item, venue and client pages take the shared Tabs where their cards were peers (item: Faults · Certificates · In it · Counts · Log; venue: Details · On the map · Documents · Jobs here; client: Details · Documents · Jobs); place and vehicle stay one page; the desk's Beside split and the Stock row above a product opened beside the Catalogue stay (audit finding 25), and no desk area row is added to records that have no list beside them. Where a list is not grouped by state (a product's Faults, Home), the row takes the status pill ("Can't go out", "Fit to go out", "Fixed") and the stale comment in `StatusPill.tsx` is brought up to date; a vehicle off the road reads "Off the road until Tue 10 Nov" as a pending pill with the reason as its second line. The card gap inside a record is written once in `app.css` beside the six space roles as Colly decides (section 9).

**Effect and parts.** Consistency and aesthetic-and-minimalist (NN/g 4, 8); Recognition over recall (the head is where the name always is); states.md's rule that a value the tool knows is not plainly true is said in the row. Page head (sess-sys's own, ADR 0082), Tabs, Status pill in the head slot, Chip beside colour. A rule that a record page is one part with one head is a learning.

**Keeps.** ADR 0082 (the records built first keep their shape; this carries the rule to the rest; card headings in sentence case), ADR 0091, the Up grammar, the Repairs list's grouped stripes as the second channel, one date shape, the windowed product list.

**Size and risk.** Three or four days: seven screens take the head and a sub line (half a day each for the four with tabs, an hour for the three without), half a day for the pills, half a day for the screenshot set at phone and desk. Seven screens change at once and "Change details" moves up a few lines; the item page trades one scroll for a tab switch, which ADR 0082 chose for exactly this. One PR per area with a screenshot of each record at 390 and 1440.

**Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-consistency/record-heads-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-warehouse/stock-item-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/current-drawn-seven-ways-phone-dark.png`.

### Move 12 · An owner by role, the hand-off told, and the invoice tail (M)

**What changes:** every task has an owner by role when nobody is named; the two hand-offs the history already records ("Confirmed", "Gone out") reach the people whose turn it is as in-app notices, on Colly's yes; and the office's last step, the invoice, is listed by job on Quotes with a quiet money-only line on the job page.

**The problem** (jobflow-3, jobflow-2 as the sceptic trimmed it, office-4). When the office confirms a job the heads and the warehouse are told nothing: they meet it on tiles seven days before it goes out. Notifications for the office is empty on a company of 300 jobs because notices go only to people named on a job, while tasks are owned by role in practice; the dock says "for the warehouse", the task card "Not given to anyone yet" and the tile "down to nobody": three answers to whose it is. The job's story ends at deprep while the office's ends at invoiced and paid: a job reads "Through every stage · All done" with the client unbilled, and nothing lists jobs to invoice by job.

**The change.** Each task in `shared/src/steps.ts` has a `for` (the office; the heads of the departments on the job's calls and kit; the warehouse, meaning the heads whose department is the warehouse, since ADR 0051 made it a department and not a role), used when no owner is named; the task card reads "For the warehouse (nobody named)" with "Give it to someone" beside it only for the steps ability; the dock's rest line and the tile read the same owner, from the one table Move 4 made. Two notice kinds in `server/src/notices.ts`, read from history rows that already exist and drawn in the app's notification list only, once per transition per job, nobody emailed (ADR 0048): the job going confirmed, to the heads of the departments on it and the warehouse's heads ("Confirmed: Harbour Lights Festival, Mon 9 Nov"), and the first scan out, to the office ("Gone out: Harbour Lights Festival"). Quotes › To follow up gains "To invoice (N)" after "Answered: put the job in step", each row "Job · client · €amount · through since Mon 19 Oct" opening the quote's What happened; the job page, under the task list and only with the money ability, draws one row in the task-list shape, worked out and never ticked: "Invoice · Not invoiced yet" (opens the Quote tab), quiet before Back, amber once the job is through, "Invoiced Mon 2 Nov · paid" when done, never an amount; Home for money roles counts "N jobs through, not invoiced". Jobs that ended before the row ships and were invoiced in the accounts package get a one-off "Not needed" cut-off, so the group starts honest rather than listing every old accepted quote on day one. No ninth task, nothing holds a job in Back, the Board stays money-free.

**Effect and parts.** Default Effect (an owner by role is a default nobody has to set) and Zeigarnik (the office's open invoice in sight); NN/g 1 (a hand-off reaches the person whose turn it is) and 2 (the company's job ends at paid). The notification list, the task-list row shape (ADR 0104 §4) as a role-gated tail without a stage, which is a learning.

**Keeps.** ADR 0109's drain of Back (no task that holds a job there), ADR 0104's five stages and eight tasks under the stored keys, ADR 0048, ADR 0073 (owners are staff by name; the role sits beneath the name), ADR 0042 and 0046 (money only for the office and directors; no amount on the row), the Board without money, Your tasks empty for people never named.

**Size and risk.** Four days: a day for the owner table across `steps.ts`, `steps-view`, the task card and the dock; a day for the two notices; a day and a half for the Quotes group, the row, the Home count and the cut-off; half a day walking it as each role. A head on many jobs sees one notice per confirmed job, which is the truth and bundles as the toast does; if the bell gets noisy the two kinds are one switch to drop. The notices widen ADR 0058's "yours" to "your department's stage": built only on Colly's yes (section 9).

**Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-jobflow/notifications-office-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-jobflow/job-planning-staff-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-office/money-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-jobflow/money-office-desk-light.png`.

### What the moves leave alone

Each lens was asked what already holds and must be kept; the full lists are in section 10. Nothing above touches the Next dock's one filled verb and its rest box, the Where it is card, the scan loop with its one-line answer and Undo, the pick list head, Home › Warehouse's Going out first, Home › Me's three cards, the link's glance line and booking card, `required.ts`'s refusal voice, Confirm's safe-focused buttons, Answers to check, To sort out, the planner's three-day warning, Did you mean, the quote's autosave, the one h1, one width, one Up grammar, one date shape, one pill set, one focus ring, the tokens and reduced motion. Where a move draws a held-up part on a new surface (the dock on the pick list, the chip beside the stage line's words), it draws the same part with the same words.

## 5. The job's journey, enquiry to completion

### Today, surface by surface

The 1 November research gave every job five stages worked out from its own records (Selling, Planning, Going out, On the job, Back), eight tasks inside them and one Next button ([ADR 0104](adr/0104-stages-tasks-and-one-next-button.md)); [ADR 0109](adr/0109-next-and-the-stages-tell-the-truth.md) made them read the records honestly. The sweep mapped every place a job's position is shown, what each reads and the exact words it uses, then walked one made-up job from a new enquiry to deprep on the bench, photographing the job page, the Jobs list, the Board and Home at each stage (`screens/ux-sweep/map-flow/journey-*`).

### Four records, one roll-up

A job's position lives in four places (`shared/src/sync/steps-view.ts:180`, `shared/src/steps.ts`):

1. **The job's status** (`shared/src/jobs.ts:67-79`): enquiry, quoted, confirmed, cancelled, lost. The one thing people decide. Confirmed is the gate ([ADR 0103](adr/0103-how-a-job-moves-on.md)): it puts the job on the calendar and holds its kit.
2. **The quote's status** (`shared/src/quotes.ts:159-161`): draft, sent, accepted, declined, plus the dated facts sent on, answered on, invoiced on and paid on. The quote never moves the job by itself: marking it sent makes the app send a second command that sets the job to quoted (`web/src/jobs/QuoteDraft.tsx:70`), and the client's yes on the quote only puts the job "out of step" until someone presses Confirm the job.
3. **The task rows** (`job_steps`): one per task that has been given an owner, a due date or a tick by hand.
4. **The stage**, worked out on each device: not confirmed → Selling; last day passed → Back; on now → On the job; anything scanned out → Going out; else Planning; and *through* once Back's tasks are closed. Inside each stage sit the eight tasks, each done when ticked by hand or when the app can tell (a sent quote, confirmed offers on every day, scans out and back, the fortnight rule).

Pick counts feed Picked and loaded and Back in; offers feed Crew booked; faults keep deprep open and block the fortnight rule. **Timesheets, invoicing and payment feed nothing in the stage model.** Errands and meetings have no stages. A stopped job (cancelled or lost) has no stage: its pill carries everything.

### What each surface shows

| Surface | Who | What of the job's position it shows | In what words |
|---|---|---|---|
| Job page head | everyone | status pill, crew count, kit summary, dates; **not the stage** | Enquiry · Quoted · Confirmed · Cancelled · Lost; "3 of 5 booked, 1 to confirm"; "7 products, 1 short" |
| Next dock | everyone; the button only for the role whose ability the task needs | "Next · stage · task hint · owner · by due"; one filled verb; rest lines | 16 verbs from "Add the kit to quote" to "Tick off the deprep"; "Nothing to press: on the job until Fri"; "All done. Nothing left to press." |
| Where it is card | everyone; ticks need `steps` | the five-stage strip, the stage ringed, passed ticks (hollow if a task was left open), "In Planning · 3 of 8 tasks done", the open stage's tasks with owner and due | Selling · Planning · Going out · On the job · Back; Quote sent · Confirmed · Crew booked · Kit and vans settled · Picked and loaded · Out on the job · Back in · Checked and deprepped |
| Job page tabs | everyone; Quote only with money | counts | Phases 2 · Crew 1/1 · Kit and vans · Show pack · Quote |
| Quote card and quote page | office, directors | version, net, the last thing that happened, the quote pill; "What happened": sent, answer, invoiced, paid | Draft · Sent · Accepted · Turned down · Invoiced · Paid |
| Jobs list row | everyone | stage line (five segments + stage word + hint), status pill, "Crew b of n", "Kit short", "N tasks late" | "Selling · No quote yet · Still enquiry" + Enquiry; "Planning · No kit on the job yet" + Confirmed; "Through every stage" |
| Board | everyone (no money) | a column per stage with counts; a card with one tone line and owner; a fold of lapsed sales; a sixth column Through | "No quote yet · Still enquiry · 3 days left"; "5 of 10 booked · 7 lines of kit, 1 short"; "Through: nothing still out"; "Deprepped Fri 6 Nov" |
| Home, company view | directors, office | tiles with counts; a row per job with the compact stage word, the status word only when not confirmed, crew count, kit short; **enquiries never on Today or The week ahead; past jobs never on Home** | "Mon 9 Nov · Selling · Quoted"; "N jobs whose dates passed with no answer"; "N jobs with a task late" |
