| Home, department view | heads | tiles narrowed to the department; "Working this week" rows; no stage | "Needs attention in LX"; "Kit still out after a job is the job's, not a department's: it's on Stock." |
| Home, warehouse view | warehouse | "Going out" rows with out/need counts straight to the pick list; "Still out after the job"; no stage | "0 of 27 out"; "Ended Thu 15 Oct · 1 still out" |
| Home, me view | staff | own bookings with the offer pill, call time, offers waiting; no job stage | Offered · Asked for more · Accepted · Confirmed · Declined · Filled · Withdrawn · Can't make it |
| Planner, week and month | everyone | a lane per job; the status word only when tentative; crew short in amber; no stage | "Quoted" / "Enquiry" small; "Load in, 2 of 3 crew, 1 asked" |
| Quotes page | office, directors | groups by what the office must do; quote pill | "Answered: put the job in step"; "Waiting for an answer"; "Sent 9 days ago: worth a call"; "Dates passed with no answer"; "Not sent yet" |
| Money | office, directors | by month; accepted and unpaid, invoiced or not; owed to freelancers; margin; no stage | "Not invoiced yet: accepted, with no invoice yet: yours to invoice." |
| Pick list | warehouse scans; others read | going out or coming back mode; counts per row; **no stage strip, no Next dock, no status pill in the head (it sits inside the first card)** | "2 back, 0 still out, 1 missing"; "Kit shows here once it has gone out with this job." |
| Stock tab cards | warehouse, heads, office | jobs with kit to go out in two weeks; kit still out | "Kit short"; "Going out in the next two weeks"; "Still out after the job" |
| Call sheet (app and link) | office, heads; crew | a pill per person; job status not shown | app: Booked (green) · To confirm (amber) · Offered; link: "to confirm", "the office will confirm" |
| Crew tab and a job's Crew tab | office, heads | offers waiting on the office, calls short, a pill per offer | Confirm · Agree €290 · Release Nessa · Say no · Withdraw |
| Person page | office, heads, directors | a row per offer with its pill; no job stage or status | Offered · Accepted · Confirmed… |
| Freelancer link | freelancers, staff | glance line; offers; bookings; timesheets; **no job status or stage; a cancelled job reads as a withdrawn offer** | "Waiting on you" · "Rate sent to the office" · "Accepted, the office will confirm" · Confirmed · "You declined" · "Filled by someone else" · Withdrawn · "You've pulled out" |
| Notifications | everyone | history lines on what's yours; to-do, sort-out, kit-to-check and quotes-to-chase cards | "Gave 'Picked and loaded' on Harbour Lights Launch to Ben Moran and set it due by Sun 8 Nov" |
| History | everyone | every command in plain words | "Changed the job …: status to confirmed"; "Marked … invoiced on …, as SH-1043" |
| Current RMS cards | office, directors | the opportunity's own words plus the app's reading | "Went ahead" · "Didn't go ahead" · Open; "Order, Active" |

So one job's position is drawn in at least **seven shapes**: a status pill, a five-segment stage line with words, a stage strip with ticks, a Board column, a Next line, a quote pill, and an offer pill on the crew side; and on some surfaces (planner, pick list, call sheet, person page, freelancer link) in none at all.

### What moves a job

Sixteen transitions (`map-flow.json`, transitions). The ones a person presses: make the job (New job, import, from a Current RMS quote); mark the quote sent (which sets the job quoted); record the client's answer; **Confirm the job** (the gate: calendar, kit held); mark it lost or cancelled (which cancels its calls and withdraws its offers on every link); ask for crew and confirm answers; add kit and vans and tick Kit and vans settled while Planning; scan out (the first scan starts Going out) and scan back; tick Checked and deprepped; mark the quote invoiced and paid; give a task to someone with a due date. The ones time does alone: On the job (the first day), Back (the last day passed), lapsed (a sale whose dates passed), through (fourteen days after the end with nothing out and no open fault). Undo exists for most presses (take the answer back, take the tick off, undo a scan, take the invoice back) but not for marking a quote sent, and a confirm is only undone by changing the status under Change details, with no cascade back.

### The gaps against Colly's thirteen steps

| Step | In the app today | What is missing |
|---|---|---|
| Enquiry | Job status Enquiry; Selling with Quote sent open ("No quote yet · Still enquiry") | No "enquiry in" step: a new job starts at "0 of 8 tasks done". Enquiries never appear on Home's Today or The week ahead (`web/src/home/standing.ts:73,85`), only on the Board and the list. |
| Quote | Task Quote sent; the quote's own Draft/Sent pill | Two records kept in step by a second command; a quote sent another way (Current RMS, email) relies on the hand tick. |
| Quote confirmed | Task Confirmed = the gate; the quote's answer recorded alongside | Complete. |
| Crew booking | Task Crew booked from confirmed offers on every day; counts on head, list, Home, Board, planner | Complete: the only step with a count on every surface. |
| Logistics planning | Folded into Kit and vans settled (a hand tick while Planning) | Vans, drivers, transport and subhire have no step of their own; a van booked shows only in the kit hint and the planner. |
| Pick lists | Task Picked and loaded from scans; Going out starts on the first scan; the pick list page; Home's Going out | "Loaded" is not recorded: the task is done when everything is scanned out, on the van or not. The pick list has no stage strip or Next. |
| Preshow production | Nothing in the stage model; the Show pack tab and call sheets exist | No step for "call sheet sent" or "show pack ready"; Going out's line says only "picked, loaded and on its way". |
| Build, show, break | One task Out on the job from the dates; the phases hold Load in, Build, Show, Load out; the dock says "Nothing to press" | Running late and the day cards live on the freelancer link; nothing on the job page says which phase is on today. |
| Logistics back | Nothing; Back starts when the last day passes | Between the last day and the first scan in, the job reads "Back · 2 still out"; the warehouse's Home says "Still out after the job" only once the return days pass. |
| Warehouse check-in | Task Back in from scans; "N never scanned out: check they're back"; faults reported on the pick list | Complete for scanned kit; counted kit shows in the row words. |
| Deprep | Task Checked and deprepped, hand tick only, or the fortnight rule; the hint lists open faults | No deprep screen: nothing lists the job's items back, their condition, cleaning or cases. The tick is the only record. |
| Invoiced | The quote's "What happened" card; the Invoiced pill; Money's "Not invoiced yet" | Not a task or a stage. The job head, strip, list, Board and Home never show it: a job reads "Through every stage" and "All done" with the client unbilled. |
| Paid | The quote's Paid pill; Money's Accepted, unpaid | As above; only the office and directors see any of it, and only on quote and money surfaces. |

### Who learns what, and how

| Hand-off | How the other side learns today |
|---|---|
| The office confirms a job → the warehouse | Nothing pushed. The job appears in Home › Warehouse › Going out within seven days of its start (`HomeScreen.tsx:543`) and on Stock › Pick lists within two weeks; a notification only if they are on the job or hold a task of it. |
| The office confirms a job → the head of department | Nothing pushed. The department's Home tiles count calls short and kit lines short once calls and lines exist; the Board's Planning column; a task given to them shows on Your tasks and in Notifications. |
| Kit short on a confirmed job → office, heads | Worked out on the device: the Home tile, Stock › Kit short, the list flag, the Board line, the dock's "Sort 1 short on the kit". Never a notification, because nothing is a command. |
| A freelancer declines, counters or pulls out → whoever sent the offer | Notifications, Crew › Answers to check (until Noted), the Home tile. A pull-out frees the place. |
| The office marks a job lost or cancelled → crew on its calls | Their link's tag becomes Withdrawn; the office is offered a "tell the crew" panel afterwards; Notifications for staff on it. |
| The warehouse scans kit out → office, heads | The stage line and the Board column move. No notification. |
| The show ends → the warehouse | Home › Warehouse › Still out after the job and the bad tile, only after the job's return days (default 1); the pick list defaults to Coming back; the Board's Back column. |
| Kit is back and faults reported → whoever closes the job | Nobody is told. The dock says "Tick off the deprep"; the Board's Back column holds it; with nothing out and no fault it closes itself after fourteen days. |
| The job is through → office, directors, to invoice | Only Money › Accepted, unpaid › Not invoiced yet and the Home Money hint. Nothing on the job page or Board links the end of a job to invoicing. |
| A timesheet sent → office, heads | The Home tile, Crew › Timesheets › To approve, Notifications. |
| A task given → a member of staff | Notifications, Home › Your tasks; for the office, the tiles "jobs in Planning with the next task down to nobody" and "jobs with a task late". |

Three kinds of hand-off exist, then: a **push** (Notifications and Home tiles, for crew answers, timesheets and tasks), a **computed signal** (kit short, late tasks, lapsed sales: visible wherever the office looks, never announced), and **nothing** (a job confirmed, a job gone out, a show over, a job through and uninvoiced). The warehouse and the heads hear about new work only by looking, and nobody hears that a job has ended.

### Where the words disagree

- **Confirmed** names three things: the job's status pill, the Confirmed task (the client's answer) and an offer's state. A list row can read "Planning · Confirmed · Crew 1 of 1" where Confirmed is the job and the crew's own confirmed-ness is the count (`JobsScreen.tsx:449-458`, `steps.ts:61`, `Calls.tsx:60`).
- The same crew state has three words: the app's pill says Accepted, the call sheet's says To confirm, the freelancer's tag says "Accepted, the office will confirm"; the sheet calls a confirmed person Booked while every other surface says Confirmed (`Calls.tsx:56-65`, `SheetScreen.tsx:251`, `server/src/crew/page.ts:118-127`, `sheet.ts:110`).
- A declined quote is Turned down on the pill and the Quotes page, "Client turned it down" on the button, declined in the record and "The client said no" on the task.
- The same stage hint reads differently per surface for the same job on the same day: list "Selling · No quote yet · Still enquiry", Board "No quote yet · Still enquiry · 3 days left", dock "Next · Selling · No quote yet", Home "Selling · Quoted".
- Task labels are past-tense states (Quote sent, Picked and loaded, Back in) but Confirmed doubles as the status word and Out on the job is a present state; the dock's verb for one task changes with the data (Make a quote / Finish the quote / Open the quote / Add the kit to quote).
- A job through its fortnight reads "Through: nothing still out" on the Board, "All done. Nothing left to press." in the dock, "Through every stage" on the list row and "Taken as done: it ended … with nothing still out" on its tasks.
- The warehouse's Home rows, the department's Working this week rows, Stock › Pick lists and the pick list page carry no stage at all, though the Next rules in the research name the pick list as a place Next belongs.
- Once a job's dates pass it drops off Home entirely while it still has open work in Back; the office meets it only on the Board or under Jobs › Past, where its stage is Back with "Tick off the deprep" pending (`standing.ts:72-89`, `JobsScreen.tsx:89-92`).
- The task hints are matched by regular expressions to build the dock's buttons and the Board's tones (`NextDock.tsx:121-136`, `BoardScreen.tsx:104-136`), so the wording in `steps-view.ts` is silently load-bearing: a change of words changes behaviour.
- Roles without money read the Board and list differently for the same job: a director sees "A draft is started · Still enquiry · 6 days left" and "Quote with the client since Tue 3 Nov" where a head or staff member sees "No quote yet", because quotes aren't sent to them.

### What the programme does to the journey

The job's position already lives in one shared view (`steps-view.ts`); what the surfaces disagree about is how to say it. So the programme does not add a record or a stage. It makes four things one:

- **One position, one part.** Move 7 puts the facts a stage is made of on the view as fields, removes the regexes that made the hint text load-bearing, and draws one position chip on the job head, the Jobs row, Home's rows, the Board, the dock's line and the pick list's head, with the time it has left or has sat. The status pill is kept for what the stage cannot say: a sale (Enquiry, Quoted) or a stop (Lost, Cancelled). Lost and cancelled keep reading the same everywhere.
- **One vocabulary.** Move 4's table gives a crew booking one word on both sides of the phone call, keeps "Confirmed" for the client's yes alone, qualifies every "Waiting", names the time groups once and gives the pull-out one name on the link, the office's pill and the history line.
- **One count.** Move 3 makes the kit-still-out rule one function under ADR 0109, so Home, the Board, the Warehouse view and Stock agree on which jobs still have kit to check back in and how late they are; every other count is the length of the list it opens.
- **An end the office can see.** Move 12 lists jobs to invoice by job on Quotes and draws one quiet money-only line on the job page, without a ninth task that would hold every uninvoiced job in Back; Move 10 keeps a job in Back on Home under Just finished and heads Jobs › Past with Still in Back, so a job with open work no longer files itself as finished the day its dates pass.

The hand-offs change from pulls to pushes only where Colly says so: Move 12's two in-app notices, "Confirmed" to the heads of the departments on the job and "Gone out" to the office, from history rows that already exist, with nobody emailed or texted. The link tells a freelancer what the office changed after their yes (Move 6), which is the one hand-off that reaches outside the office today with no words at all. Everything else stays a computed signal, which is right for kit short and late tasks: they are not commands.

### The thirteen steps after the programme

| Step | After batch 5 | Still open |
|---|---|---|
| Enquiry | Chip "Selling · No quote yet · 3 days left" on every row; "Quote: with the office" for roles not sent the quote | No "enquiry in" record; an enquiry with dates this week still never on Home's Today |
| Quote | One selling line everywhere, with the age and "worth a call"; "N quotes worth a call" on Home | The second command and the hand tick for a quote sent outside the app; no Undo on marking sent |
| Quote confirmed | Complete; "Confirmed" means only this | |
| Crew booking | "Booked" on both sides; "Still needs crew"; the crew gap toned on the chip; a head's "LX 0 of 1 · all 5 of 10" | |
| Logistics planning | | Vans, drivers and transport have no task, no chip fact and no hand-off (ADR 0047 books a van on the job; nothing reads it into the stages) |
| Pick lists | The pick list's own Next and chip; the kit fact a link; the Warehouse view's rows one tap to the pick | "Loaded" is still not recorded, so Going out's line is still a guess |
| Preshow production | | Nothing says the call sheet was sent or the show pack is ready |
| Build, show, break | "You on this job" for anyone booked; "until Fri" on the chip | Nothing on the job page says which phase is on today for the office and heads |
| Logistics back | Home › Warehouse counts never-scanned kit and ages it; the Board's Back head says how many are late | No transport-back record; show over → warehouse is a signal, not a hand-off |
| Warehouse check-in | Complete: one kit-back rule, the item row says out or missing, the pending scan pill, "Repairs" | |
| Deprep | "Tick off the deprep" stays the one record; the dock on the pick list says it | No items-back list; kit back with a fault open tells nobody |
| Invoiced | "To invoice (N)" on Quotes, the quiet row on the job, the Home count, "Just finished" | The word for the row is Colly's; a cut-off for old jobs |
| Paid | The quote's pill; the freelancer's "Work done" ends at Paid | The crew side waits on the timesheets switch |

The five empty cells are the next job-flow phase's questions (section 9), and they are the same five the journey map found before any move was proposed. A programme built from themes cannot close a gap that never became a theme; it can make the gaps visible, which the table does.

## 6. Each person's app

### What each kind of person meets today

The roles mapper opened the app as each role on a phone and recorded the navigation, Home, every screen and action offered, what is hidden or locked and in what words, and every word on their screens that belongs to another role's world. The sweep then walked a day as each persona (the six lenses below). This is the shared picture.

### The same app, with buttons removed

| Surface | Director | Office | Head of department | Staff crew | Freelancer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Phone bar | Home · Jobs · Stock · Crew (74) · Menu; Quotes in the desk sidebar | the same | the same, Crew badge for their department (8) | the same, no badge; Crew opens on People | the link page: no app, no bar |
| Home default | Company (17 tiles, Today, charts, The week ahead, The season, Money) | identical | their department when known: "Needs attention in LX", the department's charts, Working this week | Me: Your day, Your work, Your leave; the other three views still offered and say "Nothing here for you to sort out" | the glance line, offers, timesheets, bookings, days off, details, documents, past work |
| Jobs list and job page | everything; Change details, Add phase, Ask for crew, Add kit, Book a vehicle, the Quote tab, the dock | the same | no New job, Change details, Add phase or Quote tab; Ask for crew (with a rate hint), Add kit, Book a vehicle, the dock | read only; the dock says "Next is kit and vans settled, for the heads of department. Nothing here for you to press."; a task says "Not given to anyone yet." | nothing of the company's: only their own offers and bookings |
| Board, planner | full | full | full; quote lines read "No quote yet" where a draft exists | the same as a head; the planner's "To sort out · Find crew" leads to actions they lack | none |
| Quotes, Money | full | full | "Quotes are for the office and directors. A director can change your role on the Crew tab." | the same refusal | none |
| Stock | all nine rows; add, scan, sort out, count, label, track | the same | the same | look only: Report damage or missing, the purchase list; "Whoever keeps the stock scans kit out and back here."; a product's "VALUE OF ONE €8,869" | "The purchase list ›", "Tell the warehouse ›" |
| Crew | Answers to check (74), Crew needed, offers, Ask for crew | the same | their department only: "Showing LX only"; Confirm and Release; "The office agrees rates: your role doesn't see them." | Crew needed only, names, no actions | none |
| People, a person | everything incl. Erase, role picker | the same, role picker read-only | Their link only in their department; "Hidden for your role" on rates | no level or rate; Call and WhatsApp; "Your role doesn't see …'s email, notes or company. The office has them." | none |
| Leave, Timesheets | approve; To approve (50) with Check on every row | the same | approver's To approve; Timesheets reads "Waiting on the office (50)", amounts hidden, while the Crew row still says "Timesheets 50" | My leave only; "No timesheets of yours yet" | timesheets with € on the link; no leave |
| Admin, Menu | everything; Menu 9 Jobs, 10 Stock, 5 Crew lines | the same minus two director cards | no Admin; Menu 5 Jobs, 9 Stock, 4 Crew lines | Menu 4 Jobs, 9 Stock, 4 Crew lines (Leave and Timesheets kept) | none |
| Notifications | the list plus the To sort out, to-do and quotes cards | the same | the same | the list only (About you, A job you're on) | none |

Directors and the office are **identical** apart from two Admin cards and the role picker. A head's app is the office's with New job, Change details, Quotes and Money taken out and the Crew tab narrowed. A staff member's app is the same five tabs with most buttons removed: Jobs, Stock and Crew are lists of other people's work with "Nothing here for you to press" where the actions were. The freelancer has a different product altogether, and it is the only one built around its person.

### Words from another world

- **Staff crew and heads read the whole Selling vocabulary**: "Selling · Still quoted", "A draft is started · Still enquiry", the Board's "Quote with the client since…", Home's "48 enquiries and quotes not yet decided", the stage strip's "Selling". Money words reach them too: a product's "Value of one €8,869", a kit tab's "All subhired from Galway Lighting", the Menu's "Timesheets: To approve, and not in yet" for people who approve nothing.
- **"The warehouse" is named as a role** in the dock's "whose" line ("for the warehouse"), in Counting ("The warehouse and heads of department do the counts") and on the freelancer's "Tell the warehouse ›", though [ADR 0051](adr/0051-freelancer-role-replaces-warehouse.md) made it a department.
- **Who owns a task is said three ways**: the dock's "the heads of department", "the warehouse", "whoever books the crew", "the office"; the task card's "Not given to anyone yet." / "Down to Ben."; the refusal's "Ask the office or a head of department to give it to you."
- **"Not your role" is worded four ways**: the page refusal ("Quotes are for the office and directors. A director can change your role on the Crew tab."), the command refusal ("Only the office and directors can … Your role is Head of department; a director can change it on your person on the Crew tab."), the person page's "Hidden for your role" and "Your role doesn't see …", and the You page's role line.
- **A head's counts disagree across surfaces**: the Crew badge counts their department (8), Home › Company counts the company (73 answers, 7 calls short), Home › Department counts LX (8 and 1), Crew needed after narrowing says "Show all 4 calls" and the Crew row says "Timesheets 50" while their Timesheets page withholds the amounts.
- **The Menu is gated by abilities only**, so lines whose page then refuses or empties still show for staff and freelancers: "Calls: Crew needed, and answers to check", "Timesheets: To approve, and not in yet", "Labels: Print labels for kit", "Counting: Stocktakes and rolling counts".
- **Empty states offer what the role can't do**: a staff member's Kit tab says "Book a van for the job … with a driver from the crew"; the Phases empty state is blank for them; the planner's "To sort out · Find crew" is drawn for everyone.
- **The freelancer's link stays in the crew's words** (Load out, Build, Needs: Safe Pass, Rate, Reply by, "the office will confirm", "Filled by someone else") with no stage words at all. Signed in to the app as a freelancer (ADR 0051), they would meet everything a staff member meets.

### What each kind of person meets after the programme

The six role lenses each named the one thing that would change their next visit; the moves were judged on whether every one of them gets it in batch 1 and the rest in order. By role:

**The director** reads Money's By month and Margin by job whole on a phone (batch 1), counts that agree with the lists they open (batch 1), and then one line above Home's tiles on whether the week is all right, Money on the second screen, "2 new since Tuesday" on the tiles, Housekeeping folded, a "Lost or lapsed" figure that Home and Money say in the same words (batch 4). The bell stays personal by decision; the delta is the smaller answer, and a company-wide feed is a later question. What they keep: every count opening its list, one tap to a job head, the figures agreeing, quiet charts, What's changed.

**The office** gets numbers that match the lists they open and Home ordered for their four questions, with a quotes-to-chase tile (batch 1); a tap from a tile or a notice landing on the part it named (batch 1); one vocabulary and one forms family with no greyed Save (batch 2); one job reading the same on Home, the list, the Board and its head, with the Planning column folded by week (batch 3); jobs to invoice by job and "Gone out" told in the app (batch 5). What they keep: one filled red Next, Answers to check, To sort out, Quotes › To follow up, the planner's three-day warning, Did you mean, the quote's autosave.

**The head of department** opens Repairs, Testing and Kit short from the Stock tab and reads their department, with the row's counts agreeing with Home, their leave count counting what they approve, no timesheet count for a list the page then calls the office's, the job head reading "LX 0 of 1 · all 5 of 10" with their calls first, and "Quote: with the office" instead of "No quote yet" (batch 1); the narrowing line naming both their department's names (batch 2); the Jobs list and the planner's To sort out starting on their department, keeping calls with no department in view (batch 3); "Confirmed: <job>" in their bell on Colly's yes (batch 5). Between batches 1 and 3 their lens holds on four of five tabs. What they keep: Home › Department, "Showing LX only. Show all departments", Leave's To approve first, what they cannot do said in words rather than greyed.

**The warehouse** sees the twelve jobs with kit to check back in on their Home view with "ended 4 days ago" on each, the strip back on Stock, a searched or scanned item saying it is out with a job since a date, "Waiting to sync" on an unsent scan, and the day's work one tap from the job head (batch 1); the pick list with its own Next and position (batch 3); a job's first day landing them on their own view and the owner by role on each task (batches 4, 5). Stock's order stays the catalogue first until Colly says otherwise. What they keep: Going out first, the pick list head, the scan loop with Undo, the fault loop closing on the job, offline scanning, the Testing intro.

**Staff crew** stop being able to record an absence on the link that skips approval, and read "Leave" and "Your bookings" in the app and on the link alike with one glance line (batch 1); read "The office books the crew." once, in one voice, with the Menu written for them (batch 2); open a job they are booked on with "You on this job" first, find "Mine (2)" on Jobs, land on People from the bar and the address alike, and find "Broken or missing kit · Report it ›" on Stock (batch 3); answer offers in the app once Colly says so (batch 5, its own ADR). A signed-in freelancer (ADR 0051) meets the same, because the card and the chip are gated on having a booking, not on a role. What they keep: Home › Me's three cards, plain refusals, Leave's figures, colleagues' numbers with Call and WhatsApp.

**The freelancer** reads 16px fields with one filled yes and a refusal in the app's words, "Call 07:30 (was 09:00)" for seven days after the office moves a call, "The job was cancelled" as the first line of a booking on a cancelled job, one name for pulling out, and past work folded with a count (batch 1); a page two screens shorter with one "Work done" list ending at Paid, set-once prose folded to a line each, and a "Since you said yes" block only when something changed (batch 2); "Booked" as the one word for a booking on both sides once Colly answers. Nothing is sent: the page reads differently next time it is opened. What they keep: the glance line's three parts, the booking card, the folds after three, posts returning to the card, the counter flow, the two-tap timesheet, "You" first on the sheet.

The two things every role meets, and which no single lens could see, are the chip and the word table: one position in one shape with one vocabulary, from the director's Board to the freelancer's booking row.

## 7. The forms

### The research, and what of it applies

Colly asked for "additional form UX research". A research pass opened 58 sources (Nielsen Norman Group, Baymard Institute, the GOV.UK Design System and Service Manual, GDS design notes, Adam Silver, Luke Wroblewski, Smashing Magazine, WCAG 2.2, alphagov's accessible-autocomplete, the Cloudscape and Atlassian systems, Material Web; Apple's HIG and m3.material.io render by script and returned no text, so they are not cited) and set each finding against what the shared design system already says. The system already carries most of the field-level research: labels outside and above, validation on blur or submit with the fault cleared on the fixing keystroke, error wording, undo over confirm, units as affixes, 16px fields and 44px targets, the combobox and the line-item editor. Nineteen such rules are listed in the sweep's working files as *already in the system* and are not proposed again. What the research adds is mostly about the **shape and flow of a form**, where the system is silent. The verdicts follow the system's own adopt / adapt / reject form.

| Verdict | Rule | Why, and the evidence | Where it bites in sess-sys |
|---|---|---|---|
| Adopt | **One column.** Two or three inputs share a line only when they are one thing (from and to, day and rate, name parts). | Baymard 2023: multicolumn forms were misread and skipped more, even with separators. NN/g 2016: a form that followed the guidelines got 78% error-free first submissions against 42%. | `.grid-form` is `1fr 1fr` with no phone override (`web/src/app.css:328`): the new job form, Ask for crew, Change the call, the person form and Admin's Office, Travel, Quoting and Labels cards. |
| Adopt | **A field is as wide as its answer.** Fixed widths for fixed-length values, one default for names and emails, a short box for a count, a rate or a phone. | Baymard 2010: a field much longer or shorter than the answer made testers doubt the label. GOV.UK ships 2, 3, 4, 5, 10 and 20 character widths. | The two-column grid gives every field half the row, so Qty, Day rate, Reply by, Call time and VAT number are as wide as Job. |
| Adapt | **Required is the default and unmarked; "(optional)" goes in the label**, never the placeholder; a form that is mostly optional marks the needed ones instead. | GOV.UK question pages; NN/g 2019; Baymard 2018 (32% hit an error when only optional was marked; 14% of checkouts mark both). The sources disagree on which to mark but agree on the cost of marking nothing. | 47 fields carry `required` with no visible mark; optional-ness is said in placeholders that vanish on typing ("Reason (optional)" `Leave.tsx:621`; "Optional, e.g. …" in the rack forms). `Timesheets.tsx:473` already does it right. |
| Adopt | **Every field opens the right keyboard and gets autofill**: `inputmode` numeric or decimal (not `type=number`), `tel` with `autocomplete=tel`, email, given-name and family-name; spellcheck off on names, codes and emails. | Baymard 2015: numeric keys about five times larger; 79% of sites neglected autocorrect; `type=number` changes a value on scroll. GOV.UK text input, telephone numbers, names. WCAG 1.3.5. | 76 `inputMode`s are mostly right; 22 `type="number"` remain; name fields set `autoComplete="off"` (`People.tsx:287-290`); the Office card's phone and email have no autocomplete tokens. The freelancer's "Save my number" is the model. |
| Adopt | **A submit button is never disabled.** A press on an incomplete form says what is missing by the field, with a one-line summary above the button when more than one thing is missing. | Adam Silver 2023; Smashing 2021; GOV.UK button and error summary; NN/g 2019. A disabled button explains nothing, feels broken when one of several faults is fixed, and sits off-screen on a phone. | Offer a call is `disabled={!chosen || …}` (`Calls.tsx:622`): a head on a phone sees a dead Offer with no word why. Sign in (`SignIn.tsx:104`), the Data card (`DataCard.tsx:88,144`). `required.ts` focuses the first empty field only. |
| Adapt | **A fault's field and its message scroll into view clear of the keyboard, the sticky readout and the footer dock**; focus lands on the field when there is only one fault. | WCAG 2.2 2.4.11 Focus Not Obscured; Smashing 2022 (the keyboard covers what is below the field). The system keeps the error below the field; keeping that and guaranteeing visibility costs less than moving every error. | The quote's sticky readout, the import and kit-from-notes `.bring` bar, the sticky top and On this screen bands and the phone dock all sit over form content; no `scroll-padding` is set. |
| Adapt | **Dates the office picks within weeks stay a native date input that can also be typed**; a date someone knows (Reply by, leave) offers typing and shortcuts; the end never before the start; three fields only for a memorable date typed by the public. | NN/g 2017; GOV.UK dates; GDS 2013 (three fields won for dates of birth because the public types them from memory); Smashing 2017 and 2021. Show dates a few weeks out, picked by crew who know the week, suit the picker. | 51 `type="date"` inputs; `min={p.start}` already blocks an end before the start. Reply by could default to a careful office choice and show as changed when edited; "Same as the job" for a phase's dates. |
| Adopt | **Each field earns its place** (why it is asked, what is done with it, who must answer); what the app already knows is filled in, not asked again; rarely used fields fold. | GOV.UK form structure (the question protocol); WCAG 2.2 3.3.7 Redundant Entry (a Level A failure inside one process); Baymard 2024 (8 fields needed, 11.3 on average); NN/g 2016. | Ask for crew asks Project even when opened from a job that has one (`Calls.tsx:712`); Change the call re-asks Project and Role; the new job form's phases ask dates most jobs share with the job. |
| Already | Two to five fixed choices are radios or a segmented control; a select only for six to about fifteen; beyond that the combobox. | NN/g 2020 and 2017; GOV.UK select (a last resort). The system's Limited Choice and Combobox say it; NN/g's thresholds sharpen it. | The new job form's Status (three live options) and Kind are selects; the offer form picks one person from up to ninety in a `<select>` with optgroups (`Calls.tsx:615-621`). |
| Already | Picking a client, venue, phase name or person uses the record-picking combobox, never a `<datalist>` or a long `<select>`. | alphagov accessible-autocomplete (a count said aloud, show all, confirm on blur); the system's Combobox, drawn from sess-sys's own product picker. | New job's Client and Venue are datalists with a Did-you-mean helper under each (`JobsScreen.tsx:521-528`); phase names a datalist; Tidy up's client field a datalist. |
| Adapt | **One save model per kind of view, said in words**: a draft someone builds saves as it goes ("Saved" / "Not saved yet"); a record's details and a setting keep Save with the changed-from-default mark; a switch acts at once and never sits in a form that also has Save. | NN/g toggles 2018; Atlassian and Cloudscape inline edit (saving on blur is not obvious on its own); the system's rule 11. The evidence for autosave itself is practitioner-level; the two failure modes are consistent across every source. | Quote lines save as they go (right); person details and the Admin cards have Save (right); Travel has a `role="switch"` inside a card whose other fields wait for Save (`TravelCard.tsx:124,150`). |
| Already | A table of small forms is the line-item editor: one row open at a time, Done and Remove inside, nothing lost on a stray blur; no Remove on a closed row. | Cloudscape; Atlassian; Smashing 2023 (hover-revealed icons cause mis-taps); the system's Line-item editor. | The quote editor follows it; the new job form's phase rows carry a Remove link on every closed row (`JobsScreen.tsx:554-563`). |
| Adapt | **The primary button sits at the start of the form's own column under the last field**, full width only on a phone; the secondary follows quieter; a destructive action never beside it; no Reset or Clear. | Adam Silver 2019 (Wroblewski's eye-tracking: one vertical axis); GOV.UK button; NN/g 2008 (consistency beats order; the dangerous one never default); NN/g 2000. | `button.primary.wide` spans the two-column grid: a 600px button on a desk (`JobsScreen.tsx:571`, `Calls.tsx:752`). Five forms end five ways. |
| Reject | No sticky Save on a phone form. Sticky only for a running readout or a bring-with-you action over a long list, with scroll padding. | Smashing 2023 (the keyboard takes up to 60% of the screen); WCAG 2.4.11; Hoober's edge targets (about 46px at the bottom). The only pro-sticky evidence was unpublished retail anecdotes. | Keep the quote readout and the import bar sticky; do not add a sticky Save to the new job form, Ask for crew or the person form. |
| Reject | One form, not one thing per page or a wizard, for the office's daily forms; one question per page only on the freelancer's link; the stepper with owners is GOV.UK's task list. | GOV.UK form structure names fast-switching internal users as the exception; NN/g 2017: a wizard punishes repeated expert use; GOV.UK task list research (statuses looked like buttons). | The freelancer's server-drawn forms already are one question with one button. Check the job's Where it is card against the task list: sentence-case statuses, done ones quiet, nothing that looks pressable but is not. |
| Adapt | A check-your-answers step only before something that contacts someone or costs money and cannot be undone. | GOV.UK check answers; NN/g 2018 (each needless confirm weakens the next); the system's rule 1. | The quote email is the review; Ask for crew needs none while it contacts nobody; when the messages switch is on, the Offer press should name who will be texted. |
| Adopt | **Hints are one short sentence under the label, before the input**; a placeholder holds only a real example of the format, never an instruction, a default or "None". | GOV.UK question pages and dates; NN/g placeholders 2014; Adam Silver 2018; alphagov (no placeholder). | 161 placeholders; instructions among them: "Pick or add one", "Paste the client's email…", "A Google Maps link, or 53.1336, -7.1512", "None" as a value word on number fields (`QuoteLine.tsx:253`, `TravelCard.tsx:146`). |
| Adopt | A phone number is one field that accepts what people type, is checked for shape, and is never masked or rewritten under them. | GOV.UK telephone numbers; NN/g mobile checklist; Baymard 2015. | The person form's phone and the Office card's phone; the freelancer's "Save my number" already does it. |
| Adopt | Sign-in never needs a transcription: paste and password managers work; the freelancer link is the sign-in. | WCAG 2.2 3.3.8 Accessible Authentication; G218. | Already so (`SignIn.tsx:102`, `/f/<token>`); the rule is to keep it. |
| Adopt | The same refusal three times is a design fault: a format the crew keep typing becomes accepted; a field they keep leaving empty gets a default or goes. | NN/g 2019; Smashing 2022; NN/g forgiving input 2015. | Nothing counts refusals per form today; the history words name every command and could. |
| Already | A server-drawn refusal keeps everything typed, prefixes the title with "Error:", focuses a summary when two fields can fail, and repeats each message by its field. | GOV.UK validation, error summary, error message; Adam Silver 2018; the system's "Without script". | The timesheet page and the details form already redraw with what was typed; "Error:" in the title and a summary for the extras rows are the gaps. |
| Already | Targets at least 24px everywhere and 44px on a phone (about 46px at the bottom edge). | WCAG 2.2 2.5.8; Smashing 2023; the system's density table. | Check Remove on phase rows, the quote line's quiet Remove, chip removes and the `.bring` bar's buttons at 390px. |

The 23-line checklist the forms reviewer worked from, and the sources with their addresses, are in the sweep's working folder (`session-hire/ux-sweep-2026-11-06-work/research-forms.json`).

### The forms family today

The forms mapper opened every form in the app and on the freelancer's link as the role that uses it, on a phone and a desk, and recorded each pattern and where it varies. Twelve patterns, eighteen variations.

| Pattern | How it is done today |
|---|---|
| Label placement | Label above the control inside a `<label>`, muted hint-size; `.grid-form` is two columns with `.wide` for a full-width field (`web/src/app.css:328-330`) on the new job form, job details, phases, Ask for crew, Change the call, kit lines, Add person, person details, Add product, product, item and place forms, Report damage, Leave, client and venue. `.office-form` is the same with a tighter gap on Admin; `.field` is single-column on Leave allowance, the timesheet check, Mark days off and Counting. The freelancer's server-drawn forms are a fourth family. |
| Required marking | No visual mark anywhere. An empty required field is only known on submit, when `required.ts` puts "Fill in the {label} first." under it with focus and `aria-invalid`. Forty-seven fields carry `required`. The link's pages let the browser's own bubble refuse ("Please fill out this field."). Optional is said in words on a few labels ("(optional)"), in placeholders on others, and not at all on most. |
| Hints and placeholders | Placeholders carry examples ("e.g. …", "250"), instructions ("Pick or add one", "Type to find a product", "From its label; empty for the next free one", "Why anything changed"), value words ("None", "0", "€", "Never, or e.g. 12") and sample content. Hints come as `p.hint` after the field, `small` inside the label, `p.muted` with `aria-describedby`, or a paragraph at the top of the form. |
| Save model | (a) Save and Cancel, the form closes: job details, phases, kit lines, Change the call, person, product, item, place, client, venue, faults. (b) One submit, the form stays and clears, a `role=status` line says what happened: Add phase ("Added Build, Thu 12 Nov."), Ask for crew, Add item, places; Add person clears with no line. (c) Submit then go to the new record: New job, New quote. (d) Saves as it goes with a head word: the quote draft ("Saved"). (e) Pending pill beside Save: Admin cards. |
| Buttons | Edit forms: an `.actions` row at the foot, primary first, Cancel plain, a destructive link third ("Remove phase", "Take off the kit"), content-width (Save job 89px, Cancel 76px). Person edit: Save and Cancel as two equal 156px halves. Add forms: one full-width submit, primary on Add job, Add product, Add item, Add to kit, Ask for crew and Apply for leave but plain (`className="wide"`) on Add phase, Add person, Log day in lieu and the product forms. `button.primary.wide` spans the two-column grid: 600px on a desk. |
| Destructive asks | An inline `<Confirm>` (the question, the yes, the safe no focused) replaces the actions for Remove phase, Take off the kit, Remove product or place, Added by mistake, Withdraw, Cancel the call, Cancel approved leave, Discard the count, New link, stopping a job with open calls. No ask on removing a phase row from the new job form, a timesheet extra, a contact, a document or a day off. The freelancer's Decline is folded behind a link, a third way. |
| Dates | Native `<input type=date>` everywhere, in From and To pairs with the end held after the start and bumped when the start passes it (51 of them). Labels vary: "From / To", "Off from / Off until", "Phase 1 from / to" (aria only), "Day worked", "Expires", "The new one runs out", "Reply by", "Sent to the client on". |
| Numbers and units | Money is always text with `inputMode=decimal`; the unit is in the label ("Day rate €", "Value of one, in euro", "Fuel price, € a litre"), inside the field as a UnitBox (the quote's € before, % and days after), after the box as a span, or only a column heading. Counts and sizes are `type="number"` (22 fields) with no in-app refusal under the minimum, so a 0 in "How many" draws nothing and the form stays open. Placeholders for the same kind of value are "250", nothing, "0", "None", "Agreed rate" and "€". |
| Pickers | Client, Venue, phase names, Category, Department and Where it's kept are a `<datalist>` with a "new" or "near {name}" line under; Add kit and Kit from the notes use the ProductPicker combobox (ADR 0070); Add person's skills a chips combobox (ADR 0063); Status, Kind, For, the call's Department, Type, Level, Charged, VAT, the item's Product and the offer's person (up to ninety, in optgroups) are native `<select>`s. |
| Row editors | The new job form's phases are a row of name, from, to and a Remove link on every row with "Add a phase" under; timesheet extras are What, € and Remove with "Add an extra" (the link's sheet has three fixed blank rows instead); client contacts are a block per contact with "Add a contact"; quote lines open one at a time in place with Done and Remove with undo; kit lines and crew calls open a grid form inside the article through a "Change" link. |
| Where a refusal sits | (1) The empty-required line under the field, orange wash, field outlined, focus moved, read aloud. (2) `<Refusal>` as a wide line above the action row on most forms. (3) After the `</form>` under the Save button on Admin's Office and Travel cards and the person page. (4) Under the field in the quote's line editor, amber. (5) In place of the total on the timesheet. |
| Success | A `role=status` line inside the form; navigation to the record; the form closing; a pending pill beside Save; "Saved" in the quote's head; a flash at the card and a sent banner on the link; nothing at all after Add person. |

The eighteen variations, in one list:

1. Add-form submits are primary on some forms and plain on others for the same act.
2. Cancel is a link on New quote and a plain button on every other edit form; the phase move question uses "Back".
3. The job is labelled "Job", "What's the job?" and "Project" on three forms, so one refusal reads "Fill in the what's the job? first."
4. The refusal line sits above the actions, below the Save button, under the field or in place of the total, by form.
5. The app marks nothing until submit; the link's pages say "(optional)" and let the browser refuse empties.
6. A number under its minimum gets no in-app refusal (`required.ts` handles only a missing value).
7. Units sit in the label, in the field, after the field or only in a column heading.
8. Days someone cannot work are three different forms (the person page, the link, Leave).
9. Save and Cancel are equal halves on the person form and content-width everywhere else.
10. After adding, some forms navigate, some stay with a line, one stays silent.
11. Four hint styles.
12. Tick controls in five looks and three sizes (22px `.tick`, 20px `.needs` chips, native checkboxes, the radio-as-button, the switch drawn as a checkbox).
13. Some removals ask, some do not, and the link's Decline is folded.
14. Primary labels are verb and object on most, bare "Save" on two, amount-specific on others ("Approve €640").
15. Note fields are named six ways ("Notes", "Note", "Details for crew", "Notes for the client", "Anything whoever approves should know", "Why anything changed").
16. Money placeholders differ field to field.
17. Control heights are 44px in the app, 48px on the link, 58px for one Admin button.
18. The staff Leave screen asks "Who are you?" with a select before any form, unlike every other screen, which reads the role from sign-in or the preview.

### The rules proposed, and where each lands

The research's adopt and adapt rows become one Form family (Move 5) plus one red-fill rule (Move 1). Each row in the table above lands in a named place:

| Rule | Where it lands | Batch |
|---|---|---|
| One column; two inputs share a line only when they are one thing | `.grid-form` one column under 640px with a `.pair` wrapper (From and To, First name and Last name); the link's days-off dates one column | 2 (app), 1 (link) |
| A field as wide as its answer | A width cap for short answers at a desk (about 8em for a count, a rate or a date) instead of half the grid | 2 |
| Required unmarked; "(optional)" in the label text | A label helper writes "(optional)" in the label's muted colour; the three optional placeholders move into labels | 2 |
| The right keyboard and autofill | `inputMode` on the 22 number fields; `autocomplete` tokens on names, phones and emails; spellcheck off on names and codes | 2 |
| A submit is never disabled | The six disabled submits press into a refusal in `required.ts`'s voice; busy keeps `aria-busy` with the label readable | 2 |
| A refused field scrolls clear of the keyboard, the sticky readout and the dock | `scroll-padding` under the sticky bars and the phone dock | 2 |
| Dates: native, typeable, the end never before the start | Kept; Reply by shows as changed from its default; "Same as the job" for a phase's dates | 2 |
| Each field earns its place | Change the call stops re-asking Project and Role; the phases ask only what differs from the job | 2 |
| Two to five fixed choices are a segmented control | Status, Kind and Leave's Type take the aria-pressed row Faults already draws | 2 |
| Picking a record is the combobox | `RecordPicker` from `ProductPicker`, Offer to… first; client and venue after a month of use | 5 |
| One save model per kind of view, said in words | The form closes and a quiet line says "Saved the job"; the Travel switch leaves its form; the quote keeps its autosave words | 2 |
| The line-item editor | Kept for the quote; Remove only on an open phase row of the new job form | 2 |
| The primary button sits under the last field; the secondary quieter; the destructive never beside it | `.grid-form > .actions`: submit first (filled only when nothing else on the page is the Next), the quiet Cancel after it, a destructive link third | 2, with Move 1's rule in 1 |
| No sticky Save on a phone | Kept as is; the readout and the import bar stay sticky with scroll padding | |
| One form, not a wizard, for the office; one question per page on the link | Kept; the link's forms already are one question with one button | |
| A check-your-answers step only before contacting someone | Noted for the day the messages switch goes on: the Offer press should name who will be texted | later |
| Hints under the label; a placeholder only a real example | Nine instruction placeholders become hints with `aria-describedby`; one example grammar ("e.g. 250"); list searches keep "Find a …" | 2 |
| A phone number is one field, never masked | Kept | |
| Sign-in never needs a transcription | Kept | |
| The same refusal three times is a design fault | Not built; a count of refusals per form is a later measure the history words could carry | later |
| A server-drawn refusal keeps what was typed, prefixes the title, lands on the field | The link's refusal in the bad wash under the field, "Error: " on the title, a redirect to the field; native `required` kept | 1 |
| 44px targets on a phone | The filter chip, the storage banner's button, Remove on phase rows and the quote line's quiet Remove checked at 390px | 1, 2 |

The eighteen variations in the family table above collapse to the one sheet and the word table: the submit's weight and place (variations 1, 2, 9, 14), the refusal's place (4), the mark for optional (5), numbers and units (6, 7, 16), the hint styles (11), the tick controls (12, to the system's keys), the note fields' six names and the job's three labels (3, 15, into Move 4's table so a refusal never reads "Fill in the what's the job? first."), the success line (10), the switch (forms-F4), the removals that ask (13, decided once in act.tsx), the control heights (17), and the staff Leave screen's "Who are you?" (18, read from sign-in as every other screen does).

**What goes back to the shared system.** The system's Field is single-column by anatomy but says nothing about the form around it. The learning is a Form part: one column on a phone with named pairs, a width scale for short answers at a desk, the actions footer (submit first, full width under 640px, the quiet secondary after it, the destructive link third), "(optional)" written in the label text and never in a placeholder, `scroll-padding` under any sticky band, and the autocomplete and spellcheck tokens as part of the Field's contract rather than each app's. The Limited Choice rule also needs a line on when a select is still right (more than five choices, or a hint under each option), and the segmented control should be a documented part.

## 8. One look: the shared design system

### How far sess-sys follows it today

sess-sys takes the system's colours as an override, the tokens beside them (washes, elevation, layers, motion) and the shared icons; its own layout, type, density and components stay its own, following the shared parts' keys, states and wording; brand red is "you" and burnt orange is bad, by Colly's decision. The mapper read `app.css` and every area stylesheet against `principles.md`, `states.md` and `components.md`, counted colour uses, measured type on the bench, and stitched the same kind of thing across areas into composites (the ten `drift-` images in `screens/ux-sweep/map-ds/`).

### Colour: the grammar holds, with five uses outside it

The tokens are vendored (`shared/src/ds/tokens.css`, 0.12.0) and renamed in `app.css:16-39`. **Red** (#ee3744) is "you": the one filled button, the Next dock, the current foot tab, the open tab's underline, links, the focus ring, the open row's bar, the badge and the New dot. **Burnt orange** is stop, short, late and clash: the bad pill, refusals, the fault stripe, overdue tests, late tasks, the danger button. **Amber** is "someone has to act": the pending pill (Sent, To confirm, Check), the inline ask's question box, kit short in the planner, running late, documents running out. **Green** is nearly gone, as the grammar asks: three uses, all "a scan or count that read" (`jobs.css:226`, `stock.css:252,142`); the settled pill is outlined grey, not green (the comment in `StatusPill.tsx:5-6` still says green). Cyan and violet are unused; Claude's suggestions are neutral with the words "Suggested by Claude". Healthy screens are quiet: the Jobs list as the office showed only outlined Confirmed pills and grey stage segments.

Counts of status-colour reads per area stylesheet: Home green 0 / amber 1 / red 1; Jobs green 1 / amber 23 / red 17; Crew 0 / 10 / 5; Stock 2 / 17 / 17.

Outside the grammar:

1. **Invalid is amber in the quote's line editor** (`jobs.css:369-372`) and burnt orange under every other form; `states.md` makes invalid bad, and amber means "nothing is broken yet".
2. **Waiting-to-sync is amber on a product's item numbers** (`stock.css:27`, dashed and warn) where everywhere else it is a dashed grey; `states.md`: never warn, it is routine.
3. **The destructive yes of an inline ask is the red-filled primary** ("Remove it", "Mark it not faulty" are the loudest buttons on the screen, `act.tsx:815`); the system arms in bad and keeps the destructive choice quiet with the safe one focused.
4. **The accent as a card edge for emphasis** (`.crew .share`, `.warehouse .claim`) where accent means you or current.
5. **Home's charts read sess-sys's own greys** (`home.css:37`) rather than the chart roles the vendored tokens carry, which `adoption.md`'s sess-sys row already lists as to do.

And one meaning drawn seven ways: **current or selected** is a red fill on the foot tab, an ink fill on the area row and filter chips, a red underline on a part-tab and the opened stage, an ink underline on On this screen, an ink ring on the stage now, a hover wash on an open task and a picked chart column (`states.md`: selected is accent.soft plus an accent edge).

### Type: five styles, mostly held; one size under the floor

`app.css:57-70` maps the old names onto the system's touch styles: a record's title 24/800, a card's heading 20/700, field and body 16, caption 12, label 12/700; fields hold 16px so iOS never zooms; tracked capitals are the group head on facts, kit groups, call dates, quote groups and the planner's tags. Departures: the five **stage names are 11px on a phone** (`steps.css:78`), the one size under the 12px floor, on every job page; literal sizes outside the styles on the count tally, Money's facts, Home's housekeeping numbers and the labels card; title and heading line heights of 30px fall off the 4px grid; **a section head inside a card is drawn four ways** (tracked capitals; sentence-case bold muted 16px on Home and the quotes to follow; the browser's default 18.72px h3 on Money's owed groups because nothing sets it; plain 16px h3 on Stock); and 800 weight does the ranking for the page title, the stage now, the badge, the count tiles and the planner tag alike, so Home has four things shouting at 800.

### Space: the six roles exist, and the area sheets step round them

`app.css:43-48` names inline 8, stack 4, group 12, section 24, inset 16, gutter 16; the page grid, cards, rows and label gaps use them. But the card-to-card gap is 24 on a list page and **16 inside a record's tab panel**, in Split and Beside columns and the quote draft, so a record page is tighter than the list it came from; **a boxed thing inside a card has three radii (8, 10, 12) and two insets (12, 16)** depending on the area (a phase's box, a call's day box, a fault, a test due, a van, a tile, a board column, a quote line) with no reason recorded; list rows are 12 apart on Jobs, Stock and Crew but 8 on quotes, the item log, follow-ups and pick rows; and `jobs.css`, `stock.css` and `crew.css` almost never read the roles, using literal `--ds-space-N` steps instead. The phone gutter and the one page width hold.

### States: the common ones are drawn; four are never drawn

Rest, hover (rows underline rather than wash: sess-sys's own, consistent), focus (one red ring; the planner's inset ring is the allowed dense-bar case), pressed (the filled primary darkens rather than taking the pressed wash: recorded as sess-sys's own), busy (words only, no spinner after a second), pending (four looks: a dashed grey pill, a dashed edge, a faded block, a dashed amber number), invalid (two colours), expanded (three chevron treatments; counts on some summaries only), empty (mostly words only, usually no action button), skeleton (as the system), offline (neutral), saved and not-saved words (as the system). **Disabled is 50% opacity of the whole control**, so edge and label both fade (`states.md`: disabled ink on a transparent container). **Modified, inactive, stale and unknown are never drawn**: no changed-from-default mark or reset on Admin's forms, no age on the offline copy. **Hover rules outside the hover media query** leave tiles, part-tabs, planner blocks and skill chips lit after a tap on a phone (`home.css:16`, `app.css:335,34x`).

### Parts: thirty-one compared

Where sess-sys follows the shared part's keys and states: the toast (one Toaster, aria-live, Dismiss), the skeleton, the field's 16px and affixes in the quote, the combobox's matching and action rows (its highlight is sess-sys's own, recorded), the inline ask's wording and focus on the safe answer, the chart's keyboard model (sess-sys's Home chart is the system's reference). Where it drifts: the **status pill is the one status part on Jobs, Quotes, Calls and Purchases, but Leave says its state in a facts grid, Timesheets in group headings with a red-filled Check on every row, and Faults and Tests in words and a stripe**; **folds are three mechanisms with three looks** (Fold.tsx: a plain button that becomes a form with a Close link, no chevron, no count; native `<details>` with the browser's triangle; two hand-drawn chevrons); **two row grammars** (Jobs and Catalogue rows are links with a right-aligned side; People rows are a button that opens in place with no end content; quote rows are 8px list items); **the area's views row and the record's tab row are two tab-like rows** drawn differently one above the other; the switch is drawn as a checkbox; no badge part (the Stock row's counts are plain muted text, the Crew tab's is a badge); **no quiet button variant** (the quote draft's rare actions use a fourth look); **the Next dock's rest box is a dashed neutral box**, the same dashed edge that means waiting to sync on a pill, a planner block and a rack panel; the dialog is never used (consistent with the app's no-dialog rule), so what affects several things asks inline; no tooltip on icon buttons.

### Motion, themes, density

Every transition reads a token; the toast, fades and skeleton follow `motion.md`; reduced motion is honoured through the vendored tokens (the two chevron turns and the scan flash keep running, allowed). Both themes hold: light read cleanly in this pass (contrast was not measured here). **Density is touch everywhere** (`index.html:2`): 16px controls and 44px rows fill a 1440px desk, so the office's all-day desk gets a phone's density; under 44px are the list filter chip (36px) and the storage banner's button (36px).

### Departures to keep

Recorded in `learnings.md` or an ADR and not re-proposed: brand red as "you" and burnt orange as bad; the filled primary darkening on press; rows underlining on hover; the combobox's own highlight and upward list near the foot; the focus ring in brand red (3.4:1 at its tightest); the no-dialog rule (inline asks); the phone foot dock as sess-sys's own part.

### Keep, fix, send back

**Keep.** The colour grammar holds on every screen the lenses walked: red as "you", burnt orange as stop, amber as "someone has to act", green all but gone, healthy screens quiet, both themes and reduced motion through the tokens, one focus ring, icons one set, the shell one on all forty-eight screens. The departures recorded in the system's learnings or an ADR stay: the inline ask in place of arming, the filled primary darkening on press, rows underlining on hover, the combobox's own highlight and upward list, the focus ring in brand red, the no-dialog rule, the phone foot dock, the two head styles inside a card (ADR 0082's sentence case beside the 12px capitals), touch density on the desk (adoption.md's sess-sys row), the 4px state stripe as a second channel beside a grouped list's head.

**Fix, by move.**

| Departure found | Fixed in |
|---|---|
| Invalid is amber in the quote's line editor; pending item numbers amber | Move 2 (`--bad`, `--muted`) |
| The destructive yes of an inline ask is the red-filled primary; Check and Checked filled on every row; a list's add filled on five lists and plain on four | Move 1 |
| The accent as a card edge for emphasis | Move 2 |
| Home's charts read local greys | Move 2 (the chart roles) |
| "Current" drawn seven ways: the open task's hover wash and the picked column | Move 2 (the chevron, the ink bar); the bar, the segmented control and the tab keep their own, one drawing per part |
| Stage names at 11px, under the floor | Move 2 (12px, "On the job" wraps) |
| A section head inside a card at five sizes; three radii and two insets for a box; leadings off the 4px grid | Move 2 (`.card h3.group`, `--inset-box`, `--radius-box`, 32/28) |
| The record page's card gap 16 against the list's 24 | Move 11, as Colly decides, one variable |
| Pending drawn four ways; the scan answer's queued state its own look | Move 3 (the dashed "Waiting to sync" pill on the row and the answer) |
| Three folds with three looks; hover rules outside the media query; two targets under 44px | Move 2 |
| A fault or a van away from its group carries its state in colour alone; the stale "green" comment | Move 11 (the pill on an ungrouped row) |
| The status pill's vocabulary assigned screen by screen; "Offered" amber on Calls, grey on the sheet | Move 4 (the words table) |
| No quiet button variant | Move 1 (`button.quiet` after `ds-btn--quiet`) |
| Two row grammars; the area's views row and the record's tab row drawn differently | Left as sess-sys's own; the record heads (Move 11) make the tab row one |
| Modified, inactive, stale and unknown never drawn; disabled fades the whole control | Not taken (ds-f7 dropped by the sceptic): the system's modified mark is the cue Move 5's undisabled Saves use, and the rest has no site that bites |

**Send back to the shared system.** The learnings, in the order the moves meet them; each is a line for `docs/learnings.md` in the system's repository, raised by PR there once Colly picks a batch:

1. A **Form part**: one column on a phone with named pairs, a width scale for short answers at a desk, the actions footer, "(optional)" in the label, `scroll-padding` under sticky bands, autocomplete and spellcheck tokens in the Field's contract (Move 5).
2. The **irreversible yes is never the accent fill**, even inside an inline ask in an app without an Undo toast: the plain button, or danger when the outcome is a loss, with the safe choice focused (Move 1).
3. A **position chip** part, stage word · one-line hint · owner · time, with tone by state and no tone when nothing is short; the status pill is for a decision, the chip for position; and a worked-out state has one line-maker in shared code with fields, never regexes, wherever behaviour hangs on its words (Move 7).
4. A **count is the length of the list it opens**, produced by the list's own function and narrowed as that list is for the viewer; a tile, a badge and a column head never work it out themselves; and when a rule changes what a stage means, every count that stood for that stage is re-read against it (Move 3).
5. A **lens chosen once** (a department, "mine") is the start of every list that can take it, remembered per tab, with one line that names the lens, both its names when a person's lens maps onto two, and undoes it (Moves 3, 9).
6. **Hidden is never missing** for derived words: when a record the words depend on is held back by role, the sentence says "with the office" rather than "not started"; states.md's "Hidden for your role" covers fields, not sentences (Move 3).
7. A **"not yours" sentence** shape for a whole view that is someone else's work, read for information: "<Who> does this. <What you can do here, if anything>.", taken from the one who-table the refusals use; and a Menu line's description is written for the roles that reach the page, not the role that owns the work (Moves 4, 9).
8. A **"you on this" part**: the first card of a record read by someone who is on it says their part first, before the record's own position (Move 9).
9. **News of others' changes on a page with no script**: the old value in brackets beside the fact for a bounded time, and a quiet block at the top when there is more than one, as the no-script form of the toast and of rule 14; and two ended words, cancelled by the client and withdrawn by us (Move 6).
10. A **person reached through two surfaces** (a signed-in app and a link) needs one owner for each thing they do; the system has no rule for an app that reaches the same person twice (Move 6).
11. The **fold-after-three rule covers prose**: a set-once block on a page ordered by what is due folds to its state line and opens itself only when it holds something that needs the person (Move 6).
12. A **record page is one part with one head** (name, a kind-and-facts sub line, state), and the system should say under Containers where a list's add lives, so the tenth record and list look like the first (Moves 1, 11).
13. A **boxed row inside a card** needs its inset and radius named (a seventh space role, or a line under Panel); sess-sys drew one five ways because nothing said; and the Section head's size on a phone should be recorded, 12px tracked capitals over a run of rows, sentence case for a sub-section with its own copy (Move 2).
14. A **shared Home view that is the same for every role** becomes the busiest role's list; a stat tile's order and a disclosure can shape one view by role without a new view; and a stat tile's delta on a page nobody pushes to is "since you last looked on this phone", said as such (Move 10).
15. A **notification list built from "what's yours"** is empty for the person whose job is to watch everything; the delta is the smaller answer and a company scope the larger; and a person's own change made on another surface is their own change, not news (Moves 6, 10, 12).
16. A **role-gated tail** in the task-list shape (the invoice) can close a flow whose stages are everyone's but whose last step is one role's, without adding a stage; and a department is never named as a role once it has become one (Move 12).
17. A **link into a part of a page lands on the part**, with focus on it, as the page's own primary does; a navigation part with a floor (On this screen's three) disappears silently when a page drops under it, so a decision that rests on it needs a test (Move 8).
18. A **list grouped by state** says the state once in the head and not on each row; a row shown away from its group carries the chip; a stripe is a second channel, never the only one (Move 11).
19. The **Limited Choice** rule needs a line on when a select is still right, and the segmented control should be a documented part (Move 5).
20. An app that goes **under the 12px floor** records it or raises it; sess-sys's 11px stage names were an unrecorded departure (Move 2).

## 9. The order to do things

Three programmes were built from the same themes under three weightings, two judges scored them and a critic looked for what all three missed; the working files hold all of it. The scores were close (user-first 25 and 26, smallest-change 26 and 27, risk-first 22 and 21 out of 30) and both judges reached the same shape: the user-first programme's first batch, because it closes every P1 and changes what each of the six personas meets on their next visit; built the smallest-change way, each move one place that every surface reads; with the risk-first programme's rules, fields before words, no batch half a rename, and a named check for every PR. What follows is that order. Colly can stop after any batch with the app consistent, and the questions at the end are the only things that wait on a word.

**Rules for every PR in the programme.** Each PR is a day or less where it can be, with its own changelog line in the office's words; a count that jumps (3 to 12 jobs with kit still out), a tile that moves or a word that changes gets its line in What's changed. A PR that renames a word renames it on both surfaces, app and link, with the history words and the e2e text matches in the same PR; old history rows keep the words they were recorded with. A PR that introduces a part puts it on every surface that part belongs on, so no screen is left half-moved. Nothing stored changes anywhere in the programme, no wire name or key moves, and no PR contacts anyone or writes to a calendar. Each batch is walked on the bench as its personas at 390×844 and 1440×900, in both themes, with the contrast checker run on any new colour use.

### Batch 1 · Monday: stop the costly mistakes, one place each

**Moves:** 1 (red fill), 2 (rhythm sheet), 3 (counts, the lens, the item row), 5a (the link's forms), 6a (staff's leave; what changed on the link), 8a (landing on a part; the Stock strip), 10a (the office's Home order and the quotes tile). About ten or eleven days in eight or nine PRs, each independent, in any order.

**Why first.** Every P1 lands here: the kit-still-out count (sceptic-jobflow-1), the head's lens on Stock (head-1), the link's forms (forms-5), the staff leave hole (staff-1), the changed call time (freelancer-2) and the Money tables on a phone (director-f1). Each PR is one place (a rule, a sheet, a function, a branch) that reverts alone; nothing reopens an ADR; and every persona meets something on their next visit.

**What Colly sees.** Home's 3 becomes the Board's 12, with "ended 4 days ago" on each row of the Warehouse view. A head opens Repairs from the Stock tab and reads LX, with the row agreeing with Home. The warehouse's Stock strip jumps to Pick lists, and a searched item says it is out with Harbour Lights since 14 October. A staff member's link no longer takes days off without approval and points at the app's Leave. A freelancer reads "Call 07:30 (was 09:00)" in 16px fields with one filled yes. "Mark it fixed" and fifty Checks are no longer red, and every list's add is at the top. Group heads are two sizes instead of five. A director's Money tables show all five columns on a phone. A tap from Home lands on the job's crew. The office's Home starts with answers to check.

**Checked by.** The tile-equals-list test on the bench; the contrast checker on `button.quiet` and the link's refusal wash in both themes; the history words test; e2e freelancer.spec (a staff token refused; a changed call time shows "was"; a cancelled job's first line), home.spec ("a tile lands on its part"), stock.spec and faults.spec; the six personas walked.

### Batch 2 · Words and forms, in the app and on the link

**Moves:** 4 (the word table), 5b (the app's forms, with the day of hygiene), 6b (the freelancer's page whole: Work done, the folds, "Since you said yes"). About ten days in three PRs.

**Why second.** Both tables and the sheet reach the link as well as the app, which is where a one-place change in the app alone stops; the word table must precede the chip so that the chip draws one vocabulary; the forms sheet needs batch 1's red rule so a submit's colour is decided; the link's restructure is one `page.ts` PR that builds on batch 1's words. Together they leave every form and every sentence the same on both surfaces.

**What Colly sees.** Staff read "The office books the crew." in one voice on every screen; "Still needs crew" everywhere; "Booked" on both sides of a phone call once answered. Every phone form is one column with named pairs, "(optional)" in the labels, units beside the numbers, and a submit that answers instead of sitting dead. The freelancer's page is two screens shorter, with one "Work done" list that ends at paid and a "Since you said yes" block only when something changed.

**Checked by.** The history words test (new lines only); e2e refusals.spec, layout.spec and screens.spec at 390px; the twelve form patterns walked as the office; the freelancer walked in both colour schemes.

### Batch 3 · Where a job stands, and arriving on it

**Moves:** 7 (fields, then the chip), 8b (the kit link, the dock's quiet slot, the pick list's Next), 9 (the lens on Jobs, "You on this job", the staff cards). About twelve or thirteen days in four PRs: the fields alone first, then the chip on every surface at once, then the arrivals, then the lens.

**Why third.** The fields PR changes no pixel and adds the test that keeps ADR 0109 true; the chip then lands on seven surfaces in one PR so no row keeps the old stage line beside a head with the chip; the pick list's dock reads the chip's words; the Jobs Filter rides on the by-department index the lens needs. Between batch 1 and this batch a head's lens holds on four of five tabs, and the doc says so.

**What Colly sees.** One job reads the same on Home, the list, the Board and its head, with "in 6 days" or "2 days after" on every chip, amber when crew is short, and "Confirmed" no longer outranking "Back". The Board's Planning column is This week, Next week and a folded Later. The pick list says "Pick the kit · 6 of 122 out" and, after the last scan, what comes next. A staff member's job opens on her call with "Mine (2)" on Jobs; a head's Jobs list opens on LX.

**Checked by.** The fields-versus-hint test on the eleven bench jobs; e2e job-moves-on.spec, steps.spec, status.spec, pick.spec, scan.spec, departments.spec and access.spec; the chip's tones through the contrast checker in both themes; the eleven bench jobs walked; a Playwright pass of a pick at each stage at 390px so the scan box and the dock never fight.

### Batch 4 · The pages that are someone's own

**Moves:** 10b (Home by role: the verdict line, Money second, Housekeeping folded, the delta, Just finished), 11 (one head and one row for every record). About eight days in three PRs, one per area for the record heads.

**Why fourth.** Both build on batch 1's counts and batch 3's chip (the pill-or-chip rule in the head slot) and are page-local, so nothing is half-moved; and the record heads help the builder as much as the person, which is why they are not earlier.

**What Colly sees.** A director's Home opens with one sentence about the week and the money, Money on the second screen, "2 new since Tuesday" on the tiles, Housekeeping folded; a job in Back stays on Home under Just finished. A venue, a client, an item or a van opens like a job, with its name in the head and tabs for its parts.

**Checked by.** e2e home.spec and counts.spec; the director and office personas reading Home top to bottom in one screen; screens.spec with each record kind at phone and desk.

### Batch 5 · What the small moves prepared, after Colly's answers

**Moves:** 12 (an owner by role, the two notices, the invoice tail), 5c (the record picker's half step), and whichever of the answered questions below say yes (in-app answering for staff as its own ADR, Stock's day's cards above the catalogue, a named "At risk" state). About seven to nine days once answered.

**Why last.** These add a thing rather than share one, and each is cheaper for what came before: the owner table is the one Move 4 made real, the notices read history rows that exist, the picker is `ProductPicker` generalised on forms Move 5 already reshaped. The timesheet-switch fortnight (ADR 0090) is its own small phase outside this programme: office-5's "Approve €290" on a matching row and freelancer-6's "sent N weeks ago".

Total: about forty-one days in batches 1 to 4 and about forty-eight with batch 5, as one person's weeks.

### Where the panel disagreed, and what was settled

- **The inline ask's yes.** Plain, with the danger look only where the outcome is a loss (user-first and smallest-change); the risk-first programme kept danger for the two arming cases alone. Loss Aversion wants a loss to look like one, and the amber question still names it.
- **The Confirmed task's name.** Keep it (ADR 0103). Once a crew booking is "Booked" on both sides, "Confirmed" has one meaning, the client's yes as status and step; the rename to "Client said yes" is not needed.
- **The kit shortfall's words and "Filled by someone else".** Kept as held up; the risk-first renames ("n missing from the kit", "Gone to someone else") would make a new collision with the Missing fault and undo audit finding 61.
- **A head's two department names.** Said on the narrowing line, "Showing LX (Lighting) only. Show all departments", not by renaming the catalogue's chip: the crew and stock department lists stay apart (ADR 0025, 0046).
- **The Board's Back column.** A job past its end stays in Back for the fortnight or while a fault is open (ADR 0109); the risk-first "drop into Done" is not taken, and the column is Through, not Done.
- **The link's refusal.** The bad wash, since invalid is bad; the native `required` check stays because it works with no script and no signal, and the server's refusal lands on the field.
- **The chip's tone on a Planning job with nothing short.** None: healthy is quiet. The risk-first programme had it right against the other two.
