But it is decided: ADR 0088 makes Quotes a sixth sidebar place on a desk with no Up and puts it on the Menu under Jobs on a phone, and 1.88.0 added the phone's '‹ Menu' as the audit asked. The cost in use is one tap on the Jobs tab to reach List, Board or Money, which does not outweigh the ADR; giving a sidebar root another area's row would make Quotes the one root page with a row and contradict 0088.)

### The forms

*Every form against the research and the shared system.*

Field by field, sess-sys's forms already carry most of the research: labels sit above and stay, every app field is 16px and 44px on a phone, dates are native with the end held after the start, required.ts refuses an empty field in the app's words with focus and a lasting line, Confirm names the outcome and focuses the safe answer, the quote draft saves itself and says so. What is missing is the form around the field. Every `.grid-form` is two columns at 390px, so First name/Last name, Mobile/Email, Client/Venue, Serial/Where, Phone/Email and From/To all sit side by side at 156–173px on a phone. Optional is said three ways (nothing, a placeholder, '(optional)' in a label) and required is never shown until the press. The company's own records are picked four ways (datalist, long select, the combobox, free text that re-asks what the job knows). The primary button is full width on one form, a 89–113px button beside Cancel on the next, and un-primary `.wide` on a third, and a dozen submits are disabled until valid. The freelancer's server-drawn forms are a second family: 14.4px fields on a phone, 48px `.yes`/`.no` buttons, a bold `.field-error` line, '(€)' in the label. None of these is a one-screen slip; each shows on four or more forms, and all six are fixable with rules the research already states and parts the design system already has.

#### forms-5 · The freelancer's forms are a second family: smaller text, other buttons, another error line and another way to say optional

**P1 · severity 3 · Kept by the sceptic** · roles F, H, S · 390×844 dark (prefers-color-scheme) · reproduced

A freelancer meets sess-sys only through /f/<token>, drawn by the server, and its forms differ from the app's in every part the design system fixes: field text is 14.4px on a phone where the app's is 16px (iOS zooms the page on focus under 16px, which principles.md's Type rule exists to prevent), buttons are 48px `.yes`/`.no`/plain where the app's are 44px `.primary`/quiet, a refusal is a bold 0.92rem `.field-error` line where the app's is the `.alert` wash, the unit is '(€)' in the label where the app writes '€' after the words, and optional is '(optional)' in the label where the app puts it in a placeholder. The same person may also be a head who uses the app, and the office answers questions about both. One product should refuse, label and press the same way on both.

- **Evidence:** /f/<token> (busiest freelancer): 'Day rate you'd do it for (€)' 325×44 at 14.4px; 'Note for the office' textarea 14.4px; 'Accept' 325×48 `.yes`; 'Decline Clare Hotels Fundraiser' 273×48 `.no`; 'Send rate' 104×48 plain beside Accept (`server/src/crew/page.ts:224-233; CSS at page.ts:793`) [getComputedStyle fontSize 14.4px on every field of 21 forms on the page; app fields 16px on all 14 forms walked] · /f/<token> › days off; › documents; › details: 'Note (optional)' in the label; 'From' required with no mark; 'The file' required; 'Save my number' plain button; fields 14.4–14.72px (`server/src/crew/page.ts:664-669, 384-388, 480-483`) · /f/<token> › a refused field: `.field-error{color:var(--bad);font-weight:600;font-size:.92rem}` and a 2px red border, against the app's `.alert` (bad-soft wash, radius, text-small) (`server/src/crew/page.ts:34,793 vs web/src/app.css:399-401`) · the app's Ask for crew and Add person: 'Day rate €' and 'Usual day rate €' with the unit after the words, no brackets (`web/src/crew/Calls.tsx:734; web/src/crew/People.tsx:345`)
- **Basis:** principles.md Type (field text never under 16px on a phone) and rule 18 (an error says where, what, what next, in one look); nngroup.md heuristic 4 (consistency); components.md Field and Button; WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8 · GOV.UK Design System, Error message and Error summary ('Error: ' title prefix); principles.md Type and density; NN/g 'Error-Message Guidelines' (2023)
- **Proposal (S):** Give the server-drawn pages the same field, button, hint and refusal styles as the app, from the vendored tokens, so the two families become one. page.ts CSS: `input, select, textarea { font-size: max(16px, 1rem) }` and 44px min-height; buttons 44px with `.yes` as the app's `.primary` look and `.no` as its quiet destructive look; `.field-error` drawn as the app's `.alert` wash (bad-soft, radius-control, text-small, role=alert) after the field; the rate label 'Day rate you'd do it for, €' or a drawn € before the box with the unit in the accessible name; `.add-away` one column on a phone with From/To as a pair; 'Error: ' prefixed to the `<title>` of a redrawn refused page.
- **Trade-off:** Each offer card grows about 8px; the page's hand-written CSS stays small by copying a dozen token values rather than importing app.css. **Depends on:** Nothing; the token values are in shared/src/ds. **Weighed:** Rendering the link from the app's React components: rejected, the page must work without script and on patchy signal.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/flink-home-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-forms/freelancer-offer-form-phone-dark.png`
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced on the busiest freelancer's link at 390px dark: 31 of 33 fields at 14.4px (one 13.6px, one 14.72px; `label{font-size:.9rem}` with `input{font:inherit}` at page.ts:789), 26 buttons at 48px against the app's 44px, `.field-error` bold 0.92rem (page.ts:793) against the app's `.alert` wash, '(€)' in the rate label, and the days-off form two columns at 161px. The viewport meta has no maximum-scale (page.ts:616), so an iPhone zooms the page on focus of every one of these fields: that is the case principles.md's rule ('Nothing a person types is under 16px on a phone') exists for, and it is a stated system rule, not taste. 1.79.2 fixed the link page's target sizes, not its type; 1.84.1 touched its words. ADR 0002/0033 decide the page is server-drawn without script, and the proposal keeps that. S is honest: a dozen CSS values in page.ts. (was: "The freelancer's forms are a second family: smaller text, other buttons, another error line and another way to say optional")

#### forms-1 · Every form is two columns on a phone, so half-width fields hold names, phones, emails and places

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles O, H, W, S, D · 390×844 dark, 1440×900 light · reproduced

`.app .grid-form` is `grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr` with no phone override, and a field is full width only when it opts into `.wide`. On a 390px phone that puts First name beside Last name, Mobile beside Email, Client beside Venue, Serial beside Where it's kept, Phone beside Email and Seats beside Payload, each 156–173px wide with its placeholder cut ('+353 87…'). Baymard's rounds found multi-column forms misread and skipped more than one-column ones even with separators, and heads and warehouse fill these on a phone with gloves on. The eye has to zigzag, the thumb lands on the wrong box, and a field no wider than its label looks like a short answer is wanted.

- **Evidence:** #crew/people › Add person: First name / Last name, Mobile / Email, Usual day rate € / Type side by side; Mobile's placeholder '+353 87…' is truncated (`web/src/app.css:328; web/src/crew/People.tsx:340-437`) [grid-template-columns 156px 156px at 390×844 (getComputedStyle); field 156×44] · #jobs › New job: Client and Venue side by side, each with 'Pick or add one' inside, their NewOrNear rows between them (`web/src/jobs/JobsScreen.tsx:515-526`) [173px 173px; Client 173×44] · #stock/product/v1-product-00001/items › Add item: Serial beside Where it's kept, both 156px with long placeholders ('The maker's, if any', 'A place, or a case's number') (`web/src/stock/common.tsx`) [156px 156px] · #admin › Office: Phone beside Email, VAT number half width (`web/src/OfficeCard.tsx:63-77`) [158px 158px (.office-form)] · #stock/vehicles › Add vehicle; #crew/leave › Apply; #jobs/<id> › Change details: Name/Registration, Kind/Seats; From/To; Client/Venue all paired at 156px (`web/src/stock/Vehicles.tsx:209-232; web/src/crew/Leave.tsx:409-413`) [156px 156px on each] · same forms at 1440×900: The same two columns at 322–563px each, so a Seats box is 361px wide and a VAT number 563px (`web/src/app.css:328`) [cols 561px 561px on Change details; 563px 563px on the admin cards]
- **Basis:** Baymard 2023 (multi-column forms misread and skipped); NN/g Top 10 form guidelines 2016 (one column; match field width to the input); GOV.UK text input widths; principles.md density (44px, phone first); nngroup.md heuristic 8 · Baymard Institute, 'Avoid Multi-Column Layouts' (2023); NN/g 'Website Forms Usability: Top 10 Recommendations' (2016); GOV.UK Design System, Text input (width classes); Luke Wroblewski, Web Form Design (label and field alignment)
- **Proposal (S):** One column on a phone with named pairs, and a cap on the width of a short answer at a desk, rather than one column everywhere. app.css: `@media (max-width: 639px) { .app .grid-form, .account .office-form { grid-template-columns: 1fr } }` with a `.pair` wrapper (From/To, First name/Last name, a certificate and its Expires) that stays `1fr 1fr` at any width; page.ts:813 the same for `.add-away`. At a desk keep two columns but give a short answer its width: `max-width: 8em` on Seats, Payload, Usual day rate, Day rate, VAT number, Registration, Travel's numbers (the Travel card's `.with-unit input { max-width: 7em }` at jobs.css:533 is already this rule, used once). Placeholders no longer truncate ('+353 87…' on Mobile).
- **Trade-off:** Phone forms get longer by about a third in field rows; a desk form keeps the office's two-column density. Pairs keep the from/to reading. **Depends on:** Nothing. **Weighed:** The reviewer's one column everywhere with a four-step width scale (M): more coherent on paper, but it changes every desk form the office fills all day for a harm that is on the phone, and the design system has no width scale to borrow yet. Preferred the phone rule now and the scale as a learning for the system.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/crew-add-person-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/new-job-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/product-add-item-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/admin-cards-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/vehicles-add-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/new-job-desk.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-forms/add-person-form-phone-dark.png`
- **Learning:** The system's Field is single-column by anatomy but says nothing about the form around it: add a 'Form' part with one column, pairs, and a width scale.
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced exactly: app.css:328 is `grid-template-columns: 1fr 1fr` with no phone rule; measured 173px/173px on New job, 156px/156px on Add person, Add vehicle, Add phase and Change details, 158px on the Admin cards at 390px, and 322px/563px columns at 1440px; the freelancer's days-off form is the same at 161px (page.ts:813). Not in the 5 November audit, no ADR, and the design system's Field says nothing about the form around it. But the rating is high: every field is already 44px high and 16px, 156px holds a phone number or a serial, and the people who fill these on a phone with gloves (W, H) meet them a few times a week, not on every load-out. The proposal is sound but bigger than the incoherence: the harm is on the phone and on the oversize desk boxes, so the smallest change is one column under 640px with explicit pairs (From/To, First/Last, Held/Expires), and a width cap on the few number and code fields at a desk; the full width scale can follow. (was: "Every form is two columns on a phone, so half-width fields hold names, phones, emails and places")

#### forms-2 · Required is never shown and optional is said three different ways, so the only way to learn what a form needs is to press and be refused

**P2 · severity 2 · Kept by the sceptic** · roles O, H, W, S, F · 390×844 dark, 1440×900 light · reproduced

No app form marks a required field; the person finds out on the press when required.ts says 'Fill in the job first.' Optional is said in a placeholder on three forms ('Optional', 'What it would cost to replace; optional', 'Reason (optional)'), in the label on two ('Note for them (optional)' on Timesheets, 'Note (optional)' on the freelancer's days off), and not at all on the rest, where Client, Venue, Email, Mobile, Serial and Notes are silently optional. Baymard found a third of testers hit a validation error when only optional was marked, and a placeholder vanishes on the first keystroke and is skipped by some screen readers. For the office this is a refused press on a form they fill daily; for a head on a phone it is a scroll back up to find what the line under the button is about.

- **Evidence:** #stock › Add product: 'Value of one, in euro' placeholder 'What it would cost to replace; optional'; 'Stock code' placeholder 'Optional'; Name required with no mark (`web/src/stock/StockScreen.tsx:575; web/src/stock/common.tsx (Stock code)`) [3 placeholders carrying 'optional' across the app (grep); 0 required marks on 14 forms walked] · #crew/leave › decline: Reason field has no visible label; its placeholder reads 'Reason (optional)' and the aria-label 'Reason' (`web/src/crew/Leave.tsx:621`) · #crew › Timesheets; /f/<token> › days off: '(optional)' in the label text, the GOV.UK way, on two forms only (`web/src/crew/Timesheets.tsx:473; server/src/crew/page.ts:668`) · #jobs › New job, pressed empty: Job field gets the red edge and the line 'Fill in the job first.' under it; nothing before the press said Job was the one field needed (`web/src/required.ts:30-37`) [1 press, focus lands on Job] · #stock/vehicles › Add vehicle, pressed empty: 'Fill in the name first.' under Name; Registration, Kind, Seats, Payload and five dates were all optional with no sign (`web/src/stock/Vehicles.tsx:210`)
- **Basis:** Baymard 2018 (mark optional and required; 32% error when only optional marked); NN/g 'Marking Required Fields' 2019; GOV.UK question pages ('(optional)' in the label, never a placeholder); NN/g placeholders 2014; nngroup.md heuristic 6; principles rule 19 · Baymard, 'Mark Required Fields and Optional Fields' (2018); NN/g, 'Marking Required Fields in Forms' (2019); GOV.UK Design System, Question pages; NN/g, 'Placeholders in Form Fields Are Harmful' (2014)
- **Proposal (S):** One rule: required is the default and unmarked; '(optional)' is appended to the label of anything that may be left empty, never in a placeholder. As the reviewer's sketch, without the 'mark required instead' clause: a `<L optional>` helper or a label convention rendering 'Mobile (optional)' in the label's muted colour; the three 'optional' placeholders move into labels; the Leave reason field gets a visible label; Add vehicle keeps Name first and puts its dates under a More fold as Add person does.
- **Trade-off:** Longer labels on forms where most fields are optional; nothing else changes in behaviour. **Depends on:** Nothing. **Weighed:** Asterisks on required (NN/g 2019): rejected as the reviewer says, since most sess-sys fields are required by use not schema and it needs a legend.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/stock-add-product-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/new-job-refused-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/vehicles-add-refused-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-forms/add-vehicle-form-phone-dark.png`
- **Learning:** The system's Field says what a placeholder is not, but not how optional is marked: add '(optional) in the label text' as the rule.
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced: no required mark on any of the walked forms (New job, Add person, Add vehicle, Add phase, Change details, the Admin cards), the refusal arriving only on the press ('Fill in the job first.', 'Fill in the name first.'); optional said as a placeholder ('Reason (optional)' Leave.tsx:621; 'Optional…' on RackSlots.tsx:136, Racks.tsx:546; 'What it would cost to replace; optional' StockScreen.tsx:575), as label text ('(optional)' Timesheets.tsx:473, page.ts:668) and nowhere else. The audit's finding 85 fixed the refusal's words, not the marking, so this is new; no ADR; the system's Field already rules a placeholder is only an example, which makes the 'Optional' placeholders a departure on their own. The proposal is GOV.UK's rule, is S, and breaks nothing. One trim: drop the second clause (mark required instead where most fields are optional), which makes two conventions out of one; on Add vehicle and Add person's More the labels can carry '(optional)' or the optional fields can sit under a More fold, which Add person already has. (was: "Required is never shown and optional is said three different ways, so the only way to learn what a form needs is to press and be refused")

#### forms-3 · The company's own records are picked four ways, and one form re-asks what the job already knows

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles O, H, D · 390×844 dark, 1440×900 light · reproduced

A product is picked with the combobox the design system took from sess-sys, but a client or venue is a `<datalist>` under a text box that says 'Pick or add one' (New job, Change details, New quote, Past quotes), a person to offer to is a native `<select>` of about sixty names under a separate search box, and a phase name, category, department, supplier and place are datalists. GOV.UK dropped `<select>` for long lists after users could not close it and typed into it; a datalist on a phone shows no list until something is typed and says nothing aloud. The office does this on every job; a head on a phone offering a call scrolls a sixty-name select. 1.82's 'Did you mean' closed the typo cost on clients and venues; what is left is three ways of picking one record where the system has one part.

- **Evidence:** #jobs › New job: Client and Venue are text boxes with `list=` and the instruction 'Pick or add one' as placeholder; 30 clients and 60 venues behind them (`web/src/jobs/JobsScreen.tsx:521-524; web/src/jobs/common.tsx:364 (Choices)`) [datalist, 30 and 60 options (volume-ids.json)] · #jobs/<id>/crew › a call › Offer…: 'Offer to…' is a native select grouped by optgroup, with a search box above it whose Enter is swallowed; submit disabled until one is chosen (`web/src/crew/Calls.tsx:604-623`) [select of ~60 people; button disabled] · #jobs/<id>/crew › Ask for crew: Project (required) and Venue typed as free text on the job's own page; Phase a free text box with placeholder 'Build, Show…' though the job's phases exist (`web/src/crew/Calls.tsx:712-736`) · #stock › Add product; #stock › Add item; #crew/people › Add person › More: Category, Where it's kept and Department are datalists with instructions as placeholders ('A place, or a case's number', 'Audio, LX…') (`web/src/stock/StockScreen.tsx:560,583; web/src/stock/common.tsx:339,357; web/src/crew/People.tsx:371-377`) · #stock › Add product › product picker; #crew/people › Skills: The combobox: typed, matched on any word, eight then a count, said aloud: the part the rest should use (`web/src/ProductPicker.tsx; web/src/crew/SkillsField.tsx`) · grep: 17 `<datalist>`s and 62 `<select>`s in web/src against 2 comboboxes [grep -c across web/src]
- **Basis:** components.md Combobox 'Picking one record'; GOV.UK Select ('a last resort'); alphagov accessible-autocomplete; NN/g 'Drop-Down Menus: Use Sparingly' 2017; WCAG 2.2 SC 3.3.7 Redundant Entry; nngroup.md heuristics 4 and 6 · GOV.UK Design System, Select ('last resort') and Autocomplete research; alphagov/accessible-autocomplete; NN/g 'Drop-Down Menus: Use Sparingly' (2017); NN/g 'Listboxes vs Dropdown Lists' (2020); WCAG 2.2 SC 3.3.7
- **Proposal (L):** One record picker: the existing combobox (ProductPicker's part) for every pick from the company's own records. Generalise ProductPicker into `RecordPicker` with the system's combobox keys: Client and Venue on New job, Change details, New quote and Past quotes (its action row 'Add "Nissan" as a new client' replaces NewOrNear and 'Did you mean'), Offer to… on a call (the search box and the select become one combobox, department as the meta line, applicants stay a checkbox under it), Where it's kept on Add item and Move some, Supplier on Subhire. Department, Category and Phase name keep a datalist (a free value with a few hints, which is the system's rule for a datalist). 'Pick or add one' placeholders go.
- **Trade-off:** The combobox is heavier than a datalist; a client list of 30 mostly works at a desk. The gain is one way to pick on every screen and a phone picker that works with gloves. **Depends on:** The combobox part is already in sess-sys. **Weighed:** Fix only Offer to… (M): removes the worst case (a 65-option select) and leaves datalists where 'Did you mean' already covers them. Worth offering Colly as the half step.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/new-job-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-forms/offer-form-crew-phone-dark.png`
- **Sceptic:** The pattern is real and reproduced: Client and Venue are datalists under 'Pick or add one' on New job, Change details, New quote and Past quotes; Offer to… on #crew is a native select of 65 options at 252px wide with its submit disabled until one is chosen (Calls.tsx:604-623); the product picker and skills field are the system's combobox; 17 datalists and 62 selects against 2 comboboxes (grep). But one piece of evidence is wrong and the WCAG claim rests on it: the Ask for crew form with a free-text Project (Calls.tsx:712) is NewCall on #crew, where there is no job to know, not the job page; the job page's Ask for crew (JobScreen.tsx:880-940) asks only phases, role, rate and details. So no redundant entry, and the 3.3.7 citation goes. Also partly decided: the 5 November audit's client-typo finding asked for the combobox and was closed in 1.82 with 'Did you mean' on the datalist, so the data-quality cost of the datalist is handled and the remaining cost is one way to pick versus three. That lowers it to P2. The proposal's RecordPicker is the right part and L is honest; the Ask for crew part of the sketch is dropped. (was: "The company's own records are picked four ways, and one form re-asks what the job already knows")

#### forms-4 · A form's buttons change shape, place and life from one form to the next, and a dozen submits are dead until the form is valid

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles O, H, W, S, D · 390×844 dark, 1440×900 light · reproduced

On New job, Add product, Ask for crew and Apply for leave the submit is `.primary.wide`, full width under the last field. On Change details, Add vehicle and every Admin card it is a natural-width `.primary` (89–233px) at the left, with Cancel after it on two of them and a Pending line on the others. On Add phase, Save count, Log day in lieu and the first Add person it is `.wide` with no `.primary` at all. On a dozen forms the submit is `disabled` until the form is valid (That's me, Offer, Save allowance ×12 rows, Subhire, Sign in, Calendar, Tidy, Past quotes, Import, Quote email, Give a department, Home's who-form), which Adam Silver and GOV.UK reject because a dead button explains nothing and sits at low contrast. NN/g says the order matters less than keeping it the same; here a person learns three shapes for 'do it' and a fourth that will not press.

- **Evidence:** #jobs › New job vs #jobs/<id> › Change details: 'Add job' 358×44 full width vs 'Save job' 89×44 with 'Cancel' 76×44 beside it (`web/src/jobs/JobsScreen.tsx:572 vs web/src/jobs/JobScreen.tsx:468-473`) [bounding boxes at 390px: 358px vs 89px] · #jobs/<id> › Add phase; #stock/product/<id>/items › Save count; #crew/leave › Log day in lieu; #crew/people › Add person: Submit is `.wide` without `.primary` (Add person: `initial ? 'primary' : 'wide'`) (`web/src/jobs/JobScreen.tsx:803; web/src/stock/common.tsx:466; web/src/crew/Leave.tsx:463; web/src/crew/People.tsx:437`) · #crew/leave › allowances; #crew/leave › who-form; a call › Offer: 'Save allowance' DISABLED on 12 rows until touched; 'That's me' DISABLED until a name is chosen; 'Offer' DISABLED until a person is chosen (`web/src/crew/Leave.tsx:704,184; web/src/crew/Calls.tsx:622; web/src/SignIn.tsx:104; web/src/CalendarCard.tsx:369`) [grep: 13 submit buttons with `disabled={` across web/src] · #admin › Travel: 'Read the AA's price' is a 158×58 secondary beside the fuel field, taller than every other button, and a role=switch sits inside the same form as 'Save travel and subsistence' (`web/src/TravelCard.tsx:118-131,124,150`)
- **Basis:** Adam Silver, 'Where to put buttons on forms' (2019) and 'Don't disable submit buttons' (2023); GOV.UK Button (one primary, left-aligned, full width tested on mobile, never disabled); NN/g 'OK-Cancel or Cancel-OK' (2008); components.md Button (one primary per region; busy is aria-disabled with the label readable); nngroup.md heuristic 4 · Adam Silver (2019, 2023); GOV.UK Design System, Button; Smashing Magazine, 'Frustrating Design Patterns: Disabled Buttons' (2021); NN/g 'OK-Cancel or Cancel-OK' (2008); Steven Hoober, touch target research as cited by Smashing 2023
- **Proposal (M):** One footer for every form: the submit first, full width under 640px and natural width above, the quiet Cancel after it; the red fill only where the form's submit is the page's one next thing; no submit disabled for an incomplete form. app.css: `.grid-form > .actions` as the one footer part (submit then secondary, `flex-wrap`, full-width children under 640px). Wrap Save job/Cancel, Save phase/Cancel, Add vehicle and the Admin cards' Save/Pending in it; Pending goes under the actions. Submits on a page with a Next dock (Change details, Add phase, Save phase) are plain, not `.primary`; Add job, Add person's first form, Add product and the Admin cards keep `.primary` as the page's one next thing. Remove `disabled` from That's me, Offer, Save allowance, Subhire, Give a department and Tidy: each has `refuse()` from useAct, so the press answers 'Choose who you are first.' / 'Choose someone to offer it to first.' / 'Change a number first.' in required.ts's wording; buttons that are busy keep `aria-busy` with the label readable. 'Read the AA's price' becomes a quiet action at the fuel field's end.
- **Trade-off:** About ten new refusal strings; the allowance rows lose the dead button as a 'nothing changed' cue, which the system's `data-modified` mark gives back. **Depends on:** required.ts's `sayWhatsNeeded` exported for selects and custom cases; forms-F4 for the Travel switch. **Weighed:** The reviewer's 'every submit is .primary': rejected because it puts two red primaries on a job page with the Next dock in view, against ADR 0104 and the system's one primary per region.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/job-change-details-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/job-add-phase-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/leave-after-me-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/admin-cards-phone.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-forms/submit-buttons-three-forms-phone-dark.png`
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced on a phone: Add job 358×44 `.primary.wide`; Save job 89×44 `.primary` with Cancel 76×44 after it; Add phase 324×44 `.wide` with no `.primary`; Add vehicle 113×44 `.primary`; the Admin cards' Saves 162–233×44 natural width with 'Read the AA's price' 158×58; That's me DISABLED, Offer DISABLED until a person is chosen, Save allowance `disabled={!touched}` (Leave.tsx:704). The audit's 'only three disabled controls' was about role, not validity, so this is new. But the proposal's first clause, that every form's submit is `.primary`, runs into a decision: ADR 0104's one Next button and the audit's finding 92 rule that the red fill marks the thing to do next, and the system's one primary per region. On a job page the sticky dock already holds the red primary (my composite's middle panel shows 'Settle the kit and vans' in the dock over the Change details form), so Add phase and Add person's `.wide` are the rule, not the slip; Save job being `.primary` under that dock is the slip. Keep the footer shape, the order, the full width under 640px and the end of disabled-until-valid; drop 'always .primary'. (was: "The primary button changes shape, place and life from one form to the next")

#### forms-6 · Numbers and units are asked seven ways

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles O, H, W, D, F · 390×844 dark, 1440×900 light · reproduced

A count is `type=number` on 22 inputs (Ask for crew, Leave, Report damage, Save count, Kit, Labels, Trackers…), eleven of them with no inputMode, and `inputMode` numeric or decimal on the rest; GOV.UK avoids `type=number` because scrolling changes the value and letters are silently dropped. The unit is written into the label seven ways ('Payload (kg)', 'Weight, in kg', 'Day rate €', 'Usual day rate €', 'Fuel price, € a litre', 'Value of one, in euro', '(€)'), while the Travel card's km and the quote line's € draw it beside the box, which is the practice the design system's Field already credits to sess-sys.

- **Evidence:** #jobs/<id>/crew › Ask for crew; #crew/leave; #stock › Report damage; items › Save count: `type="number"` inputs (`web/src/crew/Calls.tsx:728; web/src/crew/Leave.tsx:457,697,701; web/src/stock/Faults.tsx:108; web/src/stock/common.tsx:453`) [grep: 6 `type="number"` inputs in web/src] · #stock/vehicles › Add vehicle; #stock › Add product; #crew/people › Add person; #admin › Travel; /f/<token>: Unit wording: 'Payload (kg)', 'Weight, in kg', 'Power at most, in watts', 'Usual day rate €', 'Fuel price, € a litre', 'Per diem, € a day', 'Value of one, in euro', '(€)' (`web/src/stock/Vehicles.tsx:232; web/src/stock/common.tsx (SizeFields); web/src/crew/People.tsx:345; web/src/TravelCard.tsx:107,139,145; web/src/stock/StockScreen.tsx:569; server/src/crew/page.ts:231`) [7 spellings across 6 forms] · #jobs/<id>/quote › a line: The one place the unit is drawn in the box ('a day' as the charged select, € before the price), per ADR 0100 (`web/src/jobs/QuoteLine.tsx:384-392`)
- **Basis:** components.md Field (units as an affix, in the accessible name; inputmode numeric/decimal); GOV.UK text input (avoid type=number); Baymard 2015 touch keyboards; nngroup.md heuristic 4 · GOV.UK Design System, Text input ('Avoid using inputs with a type of number'); Baymard, 'Mobile form usability: the numeric keyboard' (2015); design-system components.md Field
- **Proposal (S):** One number field: `inputMode` numeric or decimal, the unit drawn beside the box with the Travel card's `.with-unit` part and carried in the accessible name. Lift `.with-unit` out of `.office-form` into app.css and use it on every number with a unit (€ before; kg, m, W, km, days, % after), labels then naming the thing ('Payload', 'Day rate'); replace `type="number"` with `inputMode` on the 22 inputs, keeping min/max as a check on submit.
- **Trade-off:** About 20 fields change markup; the `.with-unit` box fixes the input's width at 7em, which is right for every one of them. **Depends on:** Nothing. **Weighed:** Standardise the label wording only: leaves the unit in grey label text where the system puts it beside the value.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/vehicles-add-phone.png`
- **Sceptic:** The pattern holds and is larger than stated: `type="number"` is on 22 inputs in web/src, not 6 (Kit.tsx, PickScreen.tsx, Labels.tsx, CountKit.tsx, Trackers.tsx, CableLabels.tsx as well as the reviewer's), 11 of them without inputMode, and units are written into labels seven ways as listed ('Payload (kg)' Vehicles.tsx, 'Fuel price, € a litre' and 'Per diem, € a day' TravelCard.tsx, 'Usual day rate €' People.tsx, 'Day rate €' Calls.tsx, 'Value of one, in euro' StockScreen.tsx, '(€)' page.ts:231). What sharpens it: the design system's Field already records sess-sys as the app that 'draws € before the number and %, km or days after, never typed', and the Travel card's `.with-unit` (jobs.css:532) and the quote line do that, so the other twenty fields have drifted from a practice the system credits to this app. 1.79.0 fixed the quote line's day/week unit, not these. Rating as the reviewer's; size S is honest only with the Travel card's `.with-unit` as the part, which already exists, so no dependency on forms-1. (was: "Numbers and units are asked seven ways")

**Smaller findings**

- **forms-F1 · Placeholders carry instructions on at least nine fields** (P2 · severity 2 · Kept by the sceptic). 'Pick or add one', 'From its label; empty for the next free one', 'A place, or a case's number', 'With the driver's', 'Who tested it', 'Anything whoever approves should know', 'Notes for the office', 'The name they go by', 'If they trade through a company' are instructions that vanish on the first keystroke and are grey. The system allows a placeholder only as a format example. *Direction:* Move each to a `<small>` hint under the label (the admin cards already do this with aria-describedby) and leave only typeable examples as placeholders. (S). *Sceptic:* Reproduced in the walk: 'Pick or add one' (Client, Venue), 'With the driver's' (Seats), 'The name they go by', 'If they trade through a company', 'Notes for the office', 'Build, Show…' are placeholders on the forms I opened, and the grep adds 'From its label; empty for the next free one', 'Who they're hired from', 'A Google Maps link, or…'. The system's Field rule is explicit (a placeholder is only an example of the format), the audit did not touch it, and the direction (a `<small>` hint with aria-describedby, as the Admin cards do) is the system's own part. S. Screenshots: `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/product-add-item-phone.png`
- **forms-F3 · Add phase with no name does nothing at all (one form; the others are caught by required.ts)** (P3 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic). Pressing 'Add phase' with the name empty returns silently: no refusal line, no focus, nothing added. The field is not `required`, so required.ts does not catch it, and the handler returns before run(). Rule 10 says every press answers. *Direction:* Mark the New phase input `required` so required.ts says 'Fill in the new phase first.' (S). *Sceptic:* Reproduced on v1-job-00003: pressing Add phase with New phase empty gave no refusal line, no alert, focus left on the button and nothing added (JobScreen.tsx:773 returns before run(); the input at :789 has no `required`). But the claimed pattern is not there: every other `if (!x.trim()) return` guard (JobsScreen:493, ClientScreen:100, VenueScreen:84, Money:305, PastQuotes:389, Calls:680, JobScreen:414 and :635) sits behind an input that is `required`, so required.ts catches those first; Add item's count and Places have `required` too. It is one form, so a finding at P3: add `required` to the New phase input and it joins the rest. Screenshots: `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/job-add-phase-refused-phone.png`
- **forms-F4 · A switch sits inside a form that also has Save** (P3 · severity 2 · Kept by the sceptic). Travel's 'Update the fuel price from the AA each month' is a role=switch that acts at once, inside the form whose other fields wait for 'Save travel and subsistence'. NN/g and the system's Switch rule say a toggle never shares a form with a Save, because the person cannot tell which fields took effect. *Direction:* Move the switch out of the form onto its own line above it (it already saves itself), or make it a checkbox saved with the form. (S). *Sceptic:* Reproduced: the Travel card's `.office-form` holds one role=switch between Fuel price and A car uses, acting at once (setAuto), with 'Save travel and subsistence' at the end (TravelCard.tsx:124,150). The system's Switch and NN/g agree a toggle does not share a form with Save. One screen, so a finding; P3 as rated. Screenshots: `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/admin-cards-phone.png`
- **forms-F2 · Two-to-five fixed choices are drawn as a select, radios or pressed buttons depending on the screen** (P3 · severity 1 · Kept by the sceptic). Status (3) and Kind (4) on New job and Change details are selects; Numbered/Counted and Ours/Dry hire on Add product are radios; the fault kind on Report damage and the department filter are aria-pressed buttons; Leave's Type (2) is a select. The system's Limited Choice rule says two to five show at once. *Direction:* Status, Kind and Leave type become the segmented control (the aria-pressed row Faults already draws), with the 'A confirmed job goes on the calendar…' hint under it; radios stay where a hint sits under each option (Add product's tracking). (S). *Sceptic:* Reproduced: Status (3) and Kind (4) are selects on New job and Change details, Leave's Type is a select of 2 (Leave.tsx:404), Add product's tracking is radios, Faults' kind is aria-pressed buttons. The system has a segmented control (components.md:158) and the Limited Choice rule says two to five show at once. Real but polish: a 3-option select costs one tap more and hides nothing the office does not know. P3 as rated. Screenshots: `screens/ux-sweep/lens-forms/new-job-phone.png`
- **forms-F5 · Save models are five, but they mostly match the research's rule** (P3 · severity 1 · Kept by the sceptic). The quote draft saves as it goes with 'Saving… / Saved / Saved on this device / Not saved yet'; records (job, phase, person, vehicle) have Save plus Cancel; admin cards have Save plus a Pending line; the leave allowances have a Save disabled until touched; Purchases says 'Saved. … sees it in their notifications.' after a status change. The research's verdict (drafts autosave, records and settings Save) is what the app does, so this is a note not a theme: the inconsistency is only in what is said after Save (Pending line, green 'Saved.' line, the form closing, or nothing) and the disabled-until-touched allowance, which forms-4 covers. *Direction:* Keep the models; say the result the same way after every Save: the form closes and a quiet line where it was says what was saved ('Saved the job' / 'Saved on this device'), as the quote readout's words already do. (S). *Sceptic:* A note, as the reviewer says, not a theme: the save models match the research's rule (drafts autosave, records and settings Save), and the part that differs (what is said after Save, the disabled-until-touched allowance) is covered by forms-4. Keep it as the note.

**What held up:** required.ts: every empty required field is refused in the app's words ('Fill in the job first.', 'Fill in the first name first.', 'Fill in the name first.'), focus goes to the first, the line lasts and goes on the fixing keystroke, with aria-invalid and aria-describedby: one refusal voice across every app form walked. Confirm (act.tsx): outcome-labelled buttons ('Book anyway' / 'Don't book it', 'Cancel the leave' / 'Keep it'), safe answer focused, opens in place; no browser dialog anywhere (grep: 0 window.confirm). Every app field is 16px and 44px high at 390px (14 forms measured); ticks have a 44px label row. Dates are native with `min={start}` on every To field and the Reply by default with its hint; the quote line, admin numbers and most money fields use inputMode decimal. The quote draft's autosave words ('Saved', 'Saved on this device', 'Not saved yet') and the sticky readout follow ADR 0080/0100 and the system's Line-item editor (one line open, Done and Remove inside, Undo after Remove). The server-drawn forms redraw with what was typed (page.ts `typed.get(...)`) and mark the field in error with aria-invalid and aria-describedby. Hints are real `<small>` text joined by aria-describedby on the admin cards, and the office/travel cards explain each number in one sentence.

### The director

*Are we all right this week, and what needs me.*

A director opens the app to ask one thing: are we all right this week, and what needs me. Today the app answers with the office's to-do list. Home's Company view is the same list of counts the office gets (fifteen tiles on a phone, nine of them housekeeping such as '73 answers to check', '50 timesheets to approve', '12 places to count this week', '74 jobs in Planning with the next task down to nobody'), the Crew tab carries the office's 74 on every screen, and the two lines that are a director's own ('1 job below zero: Kilbeg University AGM' and the Money card) sit on the fifth phone screen. No surface says that a job is at risk: the Board, the Jobs list and Home's week ahead each carry the facts ('5 of 10 booked · 7 lines of kit, 1 short · 3 days left') in a different shape and leave the director to work out which of 31 jobs in Planning matter. Money is told in four places in four frames (Home's card, Jobs › Money as a seventh tab off the phone's screen, Quotes as its own area, each quote's own margin lines), lost or lapsed sales are counted but never summed in euro, and on a phone the By month table has lost its Quoted, Confirmed and Invoiced columns to a rule written for sent quotes. The bell is personal by decision (ADR 0058), so a director who makes no jobs and sends no offers reads 'Nothing here yet' while a quote is turned down, a fault stops kit and a job goes below zero. What held up is strong: every count opens the list that explains it, one tap from Home's week ahead lands on a job head that shows crew, kit, stage and Next in one phone screen, the figures agree everywhere, and What's changed and Status answer in one plain line.

#### director-1 · Home's Company view puts the director's own lines last and says nothing in a sentence

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles D · phone 390×844 dark, desk 1440×900 light · reproduced

Home's Company view is one list for every role with the abilities (standing.ts tiles() filters by ability only; HomeScreen.tsx:274): the director and the office see the same 15 tiles, and on a phone the director's own lines (the Money card, '1 job below zero', the lapsed sales) sit on the fifth screen, 3,297 px down a 3,880 px page. Nothing says in one line whether the week is all right: the only verdict Home has is the all-clear sentence when every count is zero, which at volume it never is. The housekeeping counts are the director's to glance at (personas.md D), so they belong on the view, but at full tile size they outweigh the fortnight's six.

- **Evidence:** #home (Company), phone 390×844 dark, director: Needs attention: 'The next fortnight' 6 tiles then 'Housekeeping' 9 tiles (73 answers to check, 15 repairs open, 6 damaged items can't go out, 50 timesheets to approve, 15 certificates and documents running out, 5 vehicle checks running out, 12 places to count this week, 7 jobs whose dates passed with no answer, 74 jobs in Planning with the next task down to nobody); Today (1); Jobs by week; Crew, the next fortnight; The week ahead (6); The season; Money last. (`web/src/home/standing.ts:197-203 (TILES_FOR.company, one list for every role); web/src/home/HomeScreen.tsx:274 (Company view draws tiles(view, today, me, can) with no role shape)`) [Page 3,880 px tall = 4.6 screens (document.scrollHeight / innerHeight); headings by offset: Needs attention 365, Housekeeping 847, Today 1,434, Jobs by week 1,578, The week ahead 2,317, Money 3,297. 15 tiles counted from a.tile; 5 toned bad.] · #home desk 1440×900 light: The same list two columns wide; the Money card and 'below zero' line are below the fold. [Money heading below 900 px on a 1,440×900 desk; the first screen is Needs attention plus Today (1).] · Every screen, director: the phone bar and desk sidebar: Crew badge '74' (aria ', 74 to check') on every screen for a director; the 74 are the office's answers to check. (`web/src/Shell.tsx:45-52 (badge shown to any role with crew)`) [Visible on all 16 phone screenshots in this folder.] · map-roles.json matrix, 'Home default + cards': Director and office Company views are recorded as 'identical'. (`web/src/home/standing.ts:119-195 tiles() filters by ability only (NEEDS), not by what the role is for`)
- **Basis:** Behaviour rule Salience (personas.md D: 'Healthy is quiet: a count that is zero isn't shown' — but a count that isn't the director's is still shown); Bundling bad news; NN/g heuristic 8 (aesthetic and minimalist design: every extra unit of information competes with the relevant units); NN/g dashboard research (a dashboard must answer its question in five seconds). · NN/g 'Dashboards: Making Charts and Graphs Easier to Understand' and 'Dashboard Design' (answer the question first, five-second test); design-system behavioural.md Salience and Bundling bad news; GOV.UK 'start with user needs'; personas.md D.
- **Proposal (S):** Add a one-line verdict at the top of the Company view and move Money up under the week ahead; fold the housekeeping behind a disclosure for directors only, open for the office. On #home Company: (1) one line above the tiles, in the hint style when healthy and as ds-notice--warn when any 'soon' tile is bad: 'This week: 4 confirmed jobs on, 2 short of crew or kit · €19,189 confirmed of €22,729 quoted' (the same figures Money's tiles use, so they agree). (2) MoneyCard moves from last to straight after WeekAhead for roles with the money ability; the two charts and The season follow. (3) 'Housekeeping (9)' becomes a ds disclosure, closed by default when role.ts says director and open otherwise; the phone remembers it like the folds (sh.home-style key). Every tile stays reachable and the office's view is unchanged in order. The Crew badge is left as it is (ADR 0041, and a director can check answers).
- **Trade-off:** The first by-role difference inside one view (ADR 0033 rejected role-shaped Home when there were no roles; ADR 0034 has since made them). A director who also does the office's work opens the disclosure once. **Depends on:** Nothing. director-2's count of jobs at risk would feed the verdict line's '2 short of crew or kit'. **Weighed:** A role-shaped Company view with 'Needs a decision' and 'At risk' sections (the reviewer's M): weighed, but 'leave waiting on you' is already a tile for approvers, counter-offers are the office's, and the persona wants a glance not a second dashboard. A fifth 'Director' view: rejected, views are places.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/01-home-full-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/01-home-top-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/01-home-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-director/home-company-director-phone-dark-full.png`
- **Learning:** A shared Home 'view' that is the same for every role becomes the busiest role's list; the system's stat tile and disclosure can shape one view by role without a new view.
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced on my server (director, phone 390x844 dark): 15 a.tile, 5 toned bad, page 3,880 px = 4.6 screens, Money heading at y 3,297, Crew badge '74, 74 to check' on every screen; the office's Home has the identical 15 tiles (3,899 px), and the desk puts Money at y 1,968 of a 900 px window. But the statement overreaches: personas.md D lists 'anything stuck (answers waiting, timesheets not approved, kit not back)' as what a director looks at, so '73 answers to check' and '50 timesheets to approve' are the director's by the persona's own words, and ADR 0033's 'Home at volume' already split the fortnight from the housekeeping and drew the housekeeping smaller. The Crew badge is legitimately offered: a director has the crew ability (roles.ts) and ADR 0041 ties the badge to Calls and Leave, so it isn't a rule broken, only a salience cost. The sketch also names a 'settled tone' the design system's notice doesn't have (components.md: --ok, --warn, --bad). What stands is real: nothing says in one line whether the week is all right, the director's own lines (Money, the job below zero, the lapsed sales) are last on 4.6 phone screens, and the view is the office's at full size. At a desk the first screen already answers (audit, 5 Nov), so NN 2, P2, and the fix is smaller than a role-shaped Home. (was: "Home's Company view speaks to the office, not to a director")

#### director-2 · A job short of crew or kit is counted differently on every surface, and never named as one thing

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles D, O, H · phone 390×844 dark, desk 1440×900 light · reproduced

The stage line says the facts ('5 of 10 booked · 7 lines of kit, 1 short · 3 days left') but only the kit part is ever toned, and no surface counts jobs in trouble the same way: Home counts calls (7) and kit lines (3), the Board's Planning head counts '1 short' from the current task's hint only (BoardScreen.tsx columnWord), so three of the four cards that say '1 short' in that column aren't counted, and a crew gap three days out is plain text everywhere. A director scanning 31 Planning cards has no word to scan for and no count to open.

- **Evidence:** #jobs/board phone dark, Planning column: Head '31 · 1 short'. Cards: 'Harbour Lights Festival … 5 of 10 booked · 7 lines of kit, 1 short, 2 vehicles · 3 days left' (the short part amber), 'Lee Tech Christmas Party … No crew asked for yet · 2 lines of kit · 6 days left' (plain), 'Moy Film Showcase … 0 of 2 booked, 1 to confirm · 2 lines of kit · 12 days left' (plain). (`web/src/jobs/BoardScreen.tsx:104-136 (card line regexes the task hints; only 'short' gets the warn tone)`) [Board 5,127 px = 6.1 phone screens; 31 Planning cards; 'days left' on every card regardless of state.] · #jobs list phone dark, Next week: 'Harbour Lights Festival … Planning · 5 of 10 booked · 7 lines of kit, 1 short, 2 vehicles' with pill Confirmed, chip 'Crew 5 of 10', flag 'Kit short'; the crew gap carries no tone. (`web/src/jobs/StageLine.tsx; web/src/jobs/JobsScreen.tsx:449`) [4.2 screens; 'Kit short' is the only flag on rows.] · #home The week ahead (6), phone dark: 'Harbour Lights Festival · Mon 9 Nov · Planning · Crew 5 of 10 · Kit short'; 'Brightwater Tech Summit · Wed 11 Nov · Planning · Crew 1 of 2 · Kit short'; 'Moy Film Conference · Sat 7 Nov · Planning · Crew 2 of 2'. Three rows, two in trouble, same weight. [The row is 2.9 screens down (y 2,468 of 3,880); one tap lands on the job head.] · #jobs/<id> Harbour Lights Festival phone dark: Head 'CREW 5 of 10 booked · KIT 7 products, 1 short'; dock 'Next · Planning · 5 of 10 booked · Book 5 more crew · Hand it to someone'; 'Where it is · In Planning · 2 of 8 tasks done'. The whole picture is one screen, but no word says the job is at risk three days out. (`shared/src/sync/steps-view.ts:297-343 (current/stage; no risk roll-up); web/src/jobs/NextDock.tsx`) [1 tap from Home; crew, kit, stage and Next all above the fold; the Quote tab (expected margin 70.8%) is 1 more tap.] · #notifications › To sort out, phone dark: 'Harbour Lights Festival 1 short · Rigger · Load in · in 3 days · Find crew' and again for Stagehand: the same job's risk, a fifth shape, by call.
- **Basis:** NN/g heuristic 1 (visibility of system status: the state that matters must be told, not computed by the reader); behavioural Salience and Loss Aversion; job-flow research 1 Nov (stage-gate gates carry risk; field-service products' 'at risk' flag). · Job-flow research 2026-11-01 (stage-gate, field-service 'at risk'); NN/g 'Visibility of System Status'; design-system principles.md colour grammar (amber: something needs a look).
- **Proposal (M):** Work out one 'at risk' state per job in steps-view, from the job's own prep days, and draw it as one warn chip with a count on the list, the Board, Home's week ahead and the job head. shared/src/sync/steps-view.ts gains atRisk(job): a job still to come whose start is inside its prep days plus three and whose crew booked is below needed, a kit line short, or a task late; with a reason list. One chip in the warn tone (the system's chip, ADR 0109: computed from records, never a tick): 'At risk · 5 crew short, 1 kit line short'. Drawn beside the stage line on the Jobs list row, as the Board card's line tone and in the column head ('31 · 4 at risk', replacing the regex count), on Home's week-ahead row where 'Kit short' is now, and in the job head beside the status pill. Home's Company view gets one tile 'N jobs at risk this fortnight' opening #jobs with a new Filter value 'At risk'. 'N days left' stays on every card.
- **Trade-off:** A new derived state to keep truthful; the threshold must be the job's own prep days, not a constant, or a job with long prep reads as fine until it isn't. The office may read a chip they already infer as noise on a desk. **Depends on:** Nothing; director-1's verdict line uses its count. **Weighed:** Toning the crew numbers amber when short (S): weighed, cheaper, fixes the tone but not the Board head's count nor a count to open. Sorting the Board by risk: rejected, soonest-first is the Board's one rule (ADR 0104).
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/24-board-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/04-jobs-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/20-home-week-ahead-row-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/21-job-head-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/09-notifications-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-director/board-planning-director-phone-dark.png`
- **Learning:** Where a system has a stage line, it needs a risk chip beside it: the stage says where, the chip says whether it will make it.
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced: the Board's Planning column reads '31 · 1 short' while four of its cards say '1 short' (Harbour Lights, Brightwater, Lagan Rugby Club Summit, Clare Hotels Fundraiser), three of them also short of crew 3 to 6 days out; BoardScreen.tsx columnWord() counts a job as short only when its current ('now') task's hint contains 'short', so a job whose Next is Crew booked ('5 of 10 booked') is never counted, and Home meanwhile says '7 calls still need crew'. The job head (job-head-harbour-director-phone-dark.png) and list carry the same words from one function (stage.hint), so 'five shapes' is overstated: it is two shapes (the stage line, and Home's 'Crew 5 of 10 · Kit short') plus the planner's call-level rows (ADR 0069, by design). The basis is also wrong in part: the 1 November research copy has no 'at risk' flag from rental or field-service products; what it adopted is task rows with a state in words ('1 short', 'Behind 3 days left'), which the app has. What's real and new: only kit 'short' is ever toned, a crew gap days out has no tone or count anywhere, and the Board's own count disagrees with its own cards. NN 2 (the facts are on every surface; the roll-up is missing), P2. The proposal's 'drop N days left on at-risk cards' loses information for the office; keep it. (was: "A job's risk is counted on every surface but never named, so the director can't see which jobs are in trouble")

#### director-3 · What was lost or lapsed is counted but never summed in euro, and Home's Money card frames the month in different words from Money's tiles

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles D, O · phone 390×844 dark, desk 1440×900 light · reproduced

Money's stat tiles say quoted, confirmed, accepted unpaid and owed to freelancers; Home's Money card says 'confirmed' and 'still a quote'; Quotes counts Turned down (7) with no euro; By month has no lost column and its note says a turned-down quote isn't counted. A director asking 'what did we lose this month' has a count on Quotes and a count on Home but no figure anywhere, and reads the month in two vocabularies between Home and Money.

- **Evidence:** #home Money card, phone dark: 'November 2026: €19,189 confirmed, €3,540 still a quote', chart Aug–Jan, '1 job below zero: Kilbeg University AGM, −€116.56.' Last card on the page. (`web/src/home/HomeScreen.tsx:287-293`) [Heading at y 3,297 of 3,880 (fifth screen).] · #jobs/money phone dark: Tab strip reads '…eek Month Clients Venues Money' (Money is the seventh chip, scrolled into view). Stat tiles 'QUOTED FOR NOVEMBER 2026 €22,729.00 · CONFIRMED €19,189.00 · ACCEPTED, UNPAID €166,715.70 with VAT, of which €98,066.70 not invoiced yet · OWED TO FREELANCERS €14,873.00'. By month then shows only Month and Freelancers (see finding director-f1). (`web/src/Views.tsx (Jobs area rows: List | Board | Week | Month | Clients | Venues | Money); web/src/jobs/Money.tsx:389-425`) [5,977 px = 7.1 screens; desk 4,526 px = 5.0 screens.] · #quotes phone dark: '‹ Menu · Quotes · To follow up': Waiting for an answer (8 shown, 'Show all 9 quotes'), 'Dates passed with no answer' with one quote (Boyne Arts Collective Christmas Party, €499.62, Sent 183 days ago, Mark the job lost), Not sent yet (9); All quotes with chips All (83) · Not sent (9) · Sent (12) · Accepted (55) · Turned down (7). No euro beside any chip or group. (`web/src/jobs/QuotesScreen.tsx:169-238 (To follow up groups), 87 (All quotes)`) [7,080 px = 8.4 screens; Home says 7 jobs whose dates passed, Quotes shows 1.] · #jobs/<id>/quote Harbour Lights Festival, phone dark: 'Expected margin €4,180.00, 70.8% From the quote's own costs. Not printed.' and 'Left after freelancers so far: €3,680.00 (€2,220.00 of freelancers booked or approved)'; What happened: Sent Fri 23 Oct · Accepted Sun 25 Oct · Invoiced Not yet. (`web/src/jobs/QuoteRecords.tsx:78-165`) · Desk sidebar: Home · Jobs · Quotes · Stock · Crew · Menu: Quotes is a top-level area, Money is inside Jobs, and both are money. (`web/src/Shell.tsx:48 (desk && seesMoney adds Quotes)`)
- **Basis:** NN/g heuristic 4 (consistency: one thing told in one frame); behavioural Loss Aversion (a loss told as a loss moves a decision; a loss never summed moves nothing); navigation.md (an area is one kind of thing). ADR 0042 put Money under Jobs when there was no Quotes area; ADR 0081 then added Quotes as an area, which is the cost shown here. · NN/g 'Consistency and Standards'; behavioural.md Loss Aversion; design-system components.md Stat tile and navigation.md; ADR 0042 and 0081.
- **Proposal (S):** Add a 'Lost or lapsed' stat tile and a By month column on Money, and make Home's Money card headline the same first tiles in the same words. #jobs/money: a fifth stat tile 'Lost or lapsed for November 2026 €x', summing quotes turned down and jobs marked lost (the version that stood when they were), opening #quotes narrowed to Turned down; By month gains a 'Lost' column (desk; on a phone the stacked row of finding director-f1). #home Money card: its first line reads as Money's tiles do, 'Quoted €22,729 · Confirmed €19,189 · Lost €x', then the chart and the below-zero line. Quotes' Turned down chip gets its euro sum in the count's place when narrowed. Addresses, sidebar and the Jobs strip stay as ADR 0088 has them.
- **Trade-off:** 'Lost or lapsed' needs a rule for lapsed-but-not-marked jobs (count them as 'at risk of loss' in the tile's unit line, not in the sum) and must agree with By month's note that a turned-down quote isn't quoted. **Depends on:** director-f1 first, so the phone's By month can show a new column at all. **Weighed:** One Money area holding Quotes (the reviewer's M): rejected, it reverses ADR 0088 for a seventh chip on the Jobs strip. Folding Money into Quotes: rejected, the accountant's files and freelancers owed aren't quotes.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/01-home-full-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/07-money-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/08-quotes-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/23-job-quote-page-full-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/07-money-top-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-director/quotes-director-phone-dark-top.png`
- **Learning:** When a second area grows beside a card (Quotes beside Money), the first card's home should be revisited: an app's areas should be nouns a director would name.
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced: Home's card ('€19,189 confirmed, €3,540 still a quote'), Money's four stat tiles (quoted, confirmed, accepted unpaid, owed to freelancers), Quotes' chips (Turned down (7), no euro) and the quote page frame the month four ways, and nothing sums turned-down or lapsed quotes in euro. But the proposal reverses ADR 0088 ('Quotes, a place of its own', Colly's own ask on 28 October: a sixth sidebar place, 'Money keeps the figures and links to Quotes'), which the reviewer did not cite; moving Money to the top and Quotes under it is a decision re-litigated without showing its cost beyond a seventh chip on the Jobs strip. The reviewer's own alternative (a Lost tile on Money, S) is the right size and keeps both decisions. Loss Aversion is a fair basis for the lost sum; the rest is a new figure, not an incoherence. NN 2, P2, size S. (was: "Money is told in four places in four frames, and what was lost is never summed")

#### director-4 · A director hears nothing: the bell is personal by decision, and Home's counts don't say what changed since the last look

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles D · phone 390×844 dark, desk 1440×900 light · reproduced

ADR 0058 makes Notifications 'what's someone's own', so a director who makes no jobs and sends no offers reads 'Nothing here yet' while a quote is turned down, a fault stops kit and a job goes below zero. Home's counts are the only channel, and a count doesn't say what changed: '15 repairs open' reads the same the day after a fault stopped a confirmed job's kit. The persona's question, 'is anything about to go wrong that I need to know about', is answered only by remembering yesterday's numbers.

- **Evidence:** #notifications, director, phone dark and desk light: 'Nothing here yet. When someone changes a job you're on, your leave or an offer you sent, it shows here.' Below it 'To sort out' (calls short in three days, clashes), which is the planner's list for the office. (`server/src/notices.ts:15-37 (what counts as someone's own); docs/adr/0058-notifications.md`) [0 notifications for the director on a company of 300 jobs with 7 turned-down quotes, 20 faults and 7 lapsed enquiries in the data.] · #home Company, phone dark: The counts are the only channel: '7 jobs whose dates passed with no answer', '15 repairs open', '6 damaged items can't go out'. No 'new since you last looked' on any tile, though What's changed (1.88.0) already marks what's new since the last look. (`web/src/home/standing.ts:119-195 (counts, no delta); shared/src/changelog.ts 1.88.0 ('marks what's new since you last looked')`) · map-flow.json handoffs: 'W/H (job through) → O, D: invoice the client — nothing on the job page or Board links the end of the job to invoicing'; 'W (scans back, reports a fault) → whoever ticks deprep: nobody is told'; 'the app (kit short) → O, H: no notification (nothing is a command)'. (`server/src/notices.ts; shared/src/sync/steps-view.ts`)
- **Basis:** Behavioural Loss Aversion and Bundling bad news (tell the bad once, together, as it happens); NN/g heuristic 1 (visibility of system status); personas.md D ('anything about to go wrong that I need to know about'). ADR 0058 decided the personal list; the cost is a director with an empty bell. · behavioural.md Loss Aversion, Bundling bad news; NN/g 'Visibility of System Status' and 'Indicators, Validations, and Notifications'; ADR 0058.
- **Proposal (S):** Give Home's tiles the design system's delta line, 'since you last looked', from a per-account last-seen cursor, and name the change on the tiles that lose money or stop work. standing.ts tiles() gain a delta from the history since the account's last Home visit (a second cursor beside notices_read, or the device's sh.home-seen as What's changed does): a tile reads '15 repairs open' with ds-stat__delta '2 new since Tuesday' in ink.secondary, and data-tone="bad" when one stops kit. For the few that lose money, the delta names the thing: '7 jobs whose dates passed with no answer · Boyne Arts lapsed Tue'. Healthy is quiet: no delta when nothing changed. The bell stays as ADR 0058 has it.
- **Trade-off:** A delta of +2 doesn't name every job or item; the tile opens the list that does. One more cursor to keep per account. **Depends on:** Nothing; the history words exist for each event. **Weighed:** 'The company' section in Notifications for roles with the money ability (the reviewer's M): weighed, truer to 'bundling bad news' but new scope on ADR 0058 and a louder bell for the office; worth returning to if the deltas aren't enough. Email or push: out, the app contacts nobody.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/09-notifications-top-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/01-home-full-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-director/notifications-director-phone-dark.png`
- **Learning:** A notification list built from 'what's yours' is empty for the person whose job is to watch everything; the system's notification list needs a company scope as well as a personal one.
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced: the director's #notifications reads 'Nothing here yet. When someone changes a job you're on, your leave or an offer you sent, it shows here' on a company with 7 turned-down quotes, faults and a job below zero, on phone and desk; notices.ts scopes the list to you / sent / on / made, as ADR 0058 decided, and Home's tiles carry no 'since you last looked'. The cost is real and the reviewer cites the ADR fairly. But a 'The company' feed is new scope on top of a decision Colly made for personal news, and it makes the office's bell louder; the smaller, system-shaped answer is the one the reviewer put second: the design system's stat tile has ds-stat__delta, 'what changed, in words', for exactly this, and Home's tiles are stat tiles. What's changed (1.88.0) already keeps a last-seen mark per device, so the pattern exists. NN 2, P2, with the delta as the proposal and the company feed as the later L. (was: "The director hears nothing: the bell is personal, and the company's bad news reaches a director only by looking")

**Smaller findings**

- **director-f1 · On a phone, Money's By month and Margin by job tables lose their middle columns to the sent quote's rule** (P1 · severity 3 · Adjusted by the sceptic). The 1.79.2 rule for a sent quote's lines on a phone (jobs.css:466, every th and td of a .quote-table that isn't first or last hidden under 640 px) is unscoped, so Money's By month shows only Month and Freelancers and Margin by job only Job and After freelancers, with no sideways scroll (scrollWidth equals clientWidth) and a footnote that explains the Quoted and Confirmed columns the phone can't see. A director on a phone reads a money table with three of five columns gone; the desk shows all of them. *Direction:* Scope the phone rule to the quote sheet's table and give the two Money tables a stacked phone row, as a sent quote's lines now read. (S). *Sceptic:* Reproduced exactly: on #jobs/money at 390 px the By month thead's Quoted, Confirmed and Invoiced th are display:none and the wrap has no overflow (scrollWidth 324 = clientWidth 324), so the columns are gone, not off-screen; the desk shows all five. jobs.css:466 '.quote-table :is(th, td):not(:first-child, :last-child) { display: none }' is unscoped, written for the sent quote sheet (audit finding 59, 1.79.2). It is wider than reported: the same rule strips Quoted and Freelancers from Margin by job (table.quote-table.money-margins, Money.tsx:582), leaving 'Job · After freelancers', and the footnote under By month still explains 'Quoted counts the quote that stands on each job… confirmed, the accepted ones' for columns the phone can't see (money-by-month-director-phone-dark.png). A regression on the one money page, a director on a phone reads a table missing three of five columns: NN 3, P1, S. Screenshots: `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/07-money-top-phone-dark.png`
- **director-f2 · Home counts 7 jobs whose dates passed with no answer, Quotes' section of the same name lists 1** (P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic). Home's tile counts every job with steps.lapsed (standing.ts:193) and opens the Board's fold of 7; Quotes › To follow up › 'Dates passed with no answer' lists sent quotes only (Money.tsx:219-233, f.passed), so it shows 1. Same words, two counts, and nothing on either screen bridges them. *Direction:* Either Quotes' section lists the same seven, an enquiry with no quote reading 'No quote was sent · Mark the job lost', or its heading says 'Quotes whose dates passed with no answer (1 of 7 jobs)' with a link to the Board's fold. (S). *Sceptic:* Reproduced: Home's tile '7 jobs whose dates passed with no answer' opens the Board's 'Dates passed, no answer (7)'; Quotes › To follow up › 'Dates passed with no answer' lists one (Boyne Arts Collective Christmas Party, €499.62, Sent 183 days ago). The code citation is wrong in file: the follow-up groups live in web/src/jobs/Money.tsx:219-233 (f.passed, sent quotes whose job lapsed), used by QuotesScreen; standing.ts:193 counts every job with steps.lapsed. Two screens, one phrase, two counts: a real consistency slip, not in the 5 November audit (finding 4 there was quotes for jobs already over, fixed differently). NN 2, P2, S; the wording fix is the cheaper of the two directions. Screenshots: `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/03-board-full-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-director/08-quotes-top-phone-dark.png`

**What held up:** Every count on Home opens the list that explains it, and the office's and warehouse's counts land on the right part (Kit short, Pick lists, Answers to check). One tap from Home's week ahead lands on a job head that shows client, venue, dates, crew 5 of 10, kit 1 short, the stage strip and the Next dock in one phone screen; the quote with its expected margin is one more tap. The figures agree: Home's Money card, Money's stat tiles, By month (on a desk) and the quote page all say €19,189 confirmed of €22,729 quoted for November. The stage line reads the same words on the list, the Board and Home ('Planning · 5 of 10 booked · 7 lines of kit, 1 short, 2 vehicles'), and the Board's Selling cards are specific and timed ('Quote with the client since Tue 3 Nov'). What's changed is in the office's words, newest first, with the version rule in one sentence and new-since-last-look marks; Status opens with a one-line answer and Check again; the Menu's lines say what each screen is for in plain words. Healthy is quiet in the charts: only crew still needed is amber; a fully booked fortnight has no colour.

**Dropped by the sceptic:** director-f3 (Admin › Status tells every director what to set in Railway: Reproduced ('5 things need a look', each 'Set X in Railway'), but it is decided and already recorded: ADR 0093 specifies the 'What to do' lines with 'Set SENTRY_DSN in Railway' as its own example, and the 5 November audit's sceptic already corrected that finding with 'Optionally lead each Railway step with Ask Colly to…' (audit line 1399), which 1.81.0 acted on for Claude and the mailbox. On live, five of the six amber lines don't appear (Neon, Google sign-in and backups are set); the harness makes them. One screen, no pattern, known to Colly: P3 at most.); director-f4 (The Department view offers a director nine departments in a select that mixes crew and stock meanings: Reproduced (nine departments in the select, the two-sentence hint), but ADR 0033 designed the Department view for a head and the reviewer rates it NN 1 and says they weighed it and left it; the Company view already sums every department. A table of departments is an M for a once-a-season glance. Polish, not a cohesion problem.)

### The office

*The two people who run jobs from a desk all day.*

The office's day is well tooled piece by piece: Did you mean on New job, one filled Next on the job page, the stage line on every list row, Answers to check bundling declines and pull-outs with a Noted-all, To follow up grouped by what to do, and To sort out warning three days ahead. What it is not yet is one flow. 'What needs me' is spread over six surfaces (Home's two groups, Crew's Answers to check and Crew needed, Notifications' To sort out and the planner's copy of it, Quotes' To follow up, Timesheets' To approve) in different words and with counts that don't quite agree (Home says 73 answers, the list it opens is headed 74). When the office follows a link into a job from any of those surfaces on a phone, they land at the top of the job page with the part they came for 1,052 px down, because only the Next button's own press brings the tab row into view. A quote waiting on a client is said four ways across the Board, the list, the job page and Quotes, and 'worth a call' only ever shows on Quotes. And the job's story on the job page and the Board ends at deprep, while the office's ends at timesheets approved, invoiced and paid, which live only on Crew › Timesheets and Money, grouped by client rather than by job. None of these is a slip on one screen: each is the same thing drawn differently in two or more places.

#### office-1 · What needs the office is spread over six surfaces, and its own Home puts its work under Housekeeping

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles O, D · desk 1440 light, phone 390 dark · reproduced

The office's question ('who's still to answer, what's not crewed, what's not followed up, what's not approved') is answered on six surfaces: Home's Needs attention (The next fortnight, then Housekeeping), Crew › Answers to check and Crew needed, Notifications' and the planner's To sort out, Quotes › To follow up and Crew › Timesheets › To approve. On the Home the office sees (the Company view, because Home has no office view and the office has no department) its two biggest items, 73 answers and 50 timesheets, sit 7th and 10th under 'Housekeeping' below warehouse tiles, while personas.md promises the office's Home 'puts answers, crew needed, timesheets, documents and running late first'. Home's '73 answers to check' opens a list headed 'Answers to check (74)' because Home pulls running late out as its own tile. The cost is a day of hopping between Home, Crew, Notifications and Quotes with no one list that is theirs.

- **Evidence:** #home (office): Needs attention › The next fortnight: 1 person running late, 7 calls still need crew, 3 kit lines short, 3 jobs with kit still out, 1 job to pick, 16 items can't go out until tested. Housekeeping: 73 answers to check, 15 repairs open, 6 damaged items, 50 timesheets to approve, 15 certificates… 74 jobs in Planning with the next task down to nobody. The office's two biggest items (73 answers, 50 timesheets) sit under 'Housekeeping' below warehouse tiles. (`web/src/home/HomeScreen.tsx:151-162; web/src/home/standing.ts:162-165`) [Answers to check is the 7th tile of 15 on the office's Home; timesheets the 10th (counted from the innerText of #home as the office persona)] · #home → #crew: Tapping '73 answers to check' lands on #crew at scrollY 180 under a heading 'Answers to check (74)'. Tapping '7 calls still need crew' lands on the same page at scrollY 996 (Playwright, phone 390×844). (`web/src/home/standing.ts:162-163 (late and answers are two tiles); web/src/crew/CrewScreen.tsx:410 (one heading counts late + docs + toSortOut + toCheck)`) [Home 73 vs Crew heading 74; both tiles open the same address #crew] · #notifications and #jobs/plan: 'To sort out' with the identical five rows (Harbour Lights Festival 1 short · Rigger · in 3 days · Find crew; Dara Quinn Check; Big Van Clash; The Luton Check) is drawn on Notifications and again on the planner's Week page. (`web/src/jobs/PlanScreen.tsx:566-578; Notifications' copy of the same list`) [5 rows, same words, two screens] · #crew, #home, #jobs/plan, #jobs (Filter), #jobs/<id>: One state, crew short, in six phrasings: 'Crew needed' (Crew heading), 'calls still need crew' (Home), 'Crew still to find' (planner legend), 'Find crew' (To sort out button), 'Short of crew' (Crew sub-heading), 'Still needs crew' (Jobs filter), 'Book 3 more crew' (Next). (`web/src/crew/CrewScreen.tsx:583,621; web/src/jobs/PlanScreen.tsx:520,578; web/src/home/standing.ts:164; web/src/jobs/NextDock.tsx:122`) [6 distinct phrasings counted across 5 screens] · #quotes, #crew/timesheets: Quotes › To follow up and Crew › Timesheets › To approve (50) are each a list of their own with no link from Home's groups other than the count tile; Home's Needs attention has no quote tile at all (quotes to chase appear only after 7 days and only under Quotes and Notifications). (`web/src/jobs/Money.tsx:186,212,261 (CHASE_AFTER_DAYS)`)
- **Basis:** NN/g heuristic 4 (consistency and standards) and 1 (visibility of status); the design system's Bundling bad news rule (one list for the answers to check) and Salience (the few things that need you stand out); personas.md O: 'Home, shown for the office, puts answers, crew needed, timesheets, documents and running late first'. The counts breaking Colly's rule that every count opens the list that explains it. · NN/g ten heuristics (consistency, visibility); design-system behavioural.md (Bundling bad news, Salience, Limited Choice); GOV.UK pattern 'task list' (one list, one place); sess-sys docs/personas.md (O)
- **Proposal (S):** Order the Company view's Needs attention for whoever is looking, by role, so the office's Home is the office's list, and make Home's answers count equal Crew's heading. HomeScreen.tsx Tiles: the SOON set (what goes under 'The next fortnight') becomes a function of the viewer's role (roles.ts): for 'office' it is late, answers, crew-needed, timesheets, tasks-nobody, no-answer, certificates, then the rest under Housekeeping; directors keep today's order; heads and the warehouse are unchanged. Add a quotes tile to standing.ts for money roles: 'N quotes worth a call' (Money's CHASE_AFTER_DAYS rule, href #quotes, part 'To follow up'), so the fourth of the office's four questions is on Home at all. Counts: either drop Home's separate running-late tile into the answers count (Crew lists late first anyway) or head Crew's card 'Answers to check (73) · 1 running late' (see office-6). Words: retire 'Crew still to find' (PlanScreen.tsx:520 legend) and 'Short of crew' (CrewScreen.tsx:621) for 'Still needs crew', the Jobs filter's phrase (shared/src/lists.ts:179); 'Crew needed', 'calls still need crew', 'Find crew' and 'Book N more crew' stay, as heading, count, button and Next. The planner's and Notifications' To sort out both stay: the planner is where the office books.
- **Trade-off:** Two people looking at the same Company view see different tile orders, which needs one line in the Menu's 'about' text; a director who does office work still has the office's items under Housekeeping. **Depends on:** standing.ts tiles already carry part keys and arrive() scrolls to them; the role is on the device (web/src/role.ts). **Weighed:** Weighed the reviewer's fifth 'Office' view: cleaner in name but one more view to maintain and explain, for the same tiles in a different order; and a Menu-level 'Needs me' screen, rejected because ADR 0033 made Home the overview and the office opens the app there.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-office/home-office-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-office/home-tile-crew-needed-lands-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-office/notifications-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-office/crew-calls-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-office/quotes-desk-light.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-office/home-office-desk-light.png`
- **Learning:** A persona's 'one list of what needs me' should be one surface; a second copy of a list on another screen should be a link, not a redraw.
