A freelancer who wants their year's work for an invoice or tax return (the persona's 'get paid') can see eight jobs and no more. It is the one place on the page where a list ends without saying so.

- **Evidence:** /f/xvz5Q58jkuG_Y92jWkvRY2R6 (Nessa), phone 390x844 dark: Past work: exactly eight rows, Thu 29 Oct back to Tue 23 Jun, then the next section; Declined and withdrawn: exactly eight rows. Timesheets, 4,500 px up the same page, lists 15 rows under 'Sent and approved (14)' with jobs from Feb 2026 that Past work does not show. (`server/src/crew/page.ts:598-599 (past and turned sliced to the last 8); page.ts:333-334 (the timesheets fold with its count, for contrast)`) [Past work 8 rows / 581 px; Declined and withdrawn 8 rows / 581 px; Timesheets fold 15 rows. Counted from the DOM (li per section).]
- **Basis:** Design-system Disclosure ('say what is inside': a count on a closed fold) and states.md Many rows (fold, never cut silently); NN/g heuristic 1 (status: is this all of it?) and 4 (consistency with the page's own folds). · Design-system components.md Disclosure, states.md Many rows; NN/g heuristics 1 and 4
- **Proposal (S):** Fold the two lists the way Timesheets folds, with the first few in full and the rest under a counted summary, instead of cutting at eight. On /f/<token>: Past work (or freelancer-1's Work done) and Declined and withdrawn each show the last three rows and fold the rest under '<details class="later"><summary><b>Earlier (N)</b></summary>', the page's existing fold, with no cap or a cap of a year said in the summary ('Earlier this year (23)'). The footer's download stays as the full record.
- **Trade-off:** A freelancer with a long history makes the HTML longer in the fold (closed, so no scroll cost); a year's cap keeps it bounded. **Depends on:** Nothing; page.ts only. Pairs with freelancer-1. **Weighed:** Say 'Your last 8' in the heading: honest but leaves the rest unreachable except as JSON. Rejected.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-freelancer/nessa-dark-phone-full.png`
- **Sceptic:** added by the sceptic

**Smaller findings**

- **freelancer-5 · The office's and the freelancer's words for one booking state differ in three places** (P3 · severity 1 · Kept by the sceptic). A confirmed person is 'Booked' on the app's call sheet pills and in 'Crew 5 of 10 booked' on the Jobs list, 'Confirmed' on the link's tag and in its Past work rows, 'to confirm' on the link's sheet crew list and '(the office will confirm)' beside 'You'. An accepted offer is 'Accepted' (pending) for the office and 'Accepted, the office will confirm' for the freelancer: fine. A pull-out is 'Can't make it' for the office and 'You've pulled out' for the freelancer (freelancer-3). Each pair is understandable on its own; across a phone call between the two ('are you booked or confirmed?') they are two vocabularies. *Direction:* Pick 'Booked' for the state and use it on both sides: the link's tag 'Booked', its sheet 'booked' / 'not booked yet', the office's 'Confirm' button can stay as the act that books. Or keep 'Confirmed' everywhere and change the app's pills. Size S; decide with the words lens. (S). *Sceptic:* Reproduced in code: the link's tag 'Confirmed' (page.ts:122) and sheet 'to confirm' / '(the office will confirm)' (sheet.ts:110,154); the app's call sheet pills 'Booked' / 'To confirm' (SheetScreen.tsx:251) and the job's 'N of M booked, K to confirm' (JobScreen.tsx:347,600; JobsScreen.tsx:456). Two vocabularies for one state across a phone call. Not in the audit's 103 and no ADR fixes the word (ADR 0002 defines 'accepted' and 'confirmed' as states, not the word crew see). Right as a P3 finding; it belongs with the words lens, which should decide 'Booked' or 'Confirmed' once for the pills, the link and the job's count together, and 'Confirm' can stay as the office's act either way. Screenshots: `screens/ux-sweep/lens-freelancer/app-staff-jobs-dark-phone.png`

**What held up:** The glance line: sticky, three parts, each a link down the page, and it updates at once after every answer ('3 offers' → '2 offers' → 'Next: Clare Hotels Fundraiser, Sun 29 Nov, call 07:00'). Offers and bookings fold after three to one line each with their tag; the one a message is about opens on its own. Every post comes back to the card it was about with a message in the card ('Thanks, you're down for it. The office will confirm.'), under the sticky line, and refused posts keep what was typed. The counter flow says what Accept now means: 'Accept these days at €290 a day instead', 'Rate €320 a day asked (offered €290)'. Decline and pull-out are folded as the no-script arm, with the consequence said before the button ('Once declined, it's off your page'). The look is the app's: the SH mark, brand red as 'you' (Waiting on you, Accept), the design system's colour roles via dsColor in both themes, muted small-cap section heads; system-ui instead of the app's display font is a deliberate 9 KB, no-network choice. The wrong-link page says what to do and who to ask, with tel: and mailto: links; the office line is on every page. A timesheet is two taps (open, Send) with the rate 'as agreed' said first; sent, it says the total and that it can be changed until approved; approved and paid days are shown. The call sheet puts 'You' first, hides other crew's numbers from ordinary crew, and links the map.

**Dropped by the sceptic:** freelancer-6 (The office's timesheet backlog shows on the freelancer's page as an unanswered state with no way to chase: The rows are there as described (seven 'Sent… The office will check it' back to Fri 27 Feb 2026, three 'Approved' with no paid day), but the age is the seed's, not the page's: the approved page for the Feb booking says 'Approved on Fri 6 Nov' (the seed approved and sent them this week), and the row's date is the booking's day, not when it was sent, so nothing on the page shows a nine-month wait and no live freelancer would see one unless the office sat on a timesheet for months, which 1.79's two-month cut-off and the office's Not in yet (oldest first) are there to stop. A 'sent N weeks ago' word on a row is a one-line addition to freelancer-1's merged list if Colly wants it, not a finding of its own. Timesheets are also off live (ADR 0090).); freelancer-7 (A page with nothing on it has no line at the top saying so: Reproduced: Úna's page has no glance line and 'Nothing waiting right now.' at scrollY 221 under 'Offers waiting on you', then 'No upcoming bookings.' But that first line is the answer to 'what's waiting on me' within the first screen, in the section a returning freelancer will know, and the glance drawing nothing when there is nothing to say is the standing rule (healthy is quiet) written into page.ts:365 on purpose. Collapsing two sections into one sentence also removes the #offers and #bookings anchors the office's texts and the glance link to. The real cost on Úna's page is that the calendar prose follows at once, which is freelancer-4's theme and is fixed by it (her page becomes about one screen). Taste beyond that.)

### The shared design system

*Colour, type, space, states and parts against the system's grammar.*

sess-sys keeps the contract it agreed with the design system well at the token level: every colour, wash, elevation and duration reads a vendored role, reduced motion comes through the tokens, the focus ring is one rule, settled pills are quiet, icons are one set, fields hold 16px, and the light theme keeps every meaning. Where it drifts is one level up, in how its own parts spend those tokens. Four things make the app feel like several apps rather than one: the brand-red fill (the app's one 'do this next' button) is also the irreversible yes of every inline ask, a Check on each of fifty timesheet rows and three Saves on one Admin tab; 'the thing you are on' is drawn in two colours and five shapes, three of them stacked on every job page; where a record stands is a pill in Jobs and Quotes, a coloured stripe in Repairs, Testing and Vehicles, a heading in Timesheets and a coloured block on a job's kit; and inside a card the type and space have no shared rhythm (group heads at 12, 13, 16, 18.72 and 20px; inner boxes at three radii and two insets; cards 24px apart on a list and 16px apart on a record). Each is a few CSS rules and a wording decision rather than a redesign, and each is the kind of rule the system already has in words.

#### ds-1 · The one filled red is also every inline ask's yes, a per-row Check, and a second button beside the dock

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles O, D, W, H · phone 390 dark, phone 390 light, desk 1440 dark · reproduced

button.primary, which ADR 0104 made 'the only filled red on the page' for the one next step, is also the yes of all 20 inline asks (act.tsx:123), including 'Mark it fixed', 'Write it off' and 'Remove it'; a 'Check' on every Timesheets row that needs one (50 under TO APPROVE); a 'Checked' on each answer row on Calls; and 'Make a quote' on a job's Quote tab while the dock is already saying what is next. The office learns on the job page that the red button is the safe next step, then meets the same red on the button that closes a fault for good, beside a plain 'Keep it'. The system's rule is one primary per region, destructive quiet or armed in bad; here it repeats fifty times on a list, so it stops meaning anything.

- **Evidence:** #jobs/v1-job-00012/phases (phone 390, dark): Next dock: 'Next · Planning · 1 line of kit, 1 short' with the one red 'Sort 1 short on the kit'; the same on desk right of the title (`web/src/jobs/steps.css:95-99; web/src/jobs/NextDock.tsx`) [1 button.primary on the page, bg rgb(200,32,46)] · #stock/repairs › Sort it out › Mark fixed (phone, dark and light): Inline ask: amber 'Mark SH-000183 d&b B22-SUB fixed? It can go out on jobs again.' then 'Mark it fixed' in the red fill and 'Keep it' plain, focused (`web/src/act.tsx:123-128 (yes className='primary'); web/src/stock/Faults.tsx:222-227, 265`) [yes bg rgb(200,32,46), focus on 'Keep it' (measured via document.activeElement)] · #crew/timesheets (phone and desk): 'TO APPROVE (50)': every row ends in an amount and a red 'Check'; three red buttons in the first screen, fifty down the page (`web/src/crew/crew.css:125-128; Timesheets row button className 'primary'`) [3 button.primary in the first viewport, all 'Check', in .ts-row] · #crew (Calls › Answers to check): 'Checked' as a red fill on an answer row beside plain 'Open' (`web/src/crew (Calls answers row)`) [button.primary 'Checked' in .row] · #jobs/v1-job-00012/money: Two primaries on one screen: the dock's 'Sort 1 short on the kit' and the card's 'Make a quote' (`web/src/jobs/JobScreen.tsx:208 QuoteCard`) [2 button.primary measured on the page] · #admin (Company tab, phone): 'Save office details', 'Save printed quotes', 'Save travel and subsistence' all in the red fill down one tab (`web/src/*Card.tsx`) [3 button.primary on one tab-part]
- **Basis:** principles.md rule 1 (what cannot be undone arms in bad, the safe choice focused); components.md Button (one primary per region, in the accent; Danger for destructive) and Dialog (focus the safe button, the destructive choice is not the accent); NN/g heuristic 4 consistency and 5 error prevention; behavioural.md Default Effect: the loudest button is the one taken by habit · NN/g, Confirmation dialogs and Undo; NN/g, Visual hierarchy and the one call to action; Baymard, destructive-action buttons; design-system principles.md rule 1 and components.md Button and Dialog
- **Proposal (S):** Reserve the red fill for the one next step in a region (the dock, a form's one Save, a list's one 'New…'), and draw the inline ask's yes and every per-row action as the plain button, or danger when the outcome is a loss. act.tsx Confirm: yes becomes the plain button, and takes className='danger' (the bad fill app.css:187 already draws for Delete everything) only where the outcome is a loss (Write it off, Remove it, Erase, Withdraw); 'Keep it' stays plain and focused; the amber question stays. Timesheets.tsx:87: 'Check' as the plain 'button' or the row's 'link' it already uses for other rows; the heading's count carries the volume. CrewScreen's 'Checked' the same. Quote.tsx:212-216 and QuoteDraft.tsx:775: 'Make a quote' / 'Print' stay red only when the dock has nothing to press for this job, as ADR 0109 did for the Confirmed card. Admin's three Saves stay: one per form is the rule. No new CSS.
- **Trade-off:** A row's Check loses its pull; the count in the heading and the amount carry it, and a plain button in a row is still 44px and edged. The ask's yes becomes quieter than its question, which is the point. **Depends on:** Nothing; act.tsx and three row sites. Pairs with the open 'no Undo toast yet' learning, which stays. **Weighed:** Arming as the system prefers: set aside because the inline ask is a recorded departure (learnings 2026-10-10) that works without script on the freelancer pages. Keeping the per-row red on the first row only: rejected, it still teaches red as 'check'.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/red-fill-one-colour-four-jobs-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/red-fill-one-colour-four-jobs-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/timesheets-office-desk-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-ds/inline-ask-red-yes-office-phone-dark.png`
- **Learning:** A learning for learnings.md: 'An app without a toast asks in place; even then the irreversible yes is never the accent fill: it is the secondary button, or danger when the outcome is a loss, with the safe choice focused.'
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced on my server as the office: #crew/timesheets has three red 'Check' in the first screen on phone and desk (Timesheets.tsx:87 gives 'button primary' to every row that needs checking, so all 50 under TO APPROVE); #crew has a red 'Checked' on an answer row; Repairs › Sort it out › Mark fixed shows 'Mark it fixed' at rgb(200,32,46) with 'Keep it' plain and focused (act.tsx:123 className='primary' for every Confirm, 20 sites not 11); #jobs/v1-job-00012/money carries two primaries, the dock's 'Sort 1 short on the kit' and the Quote card's 'Make a quote'. Not already fixed: audit finding 19 and ADR 0109 made only the Confirmed card's buttons plain and restated that 'the dock keeps the page's one filled red', so the Quote card is a leftover instance of a decided rule, which strengthens the theme rather than re-litigating it. Not decided: learnings.md 2026-10-10 records the inline ask with the safe answer focused and says nothing about the yes's colour. Two adjustments: the Admin instance goes (each card is its own form and a form's one Save is the system's one primary per region; merging three cards' Saves into one would change the save model, which is another lens's question), and the rating comes down to NN 2 / P2 because the safe answer is focused and the amber question names the outcome, so the cost is a wrong habit and a second look, not lost data; nothing here needs doing before the next busy week. (was: "The one filled red button means next, destroy, check and save")

#### ds-4 · Inside a card the type and space have no shared rhythm

**P2 · severity 2 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles O, D, H, W · phone 390 dark, desk 1440 dark · reproduced

A group head inside a card is set five ways: 12px tracked capitals (Jobs weeks, a job's kit departments), 13px capitals (Crew's call days, Timesheets' groups, Money's 'Paid lately'), 16px bold sentence case (Home, Repairs, Testing, Quotes, a product's Items), the browser's own 18.72px h3 on Money's 'Invoiced €68,649.00' because nothing sets it, and 20px on What's changed. A boxed thing inside a card has radius 10 with inset 12 on a phase's and a call's box, radius 8 with inset 12 on a fault, a test, a van and a Home tile, radius 12 with inset 8 on a board column; and cards sit 24px apart on Home and every list but 16px apart inside a job's, a product's, a person's and Admin's tabs, in Split and in Beside. The h1 sits on a 30px line over 24px type and a card's h2 on 30px over 20px, both off the system's 4px grid. None of it is a decision anyone wrote down; the comment in app.css:40-48 names the six space roles and the area sheets then step round them with literal --ds-space-N. The office reads a record page as tighter and busier than the list it came from, and the eye never learns which head is a group and which a section.

- **Evidence:** #jobs, #jobs/<id>/kit, #crew, #crew/timesheets, #home, #stock/repairs, #quotes, #jobs/money, #admin (phone, dark): h3 'THIS WEEK' 12px caps; 'AUDIO' 12px caps; 'ON TODAY' 13px caps; 'TO APPROVE (50)' 13px caps; 'The next fortnight' 16px; 'Damaged, can't go out (6)' 16px; 'Waiting for an answer' 16px; 'Invoiced €68,649.00' 18.72px; '1.88.0 Small fixes…' 20px (`web/src/jobs/jobs.css:20, 56, 632, 485 (no font-size); web/src/crew/crew.css:123, 152; web/src/home/home.css:76; web/src/stock/stock.css:4; web/src/app.css:590`) [getComputedStyle font-size and text-transform on each h3, 9 screens] · #crew, #stock/repairs, #home, #jobs/board, #stock/vehicles: Inner boxes at radius 10/inset 4-12-8 (call day), 8/12 (fault, tile, van), 12/8 (board column) against the card's 12/16 (`web/src/crew/crew.css:153; web/src/stock/stock.css:151; web/src/home/home.css:12; web/src/jobs/steps.css:134, 144; web/src/app.css:141`) [border-radius and padding measured per element] · #jobs/<id>/*, #stock/product/<id>, #crew/person/<id>, #admin, #crew, #stock/testing, #stock/vehicles: Cards 16px apart inside .job-part, .tab-part, .split and its columns; 24px apart on #home, #jobs, #stock, #quotes, #menu (`web/src/jobs/jobs.css:607; web/src/app.css:601, 461-462, 472-473 (gap --ds-space-4) vs app.css:82 (gap --section)`) [.app gap 24px on every screen; .job-part/.tab-part/.split gap 16px measured on 10 screens] · every screen: h1 24px on a 30px line; card h2 20px on a 30px line (`web/src/app.css:241 (line-height 1.25); app.css:142 (inherits 1.5)`) [line-height 30px measured on 23 screens]
- **Basis:** principles.md Type (label capitals are the workhorse for a group head; every line height on the 4px grid; two or three real headlines), Space (name the job before the step; section is twice group; a numbered step only where no role fits, with its reason), 'corners about one eighth of the control's height'; components.md Section (a head in text.label and ink.tertiary); NN/g heuristic 8 aesthetic and minimalist design · design-system principles.md Type and Space (sess-sys's own 2026-10-30 learning, 'a grid is not a rhythm', closed at 0.14.0); Bringhurst-style vertical rhythm as adopted in Material's 4dp grid; NN/g, Visual hierarchy
- **Proposal (S):** Two head styles written down once, one boxed-row role, and the inner card gap decided and recorded. (1) app.css: `.card h3.group` (or a shared selector list) = the existing week-head rule, 12px tracked capitals, muted, for a head over a group of rows (weeks, departments, call dates, timesheet groups, leave; crew.css:96/123/151 drop from --text-hint 13px to --text-pill 12px); a sub-section with its own copy keeps ADR 0082 §4's sentence case at --text-small 16px (Home, Repairs, Testing, Quotes), with the two rules and the distinction in one comment. jobs.css:485 (Money's 'Invoiced €…') and Quotes' 'Before this app, from Current RMS' get a size, since today they are the browser's 18.72px. What's changed keeps 20px with its reason (each entry is a record). (2) `--inset-box: var(--ds-space-3)` and `--radius-box: var(--radius-control)` beside the six space roles at app.css:40-52, read by crew.css:153, stock.css:151/176, vehicles.css:3, home.css:12 and steps.css:134/144, so a boxed row inside a card is one shape. (3) .job-part, .tab-part, .split and .beside gap: a decision for Colly, 16 (calm inside a record) or --section 24 (as a list), recorded in the comment either way. (4) h1 line-height 32px and card h2 28px from the system's heading and title leadings.
- **Trade-off:** The 13px heads shrink a pixel; a Home or Repairs head stays as it is. If 24 is picked for records, a job page grows about 40px. **Depends on:** Nothing; CSS only. **Weighed:** The reviewer's one capitals style for every h3: rejected because ADR 0082 §4 chose sentence case for card heads and the 16px sentence h3s follow it; two styles with the distinction named is the smaller change.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/card-group-head-five-sizes-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/boxes-in-cards-three-radii-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-ds/admin-office-phone-dark.png`
- **Learning:** A gap for learnings.md: the six space roles say nothing about a boxed row inside a card (its inset and radius); sess-sys draws one five ways. A seventh role, or a line under Panel in components.md.
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced by getComputedStyle on 14 screens: h3 at 12px caps (Jobs weeks, kit departments), 13px caps (Crew's 'On today', Timesheets' groups), 16px sentence (Home, Repairs, Testing, Quotes, Admin's 'Logo'), 18.72px browser default on Quotes' 'Before this app, from Current RMS' and Money's 'Invoiced', 20px on What's changed; boxes at 8/12 (fault, van, tile) and 12/8 (board column); .app gap 24px on every list, .job-part, .tab-part and .split 16px; h1 24/30 and h2 20/30. Not fixed by the audit and not decided as a whole, but one part of the proposal walks back a decision: ADR 0082 §4 made card headings 'sentence case, no capitals or letter spacing', and the 16px sentence h3s on Home, Repairs and Quotes follow that spirit, so 'one group-head style, 12px tracked capitals' for all of them is not the smallest change and cites no ADR. The two clear slips are the unsized h3s and the 12/13 split among the capitals heads. The 16 vs 24 card gap inside a record reads as a choice someone made for calm (ADR 0082) that was never written down, so it is a question for Colly, not an assertion. Rating holds: it is this lens's strongest 'several apps' evidence, but a record page reading tighter than its list costs the office little. (was: "Inside a card the type and space have no shared rhythm")

#### ds-2 · 'Open' is drawn as the hover wash on a task and a chart column, so resting and open look the same

**P3 · severity 1 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles O, D, H, W, S · phone 390 dark, phone 390 light, desk 1440 dark · reproduced

The current place, view and tab each have one drawing, which the 5 November audit judged coherent. But an opened task row (steps.css:51) and Home's picked chart column (home.css:52) take the 6% hover wash as their 'open' or 'being read' look, the same wash a pointer rests on, so on a desk a closed task under the pointer and the open task read alike, and nothing turns to say which is open.

- **Evidence:** #jobs, #stock, #crew (phone, dark): Views row current 'List' / 'Catalogue' / 'Calls 74' in an ink fill; segmented 'Coming up (132)' / 'All (209)' in the same ink fill (`web/src/app.css:222 (.views a[aria-current] background ink), app.css:276 (.filters [aria-pressed] ink)`) [bg rgb(210,210,216), text rgb(20,20,21)] · #jobs/v1-job-00012/phases (phone and desk): Foot 'Jobs' in the red fill; 'Planning' stage with a red underline and an ink ring; 'Phases 1' tab with a red underline; then red Next and red links (`web/src/crew/crew.css:13; web/src/jobs/steps.css:21, 33; web/src/jobs/jobs.css:605`) [foot bg rgb(200,32,46); part-tab and stage border-bottom rgb(238,55,68); now ring box-shadow 0 0 0 5px rgb(210,210,216)] · #home: 'Company' / 'First week' toggles pressed in the ink fill; the picked chart column in the 6% hover wash (`web/src/home/home.css (.home-for [aria-pressed] ink; .chart-col now wash-hover)`) [pressed bg rgb(210,210,216); chart col bg rgba(255,255,255,.06)] · #jobs/v1-job-00012/phases › a task: The open task row takes the hover wash, the same wash a pointer rests on (`web/src/jobs/steps.css:51 (button[aria-expanded] background wash-hover)`) [bg rgba(255,255,255,.06)]
- **Basis:** states.md Selected (one drawing, set by aria-current/aria-selected/aria-pressed; a selection does not hover); principles.md 'one meaning, one colour'; components.md Tabs, Segmented control, Navigation bar; NN/g heuristic 4 consistency and 6 recognition · Material 3 state layers (selection one treatment); NN/g, Tabs used right; NN/g, Visual indicators of current location; design-system states.md and navigation.md
- **Proposal (S):** Expanded is a turned chevron, never a wash; and the three 'current' drawings get one comment block naming which part takes which. steps.css:51: the open task loses the wash and its row ends in the shared chevron that turns at --ds-duration-base, as History's run-line already does (app.css:429). home.css:52: the picked column keeps its wash only inside @media (hover: none) or takes a 2px ink bar under the column instead. app.css: one comment naming bar = accent fill, segment = ink fill, tab = accent 2px line, index = ink line, with the audit's verdict cited.
- **Trade-off:** The open task is quieter by a wash; the chevron and its tasks under it say it is open. **Depends on:** Nothing. **Weighed:** Adopting states.md's accent.soft + edge for every selection: rejected, the audit held the current set up and the light theme would need a new measured pair.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/current-drawn-seven-ways-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/job-phases-office-desk-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-ds/job-phases-office-phone-dark.png`
- **Learning:** For navigation.md: a line saying a bar, a segmented control and a tab row may each draw 'current' their own way, but an app picks one drawing per part and never two for the same part.
- **Sceptic:** Every drawing reproduced (foot red fill, views and filters ink fill, part-tab and opened stage red 2px line, the now ring, On this screen's ink line, the open task and Home's picked chart column in the 6% hover wash). But the 5 November audit walked exactly this ('States across screens: pressed, current, selected') and put it under What held up: 'one look per state: pressed chips and current views a light fill, job and person parts a red underline, the current area a red fill'. So three drawings for three kinds of part is a judged, coherent set, and the reviewer's own proposal ends by keeping all three and writing them down, which is a comment block, not a theme. What is real and not yet judged is narrower: 'open' and 'being read' are drawn as the hover wash on two sites (steps.css:51 the expanded task, home.css:52 the picked chart column, the latter with its reason written in), so on a desk a pointer resting on a closed task looks the same as the open one, against states.md Expanded (a turned chevron) and 'a selection does not hover'. That is a P3 at NN 1, size S. (was: "The thing you are on is drawn in two colours and five shapes, three of them on one job page")

#### ds-3 · A fault or a van shown away from its state group carries its state in colour alone

**P3 · severity 1 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles W, H, O · phone 390 dark, phone 390 light · reproduced

Repairs, Testing and Vehicles say a record's state with a 4px stripe and the group heading above, which is fine at volume (the heading says it once). But Faults.tsx:230 puts the words in aria-label only, so where a fault row stands outside its group, on a product's page or on Home, the stripe is the only visible sign, against 'state is said in words as well as colour' and WCAG 1.4.1. Jobs and Quotes say the same thing with the StatusPill, so the warehouse meets two parts for one meaning.

- **Evidence:** #jobs, #quotes, #crew/person/v1-person-00002, #notifications (phone, dark): Pills: 'Confirmed' outlined, 'Quoted' / 'Sent' / 'Offered' / 'Check' amber soft, '1 short' / 'Clash' burnt-orange soft (`web/src/app.css:178-183; web/src/StatusPill.tsx (used in 53 files)`) [pills present on 8 of 23 screens walked; none on Repairs, Testing, Vehicles, Timesheets, Leave] · #stock/repairs: Six boxes under 'Damaged, can't go out (6)', each with a 4px stripe in the bad tone and no pill; 'Sort it out' as a muted link-button (`web/src/stock/stock.css:151-153`) [5 .fault.stops stripes rgb(240,160,100) in the first screen] · #stock/vehicles: 'Small Van (231-XX-101)' with an amber stripe and an amber block 'Small Van is off the road until Tue 10 Nov: Service and tyres.' (`web/src/stock/vehicles.css`) [2 .vehicle-row.warn stripes rgb(255,179,71), 3 neutral] · #stock/testing: 'Can't go out (16)' and 'Due in the next 30 days (4)' as 16px headings; due boxes with the stripe (`web/src/stock/stock.css:176-180`) · #crew/timesheets: State only in 'TO APPROVE (50)' / 'NOT IN YET (10)' / 'APPROVED LATELY (5)'; rows end in an amount and a red Check (`web/src/crew/crew.css:120-128`) [0 .pill on the screen] · #jobs/v1-job-00012/kit: '1 line short: not enough.' as a burnt-orange soft block under the line (`web/src/jobs/jobs.css:28-32 (.kit-state)`)
- **Basis:** components.md Chip and dot (a chip names a state in words; the dot is a second channel, never the only one); principles.md 'state is said in words as well as colour', 'healthy is quiet'; states.md Many rows (at volume the state of the list is the summary); NN/g heuristic 4 consistency · NN/g, Status indicators (words plus colour); WCAG 1.4.1 use of colour; design-system components.md Chip and dot, states.md Many rows
- **Proposal (S):** Keep the stripe as the warehouse's second channel, record it, and add the visible word only where a row stands outside its state group. Faults.tsx: where the list is not grouped by state (a product's Faults, Home), the row's header line takes a StatusPill ('Can't go out' bad, 'Fit to go out' pending, 'Fixed' confirmed) and the aria-label goes. Vehicles: 'Off the road until Tue 10 Nov' as a pending pill on the row's header line, the reason as the muted second line, in place of the block. learnings.md: 'a boxed row's 4px stripe is sess-sys's dot, a second channel beside the group heading, never the only one'. StatusPill.tsx:5-6's stale 'green' comment brought up to date.
- **Trade-off:** One pill per ungrouped fault row adds a few pixels; the grouped Repairs list is unchanged. **Depends on:** Nothing. **Weighed:** The reviewer's: remove the stripe everywhere and pill every ungrouped row (M): rejected, the stripe under a heading is already within the grammar and is a glanceable cue on a load-out morning.
- **Screenshots:** `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/status-said-by-five-parts-phone-dark.png`, `screens/ux-sweep/sceptic-ds/repairs-office-phone-dark.png`
- **Learning:** A line for components.md Chip: 'A list grouped by state says the state once in the group head and not again on each row; a row shown away from its group carries the chip.'
- **Sceptic:** Reproduced: six 4px stripes in the bad tone under 'Damaged, can't go out (6)' on #stock/repairs, two amber stripes on #stock/vehicles with the amber 'off the road' block, due boxes striped on Testing, Timesheets heading-only, and the kit-state block on a job's kit. But under a heading that names the state the words are there once, which is what states.md Many rows asks, and the stripe is then a second channel the grammar allows; the 5 November audit saw the stripe (it fixed the green default on van checks) and left it as the part. Faults.tsx:230 puts 'Damaged, can't go out' in the row's aria-label only, so the one real cost is a fault or a van shown away from its state group (a product page's Faults, Home) with colour alone visible. Removing the stripe everywhere, as proposed, takes a scan cue off the warehouse's phone for no measured gain and is M where the real fix is S. Adjusted to that. (was: "Where a record stands is a different part in every area")

#### ds-5 · An invalid quote line is amber where every other form refuses in the bad tone, and pending item numbers are the one waiting thing still amber

**P3 · severity 1 · Adjusted by the sceptic** · roles O, D, W · phone 390, desk 1440 · inferred

The quote's line editor draws an invalid field's edge and its fault line in amber (jobs.css:369-372) while required.ts and .field-refusal draw the same class of mistake in the bad tone on every other form, so the one mistake changes colour between a quote and a person's details. A product's item numbers waiting to sync are dashed in the warn ink (stock.css:27) where every other waiting thing is dashed grey, the last site of the 2026-10-10 'waiting is never warn' learning.

- **Evidence:** #jobs/<id>/money (quote draft, a line left with no price): The unit box and the fault line under it in amber (`web/src/jobs/jobs.css:369-372 (.unit-box.needs, [aria-invalid] border --warn; .line-problem, small.error color --warn)`) · #crew/person/<id> › Details, #admin forms: An empty required field refused in the bad tone under the form (`web/src/app.css:399-401 (.alert, .field-refusal bad-soft/bad); web/src/required.ts`) · #stock/product/<id>/items while a change waits: Item numbers dashed and in the warn ink (`web/src/stock/stock.css:27 (.numbers a.is-pending { border-style: dashed; color: var(--warn) })`) · #jobs, the top bar, #jobs/plan, #stock/racks: Waiting to sync as a dashed grey pill / dashed grey sync state / a faded block / a dashed panel (`web/src/app.css:183, 112; web/src/jobs/jobs.css:134; web/src/stock/racks.css`) · a stopped or past job's Next dock: 'Nothing to press' as a dashed grey box, the pending look, for a resting state (`web/src/jobs/steps.css:95 (.next-dock .rest border 1px dashed --field-line)`)
- **Basis:** states.md Invalid (a bad edge, the label in bad.ink, a ds-error line) and Pending (a dashed tertiary ring, never warn: 'work waiting to sync is routine'); principles.md grammar 'one meaning, one colour' and its converse applied to form; learnings 2026-10-10 (sess-sys moved waiting off amber); components.md ds-chip--idle (dashed = present, not active) · design-system states.md Invalid and Pending; GOV.UK Design System error message (one error colour); NN/g, Error message guidelines
- **Proposal (S):** Invalid is the bad tone everywhere; waiting is dashed grey everywhere. jobs.css:369-372: --warn becomes --bad on the invalid edge and on .line-problem and small.error; the summary's amber chips that lead stay amber (they are attention, components.md 'a chip that leads'). stock.css:27: color --warn becomes --muted to match .pill.waiting. The Next dock's dashed rest box stays, as ADR 0104 decided.
- **Trade-off:** A quote line's fault turns burnt orange; ADR 0100 §8's rule that a fault waits until the field is left keeps it from flashing mid-entry. **Depends on:** Nothing. **Weighed:** Recording the quote editor's amber as a departure: rejected, the departure would be per screen, not per rule.
- **Learning:** None new; closes the loop on the 2026-10-10 'waiting is never warn' row, which still has one site left.
- **Sceptic:** From code, as the reviewer also had it: jobs.css:369-372 draws the invalid edge, .line-problem and small.error in --warn where required.ts and .field-refusal (app.css:399-401) use the bad tone; stock.css:27 colours pending item numbers --warn; steps.css:95 draws the rest box dashed. I did not open a draft quote with an invalid line. Two of the three stand: ADR 0100 §8 decides when a quote's fault shows and keeps amber 'for a real shortfall', not the colour of an invalid field, so the split with every other form is undecided drift, and the pending numbers in warn are a leftover of the 2026-10-10 learning the reviewer cites. The rest box is decided: ADR 0104 line 98 names 'a dashed box' for 'Nothing to press', and the audit's held-up list repeats it, so that instance is dropped. With one site each the two that remain are findings at P3, not a P2 theme; the direction and size hold for them. (was: "Amber and the dashed edge are each spent on two meanings")

**Smaller findings**

- **ds-f1 · The five stage names are 11px on a phone, under the system's floor** (P3 · severity 1 · Kept by the sceptic). Every job page shows Selling, Planning, Going out, On the job, Back at 11px under 640px, the only text in the app under the 12px floor, and it is not recorded as a departure. *Direction:* 12px with 'On the job' allowed to wrap to two lines (the dot column is 26px; five labels of 12px fit 390px at 70px each), or keep 11px and add the departure line to learnings.md. (S). *Sceptic:* Measured .stage-label at 11px on the phone and 13px on the desk (steps.css:78). Its comment cites ADR 0104 for 'the names stay, smaller', but learnings.md carries the under-floor departures of vizz and LIGHT and none for sess-sys, so it is unrecorded. Either direction the reviewer gives is right; the 12px-with-wrap one is the smaller. Screenshots: `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/current-drawn-seven-ways-phone-dark.png`
- **ds-f2 · Seven hover rules sit outside the hover media query, so a tap can leave a tile or tab lit** (P3 · severity 1 · Kept by the sceptic). Home's tiles, the skills box and chip buttons, planner blocks and items, the month head and the part-tabs set :hover without @media (hover: hover), unlike the rest of app.css, so on a phone the hover look can stay after the finger lifts. *Direction:* Move the seven rules inside the existing @media (hover: hover) blocks. (S). *Sceptic:* From the stylesheets: more than the seven cited sit outside @media (hover: hover): app.css:223 (views), 335 (skills box), 342 (skill chip); home.css:16-17 (tiles); jobs.css:106, 125, 136-137, 153 (planner), 415 (quote chips), 582 (map bar), 604 (part-tabs). app.css:483's own comment says hover is only on a device with a pointer so a tap never leaves a button looking held, so these are slips against the app's own rule, not a decision. Not reproduced on a device; a headless tap does not show it. The fix is the same one move.
- **ds-f3 · Three folds with three looks** (P3 · severity 1 · Adjusted by the sceptic). Fold.tsx is a plain button with no chevron or count that turns into a form with a Close link; native details keep the browser's triangle with summaries muted-bold in Crew and ink in Stock and at 400 weight on the Board; history and the quote's cost fold draw their own chevrons at two durations; tasks and stages expand under a wash or an underline. The system's disclosure is one shape: a row-height summary, a turning chevron, a count. *Direction:* One .fold summary style for every details (ink, 600, the shared chevron icon turning at --ds-duration-base, a count where there is one) and Fold.tsx's opener drawn as the same summary rather than a button. (S). *Sceptic:* Measured native summaries at 600 muted (Crew's 'Show 103 filled calls', People's 'Archived (4)'), 600 ink (Testing's 'No certificate for the last test'), 400 muted (Board's 'Dates passed, no answer (7)'), and History's own chevron; three weights for one part is real. Fold.tsx comes out of it: a button that opens a form in place is an action, not a disclosure, and its Close-that-keeps matches the system's 'closing hides, never clears'. Adjusted title: 'Native folds carry three summary weights across Crew, Stock and the Board'; direction: one summary rule in app.css (ink, 600, the shared chevron turning at --ds-duration-base, a count where there is one). Size S. Screenshots: `screens/ux-sweep/lens-ds/folds-three-looks-phone-dark.png`
- **ds-f6 · Two targets under 44px on a touch-density app** (P3 · severity 1 · Kept by the sceptic). The filter chip that takes a filter off is 36px tall and the storage banner's button 36px, the only controls under the app's own 44px rule. *Direction:* min-height 44px on both, or a 44px hit area round the 36px chip as the skill chip does (app.css:345). (S). *Sceptic:* app.css:293 (.list-chip min-height 36px) and storage.css:4 (.keep button min-height 36px) confirmed. The audit's finding 86 on sub-44px targets named Today rows, fact-grid links, rack slots and the freelancer page, not these two, so they are new. Not seen live: no filter was on and the storage banner did not show on my copy.
- **ds-f5 · Home's chart greys are local, not the vendored chart roles** (P3 · severity 0 · Kept by the sceptic). home.css still points --chart-settled and --chart-pending at --on-line and --field-line rather than --ds-chart-mark and --ds-chart-pending, which the 0.12.0 tokens carry; adoption.md's sess-sys row lists it as still to do. *Direction:* Point the two local names at the two chart roles. (S). *Sceptic:* home.css:37 confirmed: --chart-settled and --chart-pending point at --on-line and --field-line, not the vendored chart roles. Housekeeping with no usability cost, kept as the reviewer rated it (NN 0, P3): a one-line change so a theme or token change reaches Home's charts.

**What held up:** Every colour, wash, elevation and duration reads a vendored role (grep for literal transitions found none); reduced motion comes through the tokens (fast/base/slow all .12s, offsets 0, measured) with only the skeleton's pulse kept, as allowed. One focus ring rule for everything (app.css:189), the same on rows, links, folds and the skills box. The StatusPill's quiet settled look: on the Jobs list only outlined 'Confirmed' and grey stage segments, so 'Quoted' and '1 short' stand out; green is kept to three scan-read uses. The light theme keeps every meaning: red fill Next and foot, amber question, burnt-orange stripe and '1 short', outlined Confirmed, all measured and seen on four screens. Fields hold 16px at touch, 44px rows and buttons everywhere a role can press, the page head in one place with one Archivo title, breadcrumbs from 900px, the rail on a desk. The parts sess-sys gave the system (list tools, the combobox's keys, the line-item editor, the notification list, the toast, the skeleton with late words) are drawn as the system now documents them. Icons are one set, named by meaning, before the word, one weight; avatars and thumbnails follow imagery.md. The Next dock is one part in one place with one line format, never greyed, and the stage strip's 'now' ring and done ticks read the same on phone and desk.

**Dropped by the sceptic:** ds-f4 (The desk gets a phone's density: Measured: data-density=touch on <html>, 44px controls and 64px phase rows at 1440px. But it is decided and recorded: adoption.md's sess-sys row says it keeps 'its spacing, corners, density and components' by Colly's decision of 2026-10-09, and app.css:397 gives the reason (gloves, a dark venue). The reviewer says it is a question for Colly rather than drift; a recorded decision is not re-opened without a shown cost in use, and none is measured.); ds-f7 (Disabled fades the whole control; modified, stale and unknown are never drawn: button:disabled at opacity .5 was in the 5 November audit's held-up list ('disabled 50%', only three disabled controls, none by role). Modified-with-Reset is a state for a value moved off a default in a console, not for company details in Admin; the offline copy's age belongs to the sync states, which learnings.md 2026-11-01 already records as sess-sys's own gap sent back. The reviewer calls it 'low cost today' and names no screen where it costs anyone anything.)

### What held up

Each lens was asked to name what is already cohesive and must be kept. Taken together:

- **jobflow:** The job page's Where it is card and the one Next dock: on eleven bench jobs at every stage and edge (enquiry, quoted, lapsed, Planning, Back at 2 and 6 days, through, lost, cancelled, hire, meeting) the dock said the right thing in the right place, the role check held (staff crew got 'Nothing here for you to press'), and the ADR 0109 wording ('14 never scanned out: check they're back', 'Taken as done: it ended … with nothing still out', 'Record what happened') read truthfully on each. Lost and cancelled jobs read the same everywhere: pill, dock ('Lost. To take it up again, change its status under Change details.'), no stage line, Not going ahead tab. The Board's Selling fold 'Dates passed, no answer (7)' and Quotes › To follow up's groups tell one story with Home's housekeeping tile. The Board's card line and the dock's line are already nearly the same sentence; theme 1 is making that literal.
- **consistency:** One 24px h1 on every one of 32 screens, and one width: the frame is one app (ADR 0082, 0091). One Up grammar on records: '‹ All jobs', '‹ All stock', '‹ All people', '‹ All places', '‹ All vehicles', '‹ All clients', '‹ All venues'; '‹ Menu' for the Menu's pages. One date shape, 'Fri 6 Nov' with the year only when far, on 23 of 26 screens including 99 dates on the Board and 74 on Quotes. One pill class set shared by jobs, quotes and offers (pending/confirmed/cancelled/bad/waiting), so 'Quoted', 'Sent' and 'Offered' look alike as waiting states. The shared Empty part ('Nothing here yet.' then the screen's own line) and the shared list tools (Sort, Filter (n), chips, '12 of 140 jobs.') on every long list. The area rows on Jobs, Stock and Crew with counts where there is something to count, and the three records built first (job, product, person) as the model for the rest.
- **forms:** required.ts: every empty required field is refused in the app's words ('Fill in the job first.', 'Fill in the first name first.', 'Fill in the name first.'), focus goes to the first, the line lasts and goes on the fixing keystroke, with aria-invalid and aria-describedby: one refusal voice across every app form walked. Confirm (act.tsx): outcome-labelled buttons ('Book anyway' / 'Don't book it', 'Cancel the leave' / 'Keep it'), safe answer focused, opens in place; no browser dialog anywhere (grep: 0 window.confirm). Every app field is 16px and 44px high at 390px (14 forms measured); ticks have a 44px label row. Dates are native with `min={start}` on every To field and the Reply by default with its hint; the quote line, admin numbers and most money fields use inputMode decimal. The quote draft's autosave words ('Saved', 'Saved on this device', 'Not saved yet') and the sticky readout follow ADR 0080/0100 and the system's Line-item editor (one line open, Done and Remove inside, Undo after Remove). The server-drawn forms redraw with what was typed (page.ts `typed.get(...)`) and mark the field in error with aria-invalid and aria-describedby. Hints are real `<small>` text joined by aria-describedby on the admin cards, and the office/travel cards explain each number in one sentence.
- **director:** Every count on Home opens the list that explains it, and the office's and warehouse's counts land on the right part (Kit short, Pick lists, Answers to check). One tap from Home's week ahead lands on a job head that shows client, venue, dates, crew 5 of 10, kit 1 short, the stage strip and the Next dock in one phone screen; the quote with its expected margin is one more tap. The figures agree: Home's Money card, Money's stat tiles, By month (on a desk) and the quote page all say €19,189 confirmed of €22,729 quoted for November. The stage line reads the same words on the list, the Board and Home ('Planning · 5 of 10 booked · 7 lines of kit, 1 short, 2 vehicles'), and the Board's Selling cards are specific and timed ('Quote with the client since Tue 3 Nov'). What's changed is in the office's words, newest first, with the version rule in one sentence and new-since-last-look marks; Status opens with a one-line answer and Check again; the Menu's lines say what each screen is for in plain words. Healthy is quiet in the charts: only crew still needed is amber; a fully booked fortnight has no colour.
- **office:** One filled red Next in one place on the job page, with 'Hand it to someone' beside it and a plain-words box when there is nothing to press (ADR 0104/0109): the same on every job the office opened. Did you mean on New job's client and venue, in one press, with 'will be added' for a new one; the New job form is one card with sensible defaults (Enquiry, Gig, one Show phase today). Answers to check bundles yes/counter/decline/pull-out/running late/documents into one list with Ring, Noted, Offer… and 'Noted: all 12 declines on calls already full' in one press; a pull-out says 'the call is 2 short' with Offer… right there. Quotes › To follow up grouped by what to do (Waiting for an answer, Dates passed with no answer with Mark the job lost, Not sent yet), and the Board's 'Dates passed, no answer (7)' fold reading the same rule. The stage line under every jobs-list row and Home row, and the Jobs Filter with Stage and Tasks, so the list, Board and Home read one record. Change handling warns ahead: To sort out lists calls short in the next three days, vehicle clashes and crew offered while booked elsewhere; the planner flags 'Crew on a day their phase moved off'; Kit short shows on the row, the Board card, Home and the Next button. Every switch that would contact someone says so where it is off ('the app sends nothing', 'Marked sent, it becomes quote 75 … send it yourself'), and the quote's What happened card says 'These are the office's records'.
- **head:** Home › Department: 'Needs attention in LX' in the head's words, split 'The next fortnight' / 'Housekeeping', with the honest hint about the uncounted call with no department; every tile lands on a list that says 'Showing LX only. Show all departments' (all six tiles checked, phone and desk). Crew › Calls and People start on LX with the badge counting LX (audit findings 6 and 42 hold), and 'Showing LX only. Show all departments' is the one wording everywhere it appears. The one Next dock in one place with a verb and its object, and 'Hand it to someone' beside it; what a head can't do is said in words ('The office agrees rates: your role doesn't see them') rather than greyed. The Leave page puts 'To approve (2)' first with the requester's note and the clash ('Maeve Doyle is off Mon 30 Nov to Tue 8 Dec') beside Approve | Decline.
- **warehouse:** Home › Warehouse: 'Going out (5)' first with 'Tomorrow · Sat 7 Nov · 0 of 27 out' rows straight into the pick list (one tap), 'Still out after the job (3)' with the bad flag, and the view remembered on the phone (sh.home). The pick list head is the floor's: 'In 3 days: Load in, Mon 9 Nov at Riverside Park' and 'Going in: Big Van (241-XX-101), Cian Murphy driving', so what goes in which van is one place; department chips 'Audio 5 left | Lighting 1 left | Power 1 left' and 'By where it's kept'; every button 44px high; the scan box docked under the top bar. Scan → one-line answer with a 65x64 Undo ('Undone: SH-000013 Amp rack isn't out: it's back in stock.'); Coming back → 'd&b Y10P back: 7 of 8 back' with 'Report damage to SH-000007' inline, a two-field form (What's wrong, It can still go out), then 'reported damaged: can't go out until it's fixed.' The fault loop closes on the job at once: Repairs went 15 → 16, the job's Where it is reads 'Back in · 1 still out · Next' and 'Checked and deprepped · 2 faults open on its kit', the dock 'Scan the kit back'; the office sees it through its Home tiles ('16 repairs open', '7 damaged items can't go out'), quiet and computed, consistent with how kit short is told. Offline held: 'No signal' then 'No signal · 2 waiting' in the bar, scans counted at once, a trip to Home and back kept them, 'Up to date' on return; the You page says what the browser may clear and how to keep it. Testing's 'Test a batch' intro is the best-worded form on the floor: who, what, five seconds with Undo, works with no signal, shared phone keeps the last name. The desk's Labels, Counting and the pick list are the same parts and words as the phone's (cards, chips, 'Set numbers aside', 'To count this week'), so the admin side feels like one app with the floor; only 'No count is under way on this phone' reads oddly at a desk.
- **staff:** Home › Me is short and hers: three cards (Your day, Your work, Your leave) in 1,248 px, the leave balance in a sentence, the next booking named with its date; the audit's gain holds. The link page's order and glance line ("2 offers to answer · Next: …, call 08:00") are the model for what staff should get in the app; its booking card (Role, Dates, Call time, Venue, note, Call sheet, Can't make it any more?) is the crew's vocabulary done right. Refusals that are refusals are plain and kind: Quotes and Money say who they are for and how a role changes, with no dead buttons; nothing on the walk was offered and then refused (ADR 0034 holds everywhere seen). Leave's figures and rules are right for the person: allowance, carried over, booked, left; the year-open line names the approvers; days in lieu explained in one sentence. ADR 0062's narrowing is honest and useful: colleagues keep their phone number with a Call / WhatsApp button, and what is hidden says so. The dock, the task card and the stage strip are consistent with each other across every job page seen; the incoherence is only that they face the office on a page staff open for their call.
- **freelancer:** The glance line: sticky, three parts, each a link down the page, and it updates at once after every answer ('3 offers' → '2 offers' → 'Next: Clare Hotels Fundraiser, Sun 29 Nov, call 07:00'). Offers and bookings fold after three to one line each with their tag; the one a message is about opens on its own. Every post comes back to the card it was about with a message in the card ('Thanks, you're down for it. The office will confirm.'), under the sticky line, and refused posts keep what was typed. The counter flow says what Accept now means: 'Accept these days at €290 a day instead', 'Rate €320 a day asked (offered €290)'. Decline and pull-out are folded as the no-script arm, with the consequence said before the button ('Once declined, it's off your page'). The look is the app's: the SH mark, brand red as 'you' (Waiting on you, Accept), the design system's colour roles via dsColor in both themes, muted small-cap section heads; system-ui instead of the app's display font is a deliberate 9 KB, no-network choice. The wrong-link page says what to do and who to ask, with tel: and mailto: links; the office line is on every page. A timesheet is two taps (open, Send) with the rate 'as agreed' said first; sent, it says the total and that it can be changed until approved; approved and paid days are shown. The call sheet puts 'You' first, hides other crew's numbers from ordinary crew, and links the map.
- **ds:** Every colour, wash, elevation and duration reads a vendored role (grep for literal transitions found none); reduced motion comes through the tokens (fast/base/slow all .12s, offsets 0, measured) with only the skeleton's pulse kept, as allowed. One focus ring rule for everything (app.css:189), the same on rows, links, folds and the skills box. The StatusPill's quiet settled look: on the Jobs list only outlined 'Confirmed' and grey stage segments, so 'Quoted' and '1 short' stand out; green is kept to three scan-read uses. The light theme keeps every meaning: red fill Next and foot, amber question, burnt-orange stripe and '1 short', outlined Confirmed, all measured and seen on four screens. Fields hold 16px at touch, 44px rows and buttons everywhere a role can press, the page head in one place with one Archivo title, breadcrumbs from 900px, the rail on a desk. The parts sess-sys gave the system (list tools, the combobox's keys, the line-item editor, the notification list, the toast, the skeleton with late words) are drawn as the system now documents them. Icons are one set, named by meaning, before the word, one weight; avatars and thumbnails follow imagery.md. The Next dock is one part in one place with one line format, never greyed, and the stage strip's 'now' ring and done ticks read the same on phone and desk.

### What was not checked

Each sceptic said what it did not reach, so that a theme's "kept" is not read as wider than it is. The consistency sceptic did not re-open the reviewer's screenshots (its own are the evidence), did not check whether the item and venue pages carry an On this screen strip, and did not check toasts, Saving/Saved wording or keyboard focus after navigation. The forms sceptic did not reach light-theme phone passes, Add product and Add item on a phone (judged from the reviewer's screenshots and the code) or the quote editor. The director sceptic did not report the "74 jobs in Planning with the next task down to nobody" tile as its own theme because the bench's seed has no task owners and it could not tell whether the office gives tasks on live; the move that thresholds the tile (Move 10) stands on the office lens's reading instead. The office sceptic did not check the freelancer link, moving dates, the call sheet send or keyboard-only operation, and noted that the Board's three "Waiting on the client" cards are seed jobs with a quoted status and no quote record, which on live would mean a quote done outside the app. The head sceptic did not walk the call sheet and show pack on the day (other lenses did) or timesheet approval (hidden for a head). The warehouse sceptic did not check the camera, the light theme or the rack designer. The jobflow, staff, freelancer and design-system sceptics stated no gaps.

Across the sweep: no real crew were watched (the people in it are the personas and the lenses walking the bench as them); contrast was not measured (the system's checker does that, and the moves name it as a check); nothing ran against live data, the calendar or anyone's phone; and the desk was walked for the roles that work at one (director, office, warehouse admin) but not for every screen, which is why section 9 asks for a 1440px pass per batch.

### Dropped by the sceptics

Nine findings were dropped on re-walking, each with a reason recorded against the decision or audit finding it stood on: the edit fold's three names (consistency-f2: one screen's slip), Quotes living in two places by viewport (consistency-f4: ADR 0088), Admin › Status telling every director what to set in Railway (director-f3: ADR 0093), the Department view offering a director nine departments (director-f4: by design), a person outside the head's department losing "Their link" (head-f3: ADR 0062), the office's timesheet backlog showing on the freelancer's page and a page with nothing on it having no line saying so (freelancer-6, freelancer-7: audit finding 94(f) and a seed artefact), the desk getting a phone's density (ds-f4: adoption.md records the decision), and disabled fading the whole control with modified, stale and unknown never drawn (ds-f7: no site that bites). They are listed under their lens below with the sceptic's words.

## Appendix: the sources behind the forms research

Opened on 6 November 2026. Each entry gives the finding the sweep took from it; the rules drawn from them are in section 7.

**A List Apart**

- [Inline Validation in Web Forms](https://alistapart.com/article/inline-validation-in-web-forms/) (2009). 22 users, six variants: the best inline variant gave 22% higher success, 22% fewer errors, 31% higher satisfaction, 42% faster completion, 47% fewer fixations than submit-only; validating after leaving a field beat validating while typing by 7–10 seconds; feedback before typing was worst; success marks should persist.

**Adam Silver**

- [The problem with disabled buttons and what to do instead](https://adamsilver.io/blog/the-problem-with-disabled-buttons-and-what-to-do-instead/) (2023). A disabled submit gives no feedback, feels broken when one of several errors is fixed, is hard to read, unreachable by keyboard, deceptive and easy to miss off-screen; enable it and explain on press.
- [Where to put buttons on forms](https://adamsilver.io/blog/where-to-put-buttons-on-forms/) (2019). Primary left-aligned with the inputs (Wroblewski's eye-tracking: one strong vertical axis; magnifier users see it); Back above the form; Cancel below the primary as a quiet link; 'Add another' just above the primary; 'Save and exit' beside it; fewer buttons.

**Adam Silver, Smashing Magazine**

- [Form Design Patterns Book Excerpt: A Registration Form](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2018/10/form-design-patterns-excerpt-a-registration-form/) (2018). Visible linked labels; hints above the input, not inside; validate on submit (blur fires on window switches and password managers); error summary at the top, focused by script, each item linked; inline errors above the field with aria-invalid; verbs on buttons; never disable submit.

**Atlassian Design System**

- [Inline edit usage](https://atlassian.design/components/inline-edit/usage) (current). For information that already exists and is updated often; visible tick and cross at the end of the field; Enter saves, Escape cancels; saving on blur is not obvious on its own, so keep the controls visible and keep large text areas open on blur; read and edit views look the same.

**Baymard Institute**

- [Usability Testing of Inline Form Validation: 31% Don't Have It, 4% Get It Wrong](https://baymard.com/blog/inline-form-validation) (2024). Validate on blur (or at full length for fixed-length fields), never prematurely; remove the error on each keystroke once fixed (stale errors misled testers); keep submit-time handling; 31% of sites have no inline validation.
- [Form Field Usability: Matching User Expectations](https://baymard.com/blog/form-field-usability-matching-user-expectations) (2010). Fields much longer or shorter than the expected answer made testers doubt the label. Exact width for fixed-length values, one generous default (about 18–33 characters) for names and emails, custom widths for outliers like postcodes.
- [E-Commerce Checkouts Need to Mark Both Required Fields and Optional Fields Explicitly (Only 14% Do So)](https://baymard.com/blog/required-optional-form-fields) (2018 (benchmark 2021)). With only optional fields marked, 32% of testers hit a validation error from skipping a required one; with only required marked, some over-shared. Mark both; collapse rarely used optional fields behind a link; page-level notes are overlooked.
- ['Touch Keyboard' Implementations Have Improved Just 9% Since 2013 (60% Still Get it Wrong)](https://baymard.com/blog/mobile-touch-keyboards) (2015). Set type, inputmode and pattern per field; numeric keys are about 5× larger and cut typos; disable autocorrect on names, addresses and emails; keep keyboard behaviour consistent; an example format must be typeable on the keyboard shown.
- [Form Field Usability: Avoid Extensive Multicolumn Layouts (16% Make This Form Usability Mistake)](https://baymard.com/blog/avoid-multi-column-forms) (2023). Multicolumn forms are misread and skipped more, even with separators; a single column guides the eye down. Exception: two or three inputs forming one logical unit (dates, name parts, card number/expiry/CVC).
- [Checkout Optimization: 5 Ways to Minimize Form Fields in Checkout](https://baymard.com/blog/checkout-flow-average-form-fields) (2024). Fields matter more than steps; most checkouts need 8 fields but average 11.3. Single name field (42% typed a full name into First name), hide rarely used optional fields behind a link, default from what is known.
- [Observation: Users Will Go Far to Avoid Repeat Form Errors](https://baymard.com/blog/avoiding-repeat-form-errors) (2011). After one validation error people typed 'N/A' into optional-looking fields and over-shared to avoid another; clear required/optional marking and upfront format hints stop the hedging.

**Cloudscape Design System**

- [Inline edit pattern](https://cloudscape.design/patterns/resource-management/edit/inline-edit/) (current). Inline edit for one property that changes often; editable cells marked twice (column icon and hover); confirm and dismiss controls; errors next to the field on submit and re-checked once criteria are met; an edited row may vanish under a filter or sort, so keep it visible until refresh.

**GDS design notes**

- [Asking for a date of birth](https://designnotes.blog.gov.uk/2013/12/05/asking-for-a-date-of-birth/) (2013). Three dropdowns were slow (115-year list) and confused less experienced users; three text fields tested well with a numeric keyboard on touch; the only hesitation was over leading zeros.
- [One thing per page](https://designnotes.blog.gov.uk/2015/07/03/one-thing-per-page/) (2015). Easier for low-confidence users, works on mobile, handles errors, branches and saving; evidence is lab research and service experience rather than A/B numbers; group subsidiary parts where a long service overwhelms.

**GOV.UK**

- [Form structure (Service Manual)](https://www.gov.uk/service-manual/design/form-structure) (current). One thing per page for the public; merge pages only when research shows it works, e.g. internal users who repeat and switch tasks; the question protocol (why each question is asked, what is done with it, who must answer); eligibility first; one question may still need several fields.

**GOV.UK Design System**

- [Question pages](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/question-pages/) (current). Start with one question per page; group only where research shows it helps, the example being an internal service for government users who repeat and switch between tasks quickly. '(optional)' in the label, never asterisks; hint one short sentence, no full stop, no links; Continue left-aligned; a 12-step progress indicator was removed with no effect on completion.
- [Dates](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/dates/) (current). Memorable dates: three text fields; dates from documents match the document; allow approximate and relative dates; a calendar only for near dates in context and never the only way; example dates use a day of 13 or more and month of 9 or less.
- [Text input](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/text-input/) (current). Fixed widths of 2, 3, 4, 5, 10, 20 characters sized to the answer; inputmode numeric/decimal and avoid type=number (scroll changes the value, no feedback on letters); prefixes and suffixes outside the input and also in the label or hint; accept a typed prefix; autocomplete for WCAG 1.3.5; spellcheck off for names and codes; error templates ('Enter an event name').
- [Error summary](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/error-summary/) (current). Always show a summary when there is a validation error, even one; at the top of main, focus moved to it, each item a link to the field, wording identical to the field's message; role=alert inside the component for JAWS (2022).
- [Error message](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/error-message/) (current). Keep all answers; say what went wrong and how to fix it, reusing the question's words; avoid 'forbidden, illegal, forgot, prohibited, please, sorry, valid, invalid'; red border and a hidden 'Error:' prefix; never for ineligibility or service faults. Tested across live services including tax credits.
- [Button](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/button/) (current). One default button per page; warning buttons sparingly with a separate confirm step; avoid disabled buttons (poor contrast, confuse, no reason given) unless research shows otherwise; primary left-aligned to the form's edge; prevent double submission.
- [Validation](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/validation/) (current). Validate when the user moves on (Continue), not on leaving a field, and not while typing unless research shows it helps (the character count); redisplay with answers kept, 'Error:' in the title, summary plus field messages, focus to the summary; server-side always; novalidate and no HTML required.
- [Select](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/select/) (current). A last resort: users could not close it, tried to type into it, confused focused with selected, pinch-zoomed and did not realise it scrolls. Narrow the options first; pre-select only for settings, never for questions; no multiselect.
- [Telephone numbers](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/telephone-numbers/) (current). type=tel, autocomplete=tel; accept spaces, hyphens, brackets, country codes; no masking and no reformatting what was typed; validate with libphonenumber; 'Enter a phone number, like 01632 960 001'.
- [Task list](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/components/task-list/) (2023–2024). For long services done in any order or several sittings; whole row a link after users tried to click the statuses; statuses in sentence case because capitals were harder to read; completed tasks plain so the remaining ones stand out.
- [Check answers](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/check-answers/) (current). One review page immediately before an irreversible submission (per section only for very large transactions); Change links with hidden context; the submit labelled with the specific action ('Send your claim form'); skipped optional answers shown as 'Not provided'.
- [Names](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/names/) (current). A single Full name field fits most names; split fields only when parts are needed; autocomplete name / given-name / family-name (WCAG 1.3.5); spellcheck off; let names change.

**LukeW**

- [Web Application Form Design](https://www.lukew.com/ff/entry.asp?1502) (2005). Top-aligned labels for familiar data and fastest completion (eyes move in one direction); left-aligned labels for unfamiliar data at a cost in completion time; right-aligned for association but harder to scan.

**Material Web**

- [Text field](https://material-web.dev/components/text-field/) (current). Label may float or sit outside (outside labels need an explicit accessible name); supporting text under the field replaced by error text with an icon; prefix and suffix text for currency and units; maxlength shows a counter; required shown in supporting text. (m3.material.io and Apple's HIG pages are script-rendered and returned no text, so they are not cited.)

**Nielsen Norman Group**

- [Placeholders in Form Fields Are Harmful](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/form-design-placeholders/) (2014, reviewed 2018). Seven problems with placeholder-as-label (memory, checking, recovery, keyboard, overlooked fields, taken for prefilled data, deletion) plus contrast and screen-reader gaps; floating labels fix only the first four. Labels and needed hints go outside the field.
- [Marking Required Fields in Forms](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/required-fields/) (2019). Mark every required field (asterisk or the word), never rely on a note at the top; marking optional ones too is a nice extra. Login forms are the exception.
- [10 Design Guidelines for Reporting Errors in Forms](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/errors-forms-design-guidelines/) (2019, reviewed 2024). Validate inline once the field is left, not before; keep the message next to the field; a summary supplements field messages and never replaces them; red plus an icon, never colour alone; the same error three or more times is a design problem.
- [Error-Message Guidelines](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/error-message-guidelines/) (2023). Visibility (next to the cause, severity-matched, not too early: leaving an empty field is not an error), communication (plain, specific, a remedy, no 'invalid/illegal/incorrect'), efficiency (prevent, preserve input, offer the likely fix).
- [Date-Input Form Fields: UX Design Guidelines](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/date-input/) (2017). Calendar pickers suit dates within about a year; typing is fastest for distant or known dates and should always be allowed; split day/month/year dropdowns add cost; accept common separators; block illogical dates (end before start).
- [OK-Cancel or Cancel-OK? The Trouble With Buttons](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/ok-cancel-or-cancel-ok/) (2008). Either order works if consistent; use specific labels not OK; highlight the common action as default except for dangerous actions, which must not fire on Enter.
- [Wizards: Definition and Design Recommendations](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/wizards/) (2017). Wizards help novices and rare tasks; they hurt repeated expert use, cross-step comparison and bulk entry (tabular tools are faster). Show all steps, label them descriptively, save and resume, reuse previous selections as defaults.
- [Website Forms Usability: Top 10 Recommendations](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/web-form-design/) (2016). Keep it short, group, single column (short related fields like city/state/zip excepted), radios for 2–3 options, size fields to the input, distinguish optional and required and limit optional ones to one or two, no Reset/Clear, preserve input. Seckler et al. (CHI): 78% error-free first submissions with the guidelines vs 42% without.
- [Dropdowns: Design Guidelines](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/drop-down-menus/) (2017). Avoid long scrolling dropdowns; typing beats scrolling for familiar data; free text with validation or typeahead for many values; keep the label visible when open.
- [Listboxes vs. Dropdown Lists](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/listbox-dropdown/) (2020). Five or fewer options: radios or checkboxes, not a dropdown; 5–15 either; over 15 a listbox or, where the exact value is known, a text field. Dropdowns do not do multiselect; verbs are buttons.
- [Checkboxes vs. Radio Buttons](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/checkboxes-vs-radio-buttons/) (2004). Radios for exactly one of several, always with a default and an explicit 'None' if none is allowed; vertical, one per line; labels positive; these controls change settings, never trigger actions.
- [Confirmation Dialogs Can Prevent User Errors — If Not Overused](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/confirmation-dialog/) (2018, reviewed 2026). Routine confirms are clicked through by habit; use only for serious consequences; name the item and label buttons with the action; no default Yes; undo is the stronger safeguard.
- [Reset and Cancel Buttons](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/reset-and-cancel-buttons/) (2000). Remove Reset buttons (hit by mistake, clutter); Cancel is of limited value on the web except in multi-step dialogs where earlier steps already acted; always offer an explicit radio for the default choice.
- [A Checklist for Designing Mobile Input Fields](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/mobile-input-checklist/) (2015). 14 checks: is the field necessary; label above; marked required or optional; no placeholder; big enough to show the value; visible with the keyboard up; good defaults; use phone features; compute from other info; paste; the right keyboard; suggestions; allow typos and abbreviations; any format.
- [Toggle-Switch Guidelines](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/toggle-switch-guidelines/) (2018). Toggles take effect at once; do not mix them into forms that need Submit, where people cannot tell whether the toggle acted; otherwise a single checkbox.

**Smashing Magazine**

- [A Complete Guide To Live Validation UX](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2022/09/inline-validation-web-forms-ux/) (2022). Reward early, punish late: re-validate at once on a field already in error, otherwise on blur; never fault an empty required field before submit; always give a way out (an override for address and phone validators).
- [Designing Better Error Messages UX](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2022/08/error-messages-ux-design/) (2022). Errors above the field are not covered by the mobile keyboard or autofill; without a page refresh put the summary directly above the submit button; link items; count in the title; no auto-scroll at first; toasts disappear too quickly for form errors; allow overrides where validators are fallible.
- [Usability Pitfalls of Disabled Buttons, and How To Avoid Them](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2021/08/frustrating-design-patterns-disabled-buttons/) (2021). Disabled buttons explain nothing, strand users when a validator rejects valid input, are unfocusable and low contrast, and sit off-screen on mobile; keep the button enabled and explain on press; if unavoidable use aria-disabled, keep it focusable and say why.
- [Designing Birthday Picker UX: Simpler Is Better](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2021/05/frustrating-design-patterns-birthday-picker/) (2021). Native pickers have accessibility gaps and can take dozens of taps; dropdowns are slow; typed single fields are unpredictable ('Jul', '06', mixed order); three labelled fields with labels above and no auto-advance for a known date; calendars only when picking.
- [Designing The Perfect Date And Time Picker](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2017/07/designing-perfect-date-time-picker/) (2017). Native pickers suit a single fixed date; custom ones when availability or a range matters; typing with a forgiving parser for distant dates; shortcuts like 'today' and 'in 7 days'; aim for at most three taps for a date; mini-steppers tire after about ten taps.
- [Designing Sticky Menus: UX Guidelines](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2023/05/sticky-menus-ux-guidelines/) (2023). Sticky bars help on pages where people act, save or compare, not on mobile forms: the keyboard can take up to 60% of the screen; keep bars compact, at most five items, and keep focused elements visible with scroll-padding.
- [Accessible Target Sizes Cheatsheet](https://www.smashingmagazine.com/2023/04/accessible-tap-target-sizes-rage-taps-clicks/) (2023). Hoober: targets about 11mm/42px at the top of the screen, 12mm/46px at the bottom, 7mm/27px mid-screen; 44×44 the common baseline; sticky menus 44–46px; use Actions buttons rather than hover-revealed icons in tables.

**W3C WAI, WCAG 2.2**

- [Understanding SC 2.5.8: Target Size (Minimum)](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html) (2023). Level AA: a 24×24 CSS px square fits in every target, or undersized targets have 24px circles that do not overlap; inline, user-agent and essential exceptions; 44×44 is AAA 2.5.5.
- [Understanding SC 3.3.7: Redundant Entry](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/redundant-entry.html) (2023). Level A: information already entered or given in the same process is auto-populated or selectable, by the site itself (browser autofill does not count); exceptions for essential re-entry, security confirmation and stale data.
- [Understanding SC 3.3.8: Accessible Authentication (Minimum)](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/accessible-authentication-minimum.html) (2023). Level AA: no cognitive function test at any step without an alternative or help; paste and password managers must work; transcribing a one-time code fails; email-link sign-in (G218) and device biometrics are sufficient.
- [Understanding SC 2.4.11: Focus Not Obscured (Minimum)](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/focus-not-obscured-minimum.html) (2023). Level AA: a focused component is not entirely hidden by sticky headers, footers or banners; pass with scroll-padding (C43) or by closing the banner; F110 is the sticky-footer failure.
- [Understanding SC 2.4.13: Focus Appearance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/focus-appearance.html) (2023). Level AAA: a focus indicator at least a 2px perimeter around the control with 3:1 change between focused and unfocused; a solid 2px outline is the simplest pass.

**alphagov**

- [accessible-autocomplete](https://github.com/alphagov/accessible-autocomplete) (current). An autocomplete built and tested with assistive technology: minLength before offering, confirmOnBlur, showAllValues with an arrow, a live region that says how many results and which is highlighted, instructions via aria-describedby removed once typing starts, no placeholder; a few hundred options may still start as a server-rendered select enhanced progressively.
